# Research dump - 2026-10-09

Raw findings from the research workflow (8 topics, each fact-checked by a second agent). Sources and dates are as reported by the agents; docs/PLAN.md holds the distilled decisions.

## yt-policy: YouTube policies affecting AI-made faceless videos (YPP inauthentic content, AI disclosure labels, enforcement 2025-2026, competitor title/thumbnail reuse, production rules) as of 2026-10-09

### Summary

As of October 2026 YouTube does not ban AI-made or faceless videos, but it has moved from a loose "repetitious content" rule to a three-bucket "inauthentic content" monetization policy that is enforced at channel level by automated pattern detection plus human review. The July 15, 2025 update renamed the policy and added verbatim examples that describe exactly the default output of an automated slideshow pipeline: "image slideshows, templated storylines, or scrolling text with minimal or no narrative" and "AI-generated content made with generic or unoriginal templates giving the impression of mass production". On July 16, 2026 YouTube's VP of Trust & Safety Matt Halprin clarified three buckets: (1) generic/template/repetitive content, (2) "unsatisfying or off-putting" content (emotionally manipulative, interchangeable, shock-for-views, "videos that stitch together unrelated or inconsistent AI clips", deceptive imagery), and (3) AI personas delivering advice on sensitive topics (health, finance, legal, politics), which are not monetizable at all; this last bucket is directly relevant to a product called "Cashflow Studio". Enforcement is real: in January 2026 YouTube terminated or wiped 16 channels with ~35M subscribers, 4.7B views and ~$10M/yr revenue, and legitimate human-made faceless channels reported collateral demonetization. The AI-disclosure requirement (realistic altered/synthetic content: real person made to say/do something, altered footage of real events/places, realistic scenes that did not occur, AI music as the main focus) has been mandatory since 2024; since May 27, 2026 the label sits directly under the player (long-form) or as an overlay (Shorts) and YouTube auto-applies it when it detects significant photorealistic AI, with permanent labels for C2PA "fully generative" files, while stating that a label alone does not affect recommendations or monetization. Cloning your own voice for voiceovers/dubs is explicitly exempt from disclosure; cloning any other real person's voice requires disclosure and risks impersonation/likeness takedowns (face detection live for all 18+ creators since May 16, 2026, audio "in 2026"). Using competitor titles and thumbnails as structural inspiration is allowed; copying the actual image, branding or look-and-feel, or promising something the video does not deliver, falls under copyright, impersonation and "malicious clickbait" rules, and "mimics existing formats or stories to a degree that the videos feel interchangeable" is now a named monetization violation. Separately, YPP entry thresholds double for new applicants from February 1, 2027 (8,000 qualified watch hours or 20M qualified Shorts views, plus a rolling 10M/90-day Shorts-pool test), which affects any new-channel strategy. The rest of this report turns YouTube's own wording into do/don't QA gates for the pipeline.

### Recommendation

Encode the following as pipeline QA gates (fail = block export/upload until a human overrides with a logged reason). All rules below map to YouTube's own wording cited in facts.

HARD BLOCKS (never export):
1. No AI persona/avatar or narrator framed as a doctor, financial adviser, lawyer or political authority; no first-person credential claims ("as a CPA I recommend..."); no personalised "buy/sell/invest in X" instructions. Finance/health/legal/politics scripts must be educational, story- or case-study-based, cite sources on screen and carry a disclaimer card. (Bucket 3, July 16 2026.)
2. Never clone a voice other than the enrolled user's own; voice profile must have a stored consent/ownership record. (Disclosure category 1 + impersonation policy; audio likeness detection coming 2026.)
3. Never re-upload or re-cut competitor footage; yt-dlp is for metadata/transcripts/thumbnail analysis only. Never reuse a competitor's thumbnail image, face, logo or channel branding; thumbnail must be generated from scratch and pass a perceptual-similarity threshold (e.g. pHash distance / CLIP similarity below a configurable bound) against the reference.
4. Never produce a script that is a paraphrase of the competitor transcript: enforce n-gram overlap and semantic-similarity ceilings against the source transcript and against the channel's own last N scripts ("minimal variation across videos" is a violation).
5. No thumbnail/title promise that the script does not fulfil (malicious clickbait; misleading thumbnails): the QA step must find the title's key claim in the script within the first ~20% of runtime and verify the thumbnail subject appears in the video.
6. No shock/distress/rage-bait formulas (animals in danger, fake disasters hitting real cities, fabricated arrests/quotes of real people). Any depiction of a real person must be non-fabricated.

ORIGINALITY / NARRATIVE GATES (script stage):
7. Script framework must produce a narrative arc (hook → setup → progression → payoff/takeaway) with a stated point of view, at least K unique facts/data points with sources, and an original angle statement; store the research notes as provenance. (Allowed examples: "well-researched or creative narrative", "authentic perspective when building on a popular video format".)
8. Rotate script frameworks and openings; cap re-use of the same structural template per channel per rolling 30 days.

VISUAL VARIETY GATES (storyboard/edit stage):
9. Minimum scene density (e.g. a new visual every ≤8–12 s for long-form, ≤3–4 s for Shorts), minimum share of non-slideshow elements (text cards, charts, maps, diagrams, animated callouts), varied motion presets and transitions, consistent per-channel art style so clips are not "unrelated or inconsistent AI clips". Reject videos where >X% of scenes are static AI images with identical zoom-in.
10. On-image text/popups must paraphrase the spoken line, never contradict it, and never be the only content (scrolling-text-only videos are a violation).
11. Per-channel image dedupe (pHash) so the same AI image is not reused across videos except branded intro/outro, which YouTube explicitly allows.

DISCLOSURE GATES (export/upload stage):
12. Default "altered or synthetic content" = YES and auto-write a one-line description note; allow a per-video override only if a per-scene classifier confirms no photorealistic real person/place/event and no AI music as the main focus. Own-voice clone needs no disclosure, but keep the flag on by default because YouTube says the label alone does not affect reach or monetization, while repeated non-disclosure can suspend YPP.
13. Record whether the image tool embeds C2PA metadata (permanent label) and surface that to the user; never strip provenance metadata.
14. Background music must be licensed/royalty-free with licence stored; AI-generated music used as the main focus triggers disclosure.

CHANNEL-PATTERN GATES (multi-user / multi-language):
15. Mandatory human review checkpoints with logged decisions at script, storyboard and final-cut (evidence of editorial judgement for appeals; 21-day appeal window). The editor UI should make at least one manual change easy and record it.
16. Configurable upload-cadence caps per channel (default ≤1 long-form/day, ≤3 Shorts/day) and metadata-template rotation (title patterns, thumbnail layouts, description boilerplate) because reviewers look at metadata uniformity, newest videos and most-viewed videos.
17. For the 10 target languages prefer one video with multi-language audio tracks (own-voice dubs are disclosure-exempt) or localised scripts and visuals per channel; do not mirror identical videos across 10 channels, which reads as copied/mass-produced content.
18. Keep a per-video provenance bundle (research notes, script versions, prompts, review log, disclosure decisions, licences) exportable as PDF/JSON for appeals.

PRODUCT PLANNING NOTES:
19. New channels launching after 2027-02-01 need 8,000 qualified watch hours or 20M qualified Shorts views; Shorts-pool revenue requires 10M qualified Shorts views every rolling 90 days. Show these thresholds in the app's channel dashboard.
20. Policy pages change often (last material changes 2025-07-15, 2026-05-27, 2026-07-16, 2026-08-10): keep the rule set in a versioned config file with source URLs and a "last verified" date, and re-verify quarterly.

### Options

| Option | Pros | Cons | Pricing | API / integration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Voice A: user's own cloned voice (from the paid voice-cloning tool) | Explicitly exempt from AI disclosure ("Cloning one's own voice to create voiceovers or dubs"); authentic human identity behind the channel; same voice across languages via dubbing is also exempt. | Needs documented consent/ownership per user (multi-user app); using one voice across many channels or many videos with near-identical narration is itself a listed pattern ("slideshows with repeated narration"); audio likeness detection arrives in 2026 so any mismatch between enrolled identity and voice owner becomes visible. | User's existing subscription | User-provided voice tool with word timestamps; store a signed consent/ownership record per voice profile. |
| Voice B: generic synthetic stock voice (not modelled on a real person) | Allowed; no real-person impersonation risk; disclosure not required unless it depicts a real person speaking. | Identical voice + identical structure across uploads matches "identical narration" examples; weak differentiation; viewer 'AI slop' ratings may be worse. | varies | Any TTS |
| Voice C: clone of a third party's / famous voice | None relevant. | Requires disclosure (real person made to say things they did not), violates impersonation policy, exposes channel to likeness/privacy takedowns and the NO FAKES Act; voice detection coming 2026. Must be hard-blocked in the pipeline. | n/a | Block at QA |
| Visual A: pure AI-image slideshow with Ken Burns zoom/pan only | Cheapest, fully automatic. | Matches YouTube's verbatim violation "Image slideshows, templated storylines, or scrolling text with minimal or no narrative" and the 2026 'interchangeable videos' test; high demonetization risk once the channel has 20-30 uploads. | ~$3-5 per 10-min video (images+voice) | FFmpeg zoompan |
| Visual B: mixed-media edit (AI images in a consistent per-channel art style + on-image text callouts/popups + animated charts, maps, diagrams, typographic cards + captions + music + varied transitions and per-scene motion presets) | Demonstrates editorial decisions and a "cohesive storyline"; matches YouTube's allowed examples ("using AI to visualise a unique character", "creative tools to assist in delivering a unique, well-researched or creative narrative"); on-screen text improves retention and comprehension for 10 languages. | More rendering complexity; needs a variety-scoring QA gate and a library of motion/text templates that are rotated so metadata and visuals do not become uniform. | Same API cost; more CPU time | FFmpeg filtergraph + drawtext/ASS subtitles + optional matplotlib/SVG renders |
| Host format A: narrator-only (no persona, no claimed credentials) | Keeps finance/health/legal topics out of the 'AI personas on sensitive topics' bucket; easier to keep educational/story framing. | Still a sensitive-topic channel: needs on-screen sourcing, disclaimers and mandatory human review; YouTube has not confirmed a disembodied AI narrator on finance is safe. | n/a | Script framework constraint |
| Host format B: AI avatar / 'expert' persona giving advice (finance, health, legal, political) | None for monetization. | Explicitly not monetizable since July 16, 2026 ("AI-generated podcast hosts offering financial guidance, investment tips or wealth management advice"). Hard-block. | n/a | Block at QA |
| Disclosure strategy A: disclose only when YouTube's four categories apply | Minimal labels on stylised/animated videos; label stays in description for non-photorealistic content. | Mis-classification risk: YouTube auto-labels detected photorealistic AI anyway and penalises consistent non-disclosure; requires a per-scene classifier (photorealistic real place/person/event?). | n/a | Per-scene flag in storyboard JSON -> upload 'AI use' attribute |
| Disclosure strategy B: always set 'altered or synthetic content' = yes and add a plain-language note in the description | Zero non-disclosure risk; YouTube states the label alone does not change recommendations or monetization eligibility; simpler QA. | Prominent under-player label on every long-form video and overlay on every Short; may slightly affect viewer perception. | n/a | Default-on flag, user can override per video |
| Competitor metadata A: pattern-level inspiration (title structure, keyword set, thumbnail layout/colour/emotion/text-length) | Allowed; this is what YouTube calls "building on a popular video format" with your own perspective; titles are not copyrightable. | Must still deliver the promise (clickbait rule) and vary templates across the channel; pipeline should measure similarity and reject near-copies. | n/a | Thumbnail generated from scratch with the image tool; similarity check (pHash/CLIP) against the competitor image |
| Competitor metadata B: reuse competitor thumbnail image/elements, channel look-and-feel, or re-narrate their transcript | None. | Copyright claim/strike on the image; impersonation policy (copying branding/content); 'readings of other materials you did not originally create' and 'content copied from another online source without substantive modifications' are reused-content violations. | n/a | Block at QA |

### Facts

- (high, 2025-07-15 (page accessed 2026-10-09)) YouTube's channel monetization policies page carries the dated note: "July 15, 2025: We're making a minor update to our 'repetitious content' policy to better clarify this includes content that is repetitive or mass-produced. We are also renaming this policy from 'repetitious content' to 'inauthentic content.'" - https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/1311392
- (high, accessed 2026-10-09) Verbatim examples of inauthentic content that is NOT eligible for monetization: "Similar or repetitive content with low educational value, commentary, narratives, or minimal variation across videos"; "Image slideshows, templated storylines, or scrolling text with minimal or no narrative"; "AI-generated content made with generic or unoriginal templates giving the impression of mass production". - https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/1311392
- (high, accessed 2026-10-09) Verbatim examples that ARE allowed under the inauthentic content policy: "Same intro and outro for your videos, but the bulk of your content is different"; "Similar content, like a series following a set of characters across episodes or a channel that does product reviews, but in which each video has a distinct storyline, focus, or concept". - https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/1311392
- (high, accessed 2026-10-09) Reused content policy, not eligible: "Content downloaded or copied from another online source without any substantive modifications"; "Content that exclusively features readings of other materials you did not originally create"; "Clips of moments from your favorite show edited together with little or no narrative". Eligible: "Reaction videos where you comment on the original video"; "Edited footage from other creators where you add a storyline and commentary"; "Using clips for a critical review". - https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/1311392
- (high, accessed 2026-10-09) YouTube's reviewers evaluate the whole channel, not one video: "Our reviewers may focus on your channel's: Main theme, Most-viewed videos, Newest videos, Biggest proportion of watch time, Video metadata (including titles, thumbnails and descriptions), 'About' section". - https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/1311392?hl=en-GB
- (high, accessed 2026-10-09; section introduced 2026-07-16) The "Unsatisfying or off-putting content" section (added with the July 2026 clarification) defines it as content that "relies heavily on emotionally manipulative formulas, mimics existing formats or stories to a degree that the videos feel interchangeable, or appears designed to shock or surprise viewers for the sole purpose of getting views." Violations include "Content that lacks a clear narrative arc or logical progression, such as videos that stitch together unrelated or inconsistent AI clips", "Channels that rely heavily on generic templates or emotionally manipulative themes", and "Content that uses deceptive or misleading imagery or narratives". - https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/1311392?hl=en-GB
- (high, accessed 2026-10-09) The same section lists what IS allowed, in YouTube's words: "Content with a cohesive storyline that doesn't rely solely on shock value"; "Content that showcases your authentic perspective when building on a popular video format"; "Content that expresses your unique creative voice, like using AI to visualise a unique character"; "Content that utilises creative tools to assist in delivering a unique, well-researched or creative narrative". - https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/1311392?hl=en-GB
- (high, accessed 2026-10-09; introduced 2026-07-16) AI personas on sensitive topics are not monetizable: "This policy refers to channels that use AI-generated personas to deliver information on sensitive topics ... channels uploading this content will not be allowed to monetise." Examples: "An AI 'doctor' providing medical diagnoses, health advice or wellness remedies"; "AI-generated podcast hosts offering financial guidance, investment tips or wealth management advice"; "AI personas giving legal advice or interpreting laws". - https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/1311392?hl=en-GB
- (high, 2026-07-20) On July 16, 2026 YouTube published the three-bucket clarification (generic/template; off-putting/distressing; AI personas on finance/legal/health). Matt Halprin (VP Trust & Safety) explained it in a Creator Insider video; on bucket 1 he said such videos are "very generic and don't really have a narrative arc and don't really show your creativity", and on AI generally: "Sometimes those videos are great, and it really enhances creativity." - https://techcrunch.com/2026/07/20/youtube-clarifies-policies-around-ai-slop-and-upsetting-videos/
- (medium, 2026-07-21) Halprin called the July 2026 change "a refinement based on user feedback rather than a wider policy change", said "channels already following the rules are not the target", and that the guidance "focuses on how content is made and whether it adds value, not on the use of AI by itself" (reported via Social Media Today). - https://www.relevantaudience.com/youtube-ads/youtube-studio-insights-inauthentic-content/
- (high, 2025-07) TeamYouTube's July 2025 clarification: YouTube "allows creators to use AI tools in content to enhance their storytelling, and this will not affect channel monetization eligibility"; prohibited examples given were channels uploading multiple narrated stories with only superficial differences and multiple slideshow presentations with identical narration; violations remove monetization at the channel level. - https://ppc.land/youtube-clarifies-inauthentic-content-policy-changes/
- (high, 2025-07-11) Rene Ritchie (YouTube creator liaison), July 2025: the policy targets "mass-produced or repetitive" content such as "channels that upload nearly identical narrated stories or slideshows with repeated narration"; clips/compilations/reactions "continue to be monetized if you've added significant original commentary, modifications, or educational or entertainment value". - https://www.searchenginejournal.com/youtube-clarifies-monetization-update-targeting-spam-not-reaction-channels/550123/
- (medium, 2025-07) YouTube's detection (announced 2025-07-02, live 2025-07-15) looks at visual and audio elements, metadata (titles, thumbnails, descriptions, channel info), content patterns across the channel (main theme, most viewed, newest videos) and level of transformation. - https://ppc.land/youtube-improves-detection-systems-for-unoriginal-content/
- (high, accessed 2026-10-09) Spam, deceptive practices & scams policy prohibits "Using automated tools or AI to churn out high volumes of similar content with minimal changes" and "malicious clickbait": "Using maliciously misleading titles, thumbnails, descriptions, or imagery to trick users into clicking on a video that does not deliver what was promised." - https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/2801973?hl=en
- (high, accessed 2026-10-09) Thumbnails policy prohibits "A thumbnail that misleads viewers to think they're about to view something that's not in the video", plus sexual, violent/gory and vulgar imagery or text. - https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/9229980?hl=en
- (high, accessed 2026-10-09) Impersonation policy: "YouTube does not allow unauthorized impersonation of a person, entity, or channel that may mislead viewers", which may include "deceptively copying the branding, content, or usernames", using AI to replicate someone's voice or likeness, or reposting videos without meaningful additions; AI disclosure does not override impersonation rules. - https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/2801947?hl=en
- (medium, undated secondary guide; general copyright principle) A competitor's thumbnail image is a copyrightable work; reusing the image (or its photo/art elements) can draw a copyright claim or strike, whereas titles and short phrases generally carry no separate copyright. Layout, color scheme, emotion and text-structure patterns are not protected. - https://thumbnailtest.com/guides/copyright-strike-youtube-thumbnail/
- (high, accessed 2026-10-09; policy introduced 2024-03) Disclosure rule (verbatim): "we require creators to disclose when they use AI to meaningfully alter or generate photorealistic content" that (1) "Makes a real person appear to say or do something that they didn't say or do", (2) "Alters footage of a real event or place", (3) "Generates a realistic scene that didn't actually occur", (4) "Creates music that's the main focus of the video". Examples needing disclosure include AI-generated music, "AI generated extra footage of a real place", realistic weather events hitting a real city, a public figure doing something they did not do. - https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/14328491
- (high, accessed 2026-10-09) Exempt from disclosure (verbatim): "Cloning one's own voice to create voiceovers or dubs"; "Using effects to enhance previously recorded audio"; "Video sharpening, upscaling or repair and voice or audio repair"; beauty/color filters; clearly unrealistic content such as "Someone riding a unicorn through a fantastical world"; animated content. Penalty for consistently not disclosing: "manual application of a label, or penalties from YouTube, including removal of content or suspension from the YouTube Partner Program." - https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/14328491?hl=en-GB
- (medium, 2025-11-23) Cloning a real third party's voice falls under category (1) (making a real person appear to say something they did not) and therefore requires disclosure; secondary guides list "undisclosed AI voice clones" as a demonetization trigger. YouTube's help page does not spell out a 'someone else's voice' bullet verbatim as of this check. - https://influencermarketinghub.com/youtube-inauthentic-content
- (high, 2026-05-27) YouTube blog, May 27, 2026 ("Improving AI labels for viewers and creators"): for long-form "The label will now appear directly below the video player, above the description"; for Shorts "The label will appear as an overlay on the video itself"; YouTube auto-applies a label when "our systems detect significant photorealistic AI use" and the creator did not disclose; labels are permanent for content made with YouTube's AI tools (Veo, Dream Screen) or carrying C2PA metadata indicating fully generative AI; creators can "update the disclosure status in YouTube Studio" to dispute; "A disclosure label alone does not change how a video is recommended or whether it's eligible to earn money." - https://blog.youtube/news-and-events/improving-ai-labels-for-viewers-and-creators/
- (medium, 2026-06-02) Non-photorealistic, animated or slightly altered AI content keeps its label in the expanded description only; photorealistic/meaningfully altered content gets the prominent label. - https://www.techwyse.com/news/platform-updates/youtube-automatic-ai-labels-disclosure-may-2026
- (high, 2025-10-21) YouTube's likeness detection launched to eligible YPP creators on October 21, 2025 (ID + selfie verification) and lets creators request removal of AI content using their face; YouTube backs the NO FAKES Act (April 2025). - https://techcrunch.com/2025/10/21/youtubes-likeness-detection-technology-has-officially-launched
- (high, 2026-05-16) From May 16, 2026 likeness detection expanded to all creators 18+ (Studio > Content detection > Likeness); scans new uploads only; audio detection not yet available: YouTube is "working to extend likeness detection to audio in 2026". - https://ppc.land/youtubes-ai-face-shield-now-covers-every-creator-18-and-over
- (high, 2026-01-29) January 2026 enforcement wave: 16 channels (11 terminated, 5 content-wiped) with ~35M combined subscribers, ~4.7B lifetime views and roughly $10M/yr ad revenue removed under inauthentic-content / spam rules; the largest was the Dragon Ball-themed CuentosFacinantes (~6M subs). Reported by Tubefilter (2026-01-29), XDA, IBTimes, TNW. - https://www.tubefilter.com/?p=190555
- (medium, 2026) Collateral damage: human-made faceless channels reported demonetization in the same wave; creator Doctor NOS (1.7M subs): "the people who do the same content as me without their face in it, most of them are getting demonetised"; some faceless creators hired on-camera hosts via Fiverr/Upwork. YouTube said it is "not banning AI" and that the target is "mass-produced, templated content with no human creative input"; it has not published how it separates legitimate faceless channels from AI farms. - https://thenextweb.com/news/youtube-ai-slop-crackdown-faceless-creators-collateral-damage
- (high, 2025-11-28) Kapwing 'AI Slop Report' (2025-11-28): of ~15,000 top channels (top 100 per country) 278 were AI slop, totalling 63B views, 221M subscribers and an estimated $117M/yr; a fresh account saw 104 of its first 500 recommended videos (21%) AI-generated and 33% of first 500 Shorts 'brainrot'. Kapwing defined AI slop as "careless, low-quality content generated using automatic computer applications and distributed to farm views". - https://www.kapwing.com/blog/ai-slop-report-the-global-rise-of-low-quality-ai-videos/
- (high, 2026-01-22) Neal Mohan's annual letter (reported 2026-01-22) names low-quality AI content a 2026 priority: YouTube is "building on our established systems that have been very successful in combatting spam and clickbait, and reducing the spread of low-quality, repetitive content"; "AI will remain a tool for expression, not a replacement"; >1M channels used YouTube's AI tools daily in Dec 2025. - https://au.variety.com/2026/digital/global/youtube-channels-using-ai-tools-reduce-slop-neal-mohan-letter-32190/
- (high, 2026-03-23) Since March 2026 YouTube tests a viewer prompt "Did this feel like AI slop?" with a five-point scale (Not at all / Slightly / Moderately / Very much / Extremely); whether the signal feeds recommendations or monetization is not disclosed. - https://pcworld.com/article/3096317/youtube-wants-you-to-help-spot-ai-slop-videos.html
- (medium, 2026-07-16) YPP enforcement/appeals: decisions are channel-level, made by automated systems plus human reviewers; creators get a 21-day appeal window, and a 90-day wait before reapplying with compliant content (secondary summary of YouTube's process). - https://quasa.io/media/youtube-inauthentic-content-buckets-explained-in-2026-interview
- (high, 2026-08-10) YPP overhaul announced August 10, 2026, effective February 1, 2027 for new applicants: 1,000 subscribers plus 8,000 qualified watch hours in 365 days OR 20M qualified Shorts views in 90 days (was 4,000 hours / 10M); Shorts Creator Pool payouts require 10M qualified Shorts views in each rolling 90-day window; new activity requirement (1,000 watch hours/365d, or 1M Shorts views/90d, or 2 long-form + 5 Shorts per 90d); existing partners grandfathered (VP Amjad Hanif: "If you are in YPP today, you stay in YPP") but must accept new terms by January 31, 2027. - https://ppc.land/youtube-doubles-monetization-thresholds-and-adds-a-rolling-shorts-test/
- (medium, 2026-07) July 2026 (rolling): YPP creators gained custom thumbnails for Shorts; Studio Analytics became 'Insights' with AI summary cards and consolidated monetization flags in the Content tab. - https://outlierkit.com/blog/youtube-updates-july-2026
- (medium, 2026) Dated 2025-2026 ad-suitability timeline (secondary compilation): March 2025 human review added to ad-suitability; March 2026 content showing young subjects in distress with shocking imagery lost ad eligibility; May 2026 channel descriptions became directly enforceable; May 27, 2026 auto AI labels. - https://air.io/en/monetization/youtube-monetization-policy-changes-2026-a-complete-dated-timeline
- (low, 2026-09-13) Commentators argue YouTube's signals (no on-camera host, upload cadence, format similarity, metadata uniformity) cannot distinguish a directed AI film from a bot farm, so faceless AI channels are judged on pattern-level evidence of human editorial work. - https://hackernoon.com/youtubes-ai-slop-crackdown-cant-tell-a-directed-ai-film-from-a-bot-farm

### Open questions

- Does YouTube treat a disembodied AI narrator (no persona, no credentials) covering personal-finance stories as outside the 'AI personas on sensitive topics' bucket? YouTube's examples all involve a persona offering advice; a product named 'Cashflow Studio' should get this confirmed (Creator Insider / TeamYouTube forum) and default to educational, non-advisory framing.
- Will the user's paid voice-cloning and image tools embed C2PA or audio watermarks that trigger YouTube's automatic/permanent labels, and does the pipeline need to detect and display that? (Permanent labels apply to C2PA 'fully generative' files.)
- How YouTube's audio likeness detection (promised 'in 2026') will handle a user's own cloned voice across several channels or dubbed languages, and whether multi-user studios need separate verified identities per channel.
- No numeric thresholds exist in YouTube's text for 'minimal variation' or 'interchangeable'; the scene-density, similarity and cadence limits above are heuristics that need calibration against real channel outcomes.
- Legal status of using yt-dlp to pull competitor metadata/transcripts/thumbnails for research (YouTube Terms of Service restrict downloading); confirm with counsel whether metadata-only use is acceptable for a commercial product.
- Whether near-identical content mirrored across 10 language channels is treated as copied/mass-produced; YouTube's multi-language audio tracks may be the safer route and should be checked for availability in each target region.
- Whether the viewer 'AI slop' rating pilot (March 2026) now feeds recommendations or YPP reviews; YouTube has not said.
- Exact definition of 'qualified' watch hours/Shorts views in the 2027 YPP rules has not been published in full.
- The YouTube help page (answer/1311392) sections for 'unsatisfying or off-putting content' and 'AI personas' were read via a fetch tool; verify their exact current wording directly before hard-coding strings into the QA rule file.

### Fact-check

Confirmed claims: 30

- REFUTED: Claim 25: January 2026 enforcement wave of 16 channels (11 terminated, 5 content-wiped), ~35M subscribers, ~4.7B views, ~$10M/yr, largest CuentosFacinantes (~6M subs), 'reported by Tubefilter (2026-01-29)' at https://www.tubefilter.com/?p=190555 - Fetched the cited Tubefilter article (2026-01-29): it says 'at least 18 channels that published AI slop have either been deleted or have had their videos hidden', gives no terminated/wiped split, no combined subscriber, total-view or revenue figures, and only says CuentosFacinantes 'amassed more than 1.2 billion views'; it credits Kapwing for its data. The 16 / 35M / 4.7B / $10M figures actually appear in The Next Web (2026-06-15), which gives no source for them. The 11/5 split was found in neither source. The ~6M subscriber figure comes from Kapwing's report (5.95M). Correction: Use: Tubefilter (2026-01-29) — 'at least 18' slop channels deleted or hidden; CuentosFacinantes >1.2B views. TNW (2026-06-15, unsourced) — 16 channels terminated in Jan 2026 with ~35M subs, ~4.7B lifetime views, ~$10M/yr. Kapwing (2025-11-28) — CuentosFacinantes 5.95M subscribers. Treat the 16-channel financial figures as single-source (TNW) with no primary attribution; do not present them as Tubefilter's. Confidence: high that the attribution is wrong; medium that the TNW numbers themselves are accurate. https://thenextweb.com/news/youtube-ai-slop-crackdown-faceless-creators-collateral-damage
- REFUTED: Claim 27: Kapwing 'AI Slop Report' (2025-11-28) covered ~15,000 top channels, found 278 AI-slop channels totalling 63B views, 221M subscribers and ~$117M/yr - Two separate WebFetch passes over the cited Kapwing page found no global aggregate figures (no '~15,000 channels', '278', '63 billion', '221 million' or '$117 million'). The report gives per-country counts (e.g. Spain 8, Pakistan 20, Egypt 14, South Korea 11, US 9 slop channels in the top 100), per-country totals (Spain 20.22M subs; South Korea 8.45B views) and per-channel figures (Bandar Apna Dost 2.07B views, ~$4.25M/yr; Cuentos Facinantes 5.95M subs). The fresh-account numbers (104/500 = 21% AI; 165/500 = 33% brainrot) ARE on the page. The quoted definition is also truncated: the page reads 'Careless, low-quality content generated using automatic computer applications and distributed to farm views and subscriptions or sway political opinion.' Correction: Cite only what the Kapwing page states: top-100 trending channels per country, per-country slop counts, the 21%/33% fresh-account test, and the full definition. If the 278 / 63B / 221M / $117M aggregates are kept, they must be sourced to whichever secondary outlet computed them (not found in this check) and marked as derived. Confidence: high that the aggregates are not on the cited page. https://www.kapwing.com/blog/ai-slop-report-the-global-rise-of-low-quality-ai-videos/
- REFUTED: Claim 17: thumbnailtest.com guide says a competitor thumbnail is copyrightable, that titles and short phrases carry no separate copyright, and that layout, colour scheme, emotion and text-structure patterns are not protected - The cited page (dated 2026-01-12) only says thumbnails 'are considered copyrighted works if they contain original creative elements' and advises designing your own; it does not discuss titles/short phrases or layout/colour/emotion/text-structure protection at all. Those two legal points are correct but come from US copyright law, not the cited guide. Correction: Keep the conclusion, fix the source: US Copyright Office Circular 33 ('Works Not Protected by Copyright') states that 'words and short phrases such as names, titles, and slogans' and 'the format, layout, or design of a work' as well as 'ideas, procedures, methods, systems, and processes' are not protected by copyright. Use thumbnailtest.com only for the 'thumbnail image itself is a copyrightable work' point. Note this is US law; the top-10-language rollout touches other jurisdictions (EU, India, Brazil, Japan) where short-phrase and look-and-feel rules differ. Confidence: high. https://www.copyright.gov/circs/circ33.pdf
- MISSING: ZERO-COST MULTI-LANGUAGE ROUTE MISSED: YouTube's own automatic dubbing (help page https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/15569972, fetched 2026-10-09) is 'enabled by default for eligible creators', has no price, supports 24 original languages, and from an English original dubs into ~20 target languages incl. Arabic, Bengali, Dutch, French*, German*, Hebrew, Hindi*, Indonesian*, Italian*, Japanese, Korean, Malayalam, Polish, Portuguese*, Punjabi, Russian, Spanish*, Tamil, Telugu, Ukrainian (* = 'expressive speech' that replicates the original speaker's pitch/intonation). Non-English originals currently dub only to English. Dubs are auto-marked 'auto-dubbed' in the description and a 'Publish manually' channel setting lets the creator review before release. Experimental lip-sync is early-access only. This covers all top-10 YouTube languages from a single English master and sidesteps paying the voice-clone vendor for 10 languages; combine with the researcher's option 17 (one video, multiple audio tracks). Caveat: 'eligible creators' is not defined on the page. Confidence: high for facts, medium for eligibility.
- MISSING: YOUTUBE DATA API FACTS THAT CHANGE THE UPLOAD/RESEARCH ARCHITECTURE (developers.google.com, quota page 'Last updated 2026-10-08'): (a) status.containsSyntheticMedia on videos.insert/videos.update 'allows the channel owner to disclose that a video contains realistic Altered or Synthetic (A/S) content' — the disclosure gate (#12) can be set programmatically (https://developers.google.com/youtube/v3/docs/videos). (b) Since 2026-06-01 a granular quota system applies: 'The search.list and videos.insert methods have their own quota buckets. Each of these methods has a default daily limit of 100 per day' (1 unit per call); everything else shares 10,000 units/day; captions.insert = 400 units, thumbnails.set = 50 units (https://developers.google.com/youtube/v3/determine_quota_cost). Implication: a single shared API project caps ALL users combined at 100 uploads/day and 100 search.list calls/day — competitor research via Data API search is impractical, and multi-user upload needs per-user Google Cloud projects or Studio uploads. (c) Revision history (https://developers.google.com/youtube/v3/revision_history): 2025-12-04 upload cost cut from ~1600 to ~100 units; 2026-06-03 videos.batchGetStats (1 unit); 2026-08-27 view counting aligned across formats; 2026-09-11 new thumbnail sizes fhd/qhd/uhd; 2026-09-14 thumbnail and playlist-image max file size raised from 2MB to 50MB (thumbnail generator can emit 1080p+). (d) The videos.insert page now reads verbatim: 'Videos uploaded from unverified API projects are not restricted to private viewing mode.' (https://developers.google.com/youtube/v3/docs/videos/insert, fetched twice 2026-10-09) — the opposite of the 2020-era rule many guides still repeat; medium-high confidence, verify in the Google Cloud console before relying on it. Separately (general knowledge, not re-verified): the youtube.upload OAuth scope is 'sensitive' and an unverified OAuth app is limited to 100 test users, so distribution to many non-developer users needs Google OAuth app verification.
- MISSING: YT-DLP MAINTENANCE/REQUIREMENTS (the whole research stage depends on it): latest release yt-dlp 2026.08.19 (releases: 2026.08.19, 2026.07.04, 2026.06.09, 2026.03.17, 2026.03.13) — actively maintained as of 2026-10-09 (https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/releases). README lists yt-dlp-ejs as 'Required for full YouTube support' with an external JS runtime: Deno >= 2.3.0 (recommended, default), Node >= 22, QuickJS; Bun deprecated after 1.3.14 (https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/wiki/EJS, edited 2026-07-12). Python 3.10+ supported, 3.11 minimum recommended (user machines have 3.12 — fine). PO-Token guide (edited 2026-07-12): Proof-of-Origin tokens are required for streaming on web/web_safari/mweb/tv_simply/web_music/web_creator/android/ios clients AND for subtitles on the web client; without them 'HTTP Error 403, or ... your account or IP address being blocked'; recommended fix is a provider plugin (bgutil-ytdlp-pot-provider or yt-dlp-getpot-wpc) (https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/wiki/PO-Token-Guide). Architecture must bundle Deno + a PO-token provider on every user laptop and expect periodic breakage. Legal: YouTube ToS (US, 'Effective as of December 15, 2023') forbids 'access the Service using any automated means (such as robots, botnets or scrapers)' and to 'access, reproduce, download, distribute...any part of the Service or any Content' without authorization (https://www.youtube.com/static?template=terms&hl=en&gl=US) — never run yt-dlp with the user's monetized-channel cookies; isolate research from the publishing identity.
- MISSING: DISCLOSURE PAGE DETAILS THE RESEARCHER DID NOT CAPTURE (https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/14328491?hl=en, fetched 2026-10-09): YouTube applies labels automatically for 'Content made using YouTube's GenAI tools', 'Content that contains C2PA metadata' and 'content that our internal systems detect is AI generated or altered'; 'content made with YouTube's AI tools, content containing C2PA metadata, or content labeled after manual review can not be adjusted' (a third permanent case: manual review); disputes are made 'by selecting No in the AI disclosure survey under Attributes in YouTube Studio'. Gate #13 should therefore also warn that any C2PA-tagged image/clip or a manual-review label is irreversible, and the provenance bundle should record which Studio attribute was set.

Headings in order: 'Generic or Repetitive Content', 'Reused content', 'Unsatisfying or Off-putting Content', 'AI Personas Related to Sensitive Topics'. The AI-persona rule hinges on the phrase 'any content that presents itself as a human expert providing advice to viewers on topics such as health, legal issues, finances, or politics' — the trigger is posing as a human expert, which sharpens gate #1 (an openly narrated, educational finance explainer that does not pose as an expert is outside this section; still subject to bucket 1/2). Key the config to section titles and verbatim bullets, not to the umbrella word.

The 2026-08-10 announcement date comes only from secondary sources (ppc.land, air.io 2026-08-28). The monetization-policies page, the YPP overview (answer/72857) and the 2027-changes page (answer/12843009) state no appeal deadline or reapply period. Do not hardcode 21/90 days in the product; show 'check the removal email/Studio notice' instead.

## languages: Top 10 YouTube languages by audience (2025-2026) for Cashflow Studio: audience size, finance/business RPM bands, writing direction, caption script/font requirements, TTS/caption pipeline hazards, and Whisper/WhisperX word-alignment support, with ranking methodology

### Summary

YouTube does not publish audience by language, so the ranking below is built bottom-up from DataReportal's January-2025 YouTube ad-audience figures per country (India 491M, USA 253M, Brazil 144M, Indonesia 143M, Mexico 83.6M, Japan 78.7M, Germany 65.5M, Vietnam 62.3M, Philippines 57.7M, Turkey 57.5M, plus the long tail republished by WorldPopulationReview), with each country assigned to its dominant content language, India split using Google's own 54% Hindi / 16% English viewer-preference study, and Russia measured by Mediascope because Google sells no ads there. Statista's April-2026 update (India 518M, USA 259M, Indonesia third) confirms the same order at the top. Resulting rank by estimated YouTube viewers of content in the language: 1 English (~360M in the core Anglophone four, ~1.45B total reach), 2 Spanish (~300-340M), 3 Hindi (~265-280M), 4 Arabic (~190-220M, dialect-fragmented), 5 Portuguese (~155-165M), 6 Indonesian (~145-155M), 7 Japanese (~79M), 8 German (~75-80M), 9 French (~80-110M, wide uncertainty because Francophone Africa is unmeasured), 10 Russian (~100-120M viewers but Russia-located viewers are unmonetizable since March 2022 and YouTube is throttled there). Vietnamese (62M), Turkish (57.5M), Korean (43M), Filipino, Urdu and Bengali are the next candidates. The most frequently cited language-ranking blog (AIR Media-Tech, April 2026) ranks by total speakers rather than YouTube users (English 1.45B, Hindi 610M, Spanish 600M+, French 310M, Arabic 380M, Portuguese 260M, Indonesian 300M, German 180M, Japanese 125M, Vietnamese 85M, Korean 81M) and is useful only as a cross-check. Monetization is inverted relative to audience: the only well-sourced finance-niche RPM bands are US $15-26, UK $12-21, Australia $14-23, Canada $11-19, Germany $10-17, Brazil $1.60-4.20, India $0.50-1.60, Indonesia $0.40-1.30 (OutlierKit, June 2026); Japan, France, Spain, Arabic and Russian finance RPMs are not directly published and must be estimated from general RPM ($2.5-3.9 Spain/Japan/France, $0.11 Egypt, $0.65 Russia) times a 3-5x finance multiplier, so treat them as low confidence. For burned-in captions, three of the ten languages need special rendering: Hindi (Devanagari, HarfBuzz complex shaping), Arabic (RTL, contextual joining, FriBidi plus RLE/PDF wrapping of mixed numbers and Latin names, a known FFmpeg/libass artifact bug with Arial, Noto Naskh Arabic recommended) and Japanese (CJK, no word spaces, libass only wraps at ASCII spaces unless FFmpeg's wrap_unicode option with a libunibreak-enabled libass is available, kinsoku line-break rules, roughly 13 full-width characters per line). All ten languages are in Whisper's 99-language set and all ten have a default WhisperX wav2vec2 alignment model (en/fr/de/es torchaudio models; ja, pt, ar, ru, hi, id, ko, vi, tr, tl on Hugging Face), with Japanese and Chinese aligned at character level; FLEURS WER for large-v3 is roughly 3-6% for Spanish, Portuguese, English, German, French, Russian, Japanese (CER), Korean (CER), 6-9% for Indonesian and Vietnamese, 10-16% for Arabic, and 16-30% for Hindi, which on real-world audio rises to 35-45%, making Hindi the one language where Whisper-based word alignment should be treated as unreliable and the voice tool's own timestamps or sentence-level timing preferred.

### Recommendation

Adopt this ranked list for Cashflow Studio v1: 1 English, 2 Spanish, 3 Hindi, 4 Arabic (MSA), 5 Portuguese (pt-BR), 6 Indonesian, 7 Japanese, 8 German, 9 French, 10 Russian, with Vietnamese, Turkish and Korean as the next three. Ranking source: bottom-up sum of DataReportal January-2025 YouTube ad-audience by country (as published on datareportal.com and republished by WorldPopulationReview), countries assigned to their dominant content language, India split 54/16 Hindi/English per Google's 2020 viewer study, Russia measured by Mediascope because Google reports no ad audience there; Statista's April-2026 top-3 confirms the order. Note the ranking differs from AIR Media-Tech's widely copied list (which ranks Hindi over Spanish and French over Arabic/Portuguese) because that list counts speakers, not YouTube users. For the business model, separate the list into monetization tiers: Tier A (English, German, Japanese, French, Spain/US-Hispanic Spanish) where finance RPM is $6-26 and a 10-minute video at $3-5 API cost pays back on tens of thousands of views; Tier B volume languages (Hindi, Indonesian, Arabic ex-Gulf, LatAm Spanish, Portuguese) where finance RPM is $0.4-4 and the product must emphasize throughput and Shorts-to-long funnels; and Russian as a strategic exception (large audience, near-zero AdSense yield because Russia-located viewers are unmonetized since March 2022 and the platform is throttled) — ship it only if the user has a non-AdSense revenue plan. Engineering implications: (1) treat the voice-clone tool's own word/character timestamps as the primary caption clock and use WhisperX (large-v3-turbo + per-language align model, language forced) only as fallback; for Hindi, degrade to sentence-level timing when the Whisper transcript diverges from the script by more than a threshold. (2) Verify the bundled FFmpeg's libass was built with HarfBuzz, FriBidi and libunibreak (check for wrap_unicode support) before shipping Hindi/Arabic/Japanese; ship a Noto font bundle (Noto Sans, Noto Sans Devanagari, Noto Naskh Arabic, Noto Sans JP, Noto Sans KR) with a fonts.xml/fontsdir, never rely on Arial for Arabic. (3) Implement per-language caption rules: Arabic — RTL, \\an right alignment, wrap each cue in U+202B/U+202C; Japanese — kinsoku-aware wrapping at ~13 full-width chars, no ASCII-space dependence; German — allow longer lines or soft hyphens; French — non-breaking spaces before high punctuation. (4) Add an LLM 'speech normalization' pass per language before TTS: expand numbers/currency/dates in the target locale, insert Japanese readings for ambiguous kanji, optionally diacritize Arabic, provide Russian stress hints, and for Hindi choose Devanagari-only or explicit Hinglish mixed-script output. (5) Store script sentence boundaries so caption timing never depends on ASR word segmentation for Japanese. Re-verify RPM bands quarterly: all per-language RPM figures come from aggregator blogs with undocumented methods and differ by up to 2.5x between sources; only the US/UK/CA/AU/DE finance bands and the Brazil/India/Indonesia bands are corroborated by more than one source.

| 7. Japanese (ja) — rank 7 | Japan 78.7M users (6th country); premium ad market — CPM $8-15 per aggregators, general RPM $2.5-2.9 and likely $5-12 in finance (estimate). Whisper CER 4.8-5.3% on FLEURS; WhisperX ja model with character-level alignment; low competition for faceless finance/motivation in Japanese relative to English. | CJK script with no spaces: libass only breaks at ASCII spaces, so either FFmpeg's wrap_unicode (requires libass built with libunibreak) or your own kinsoku-aware line breaker inserting \N; keep ~13 full-width chars per line (Netflix guide). Fonts: Noto Sans JP/CJK or Windows Yu Gothic/Meiryo. TTS: kanji heteronyms cause >10% misreadings without context disambiguation (yomikata/furigana hints); counters and numeral readings. Whisper real-world CER 15-19.5%. Cultural localization matters more than translation. | General RPM $2.50-2.90, CPM $8-15; finance RPM unpublished | Captions: CJK, LTR, wrap_unicode/kinsoku, Noto Sans JP; Whisper ja (CER); WhisperX ja (jonatasgrosman xlsr-53-japanese, char-level); TTS needs reading disambiguation |
| 8. German (de) — rank 8 | Germany 65.5M + Austria + German-speaking Switzerland (unmeasured, est. ~10-13M) → ~75-80M. Second-highest monetization after English: finance RPM $10-17 (OutlierKit), CPM ~$9.79 (AIR), general RPM $3.00. LTR Latin with umlauts/ß; Whisper FLEURS 4.05%; WhisperX de torchaudio model; YouTube expressive auto-dubbing supports German. | Long compound words break caption line-length heuristics (allow soft hyphenation or wider boxes); decimal comma and 'Euro' verbalization; formal register (Sie) expected in finance content; strict consumer-protection sensibilities around investment advice. | Finance RPM $10-17; general RPM $1.65-4.40 | Captions: Latin LTR; Whisper/WhisperX de; TTS de-DE with locale number formatting |
| 9. French (fr) — rank 9 | France 50.4M + Belgium/Switzerland/Quebec (Canada 31.7M total, ~20-25% francophone) + Maghreb bilinguals + Francophone sub-Saharan Africa (Côte d'Ivoire, Senegal, Cameroon, DRC — unmeasured) → ~80-110M. France monetizes well: CPM ~$6.76 (AIR), general RPM $2.70 (TubeAnalytics); finance estimate $6-12 in France. LTR Latin with diacritics; Whisper FLEURS 5.97%; WhisperX fr torchaudio model; expressive auto-dubbing supported. | Audience estimate has the widest error band of the ten because African YouTube reach by language is unmeasured and African RPM is very low; typographic conventions (non-breaking space before ; : ! ?, « » quotes) must be handled in caption text; France vs Quebec vs African French vocabulary for money terms. | General RPM France ~$2.70, CPM ~$6.76; finance RPM unpublished | Captions: Latin LTR; Whisper/WhisperX fr; TTS fr-FR |
| 10. Russian (ru) — rank 10 (special case) | Still ~79M monthly viewers inside Russia (Mediascope Apr 2025) + Ukraine 21.6M (shifting to Ukrainian) + Kazakhstan/Belarus/Uzbekistan/diaspora → ~100-120M Russian-language viewers. Cyrillic is LTR and font-simple; Whisper FLEURS 5.1-5.4%; WhisperX ru model; low competition because Russian creators lost monetization. | Google serves no ads to viewers located in Russia since 3 Mar 2022 and closed Russian AdSense in Aug 2024, so only the ~20-30% of Russian-speaking viewers outside Russia monetize; YouTube has been throttled in Russia since July 2024 (daily reach halved to 27.5M) and could be fully blocked; Lenostube's Russia RPM $0.65 is likely stale. TTS: homograph stress (ударение) and numeral declension; real-world Whisper WER 29.4% on one set. | Effectively $0 for Russia-located viewers; Russian-language CPM $1-3 from non-Russia viewers (aggregator estimate) | Captions: Cyrillic LTR; Whisper/WhisperX ru; TTS needs stress disambiguation |
| Alternates 11-13: Vietnamese (vi), Turkish (tr), Korean (ko) | Vietnam 62.3M users (8th country), Turkey 57.5M (10th), South Korea 43.4M with 84% penetration and mid-tier CPM ($5.73 AIR, RPM $2.30 TubeAnalytics). All three have WhisperX default alignment models (vi nguyenvulebinh, tr mpoyraz, ko kresnik). Whisper FLEURS: Turkish 5.95%, Vietnamese 8.1-8.9%, Korean CER 2.6-4.25%. | Vietnamese: CPM ~$0.85, stacked tone-mark diacritics need a font that positions them well (test Noto Sans/Be Vietnam Pro), real-world Whisper WER 18.9%. Turkish: dotted/dotless i casing bugs, agglutinative long words. Korean: Hangul rendering needs Noto Sans KR, inconsistent spacing in casual text, real-world Whisper WER 14.1%. Filipino (57.7M) is often English/Taglish; Urdu (Pakistan 55.9M) is RTL Nastaliq with Whisper WER 21-26%; Bengali (Bangladesh 44.6M + India) has Whisper WER 48-77% and is not viable for Whisper alignment. | Vietnamese CPM ~$0.85; Korean CPM ~$5.73 / RPM $2.30; Turkish unpublished | Whisper/WhisperX supported for vi, tr, ko, tl, ur; not usable for bn |
| Word-alignment approach A: TTS-native word timestamps (voice-clone vendor) | Exact, language-agnostic, no ASR error; works for Hindi/Arabic/Japanese where Whisper degrades; zero extra compute. | Depends on the vendor exposing character/word timings; not all voice-clone tools do; must be mapped back to script sentences (script text == TTS input, so mapping is deterministic). | Included in the user's existing voice subscription | Primary source of caption timing; fall back to B only when unavailable |
| Word-alignment approach B: WhisperX (faster-whisper large-v3/turbo + wav2vec2 forced alignment) | Free, local (CPU works, GPU faster), <100 ms word timestamps, default align models for all 10 target languages plus vi/tr/ko/tl/ur; character-level for ja/zh; VAD batching. | Quality tracks Whisper WER: excellent for es/pt/en/de/fr/ru/id, acceptable for ar/ja/ko/vi, poor for hi (16-45% WER, community align model); must force language code; English loanwords in Hindi transcripts returned in Devanagari; needs ~10 GB VRAM for large-v3 or use turbo/medium on laptops. | $0 (open source) | pip install whisperx; whisperx.load_align_model(language_code); pass --align_model for unsupported languages |
| Word-alignment approach C: Whisper built-in word_timestamps (DTW on cross-attention) or stable-ts | No extra alignment model to download; works for all 99 languages including those without wav2vec2 models; stable-ts refines timestamps and supports Japanese/Chinese segmentation. | Less precise than forced alignment (timestamps drift 100-300 ms), token-level for CJK (needs regrouping), same Hindi WER problem. | $0 (open source) | openai-whisper / faster-whisper word_timestamps=True; stable-ts for regrouping |

### Facts

- (high, 2025-01) YouTube's global ad audience was 2.53 billion in January 2025 (30.9% of world population, 45.5% of internet users); top 10 countries: India 491M, USA 253M, Brazil 144M, Indonesia 143M, Mexico 83.6M, Japan 78.7M, Germany 65.5M, Vietnam 62.3M, Philippines 57.7M, Turkey 57.5M. - https://datareportal.com/essential-youtube-stats

- (medium, 2025-10) Statista October 2025 snapshot: India ~500M, USA 254M, Indonesia 151M YouTube users. - https://www.statista.com/statistics/280685/number-of-monthly-unique-youtube-users/
- (medium, 2025) Country-level YouTube users 2025 (WorldPopulationReview republishing DataReportal-type figures): Pakistan 55.9M, Egypt 50.7M, France 50.4M, Thailand 47.6M, Bangladesh 44.6M, South Korea 43.4M, Italy 42.2M, Spain 39.7M, Argentina 32.2M, Canada 31.7M, Colombia 30.6M, Saudi Arabia 27.2M, Nigeria 27M, Malaysia 25.1M, Iraq 22.3M, Ukraine 21.6M, Algeria 21.1M, Morocco 21.1M, UK 54.8M, Australia 20.9M, Peru 19.1M, Chile 14.8M, Ecuador 11.4M, UAE 8.3M, Portugal 7.5M, Dominican Republic 7.2M, Tunisia 7.0M, Guatemala 7.0M, Jordan 6.5M, Bolivia 5.9M, Honduras 4.6M, Nicaragua 4.1M, Paraguay 3.8M, Costa Rica 3.8M, El Salvador 3.5M, Oman 3.3M, Lebanon 3.2M, Kuwait 3.2M, Panama 2.4M, Uruguay 2.4M, Qatar 2.3M, Bahrain 1.2M. Table omits Russia, Venezuela, Cuba, Yemen, Syria, Libya, Sudan, Austria, Switzerland, Belgium, Kazakhstan, Angola, Mozambique. - https://worldpopulationreview.com/country-rankings/youtube-users-by-country

- (high, 2025-11-20) Pew Research (survey Feb 5-Jun 18 2025, n=5,022): 84% of US adults use YouTube, the most-used platform; 48% use it daily. - https://www.pewresearch.org/internet/2025/11/20/americans-social-media-use-
- (medium, 2020-06-04) Google's 'The Indian Online Video Viewer' report: Indian viewers' preferred video language was Hindi 54%, English 16%, Telugu 7%, Kannada 6%, Tamil 5%, Bengali 3%. This is the basis for splitting India's audience between Hindi and English; it is from 2020 and may be outdated (regional-language share has been growing). - https://www.advanced-television.com/?p=157196

Figures are total speakers, not YouTube users; methodology undocumented. - https://air.io/en/audience-growth/what-are-the-most-popular-languages-on-youtube

Aggregator blog, methodology undocumented. - https://www.bemultilingual.ca/blog/most-popular-languages-on-youtube-languages-with-the-highest-cpm-and-what

Sub-niches: credit cards/loans $20-30+, investing/stocks $15-25, tax/accounting $15-25, personal finance/budgeting $8-15. No Japanese, Spanish, Arabic, Russian or French figures; sources are creator earnings reports and 'industry benchmarks'. - https://outlierkit.com/blog/youtube-rpm-finance-niche

Note these US/AU figures are far above other sources, illustrating the spread between aggregator sites. - https://www.lenostube.com/en/youtube-cpm-rpm-rates/

- (low, 2026-03) Motivation/self-improvement niche: RPM estimate $5.70 with CPM $9-12 (March 2026); audits of faceless motivation/self-improvement channels report average RPM $10.2 (20 channels), $10.9 (32 channels), $11.9 (8 channels); low end $4-8. Business/entrepreneurship CPM $8-30, RPM $4-14 (predominantly English-language channels). - https://outlierkit.com/resources/youtube-rpm-benchmarks/
- (low, 2025-12-26) Faceless motivation channel audits (Autonolab, Nov-Dec 2025) report average RPM around $10-12 for English-language motivation and self-improvement channels. - https://blog.autonolab.com/niches/2025-12-26-faceless-youtube-motivation/
- (high, 2025-08-07) Mediascope via Meduza: YouTube monthly reach in Russia fell from at least 95 million (H1 2024) to about 79 million by April 2025; daily reach fell from 55 million to 27.5 million; throttling began July 2024 and traffic fell to 20% of normal by December 2024. - https://meduza.io/en/feature/2025/08/07/from-youtube-to-boob-tube
- (high, 2022-03-03) Google/YouTube officially paused Google and YouTube ads from serving to users located in Russia and paused all monetization features for viewers in Russia as of 3 March 2022; channels linked to Russia-based AdSense accounts cannot be monetized. - https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/87047
- (high, 2024-08) Google deactivated Russia-based AdSense accounts and sent final payments effective August 2024. - https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/google/google-deactivates-russian-adsense-accounts-sends-final-payments/
- (medium, 2026-06) YouTube auto-dubbing is now available to all creators with 27 languages; dubbing from English supported into 20 languages (incl. Hindi, French, German, Japanese, Korean, Portuguese, Spanish, Bengali); 'expressive speech' dubbing available for English, French, German, Hindi, Indonesian, Italian, Portuguese, Spanish; ~6 million daily viewers watched 10+ minutes of auto-dubbed content in December 2025; creators can also upload their own multi-language audio tracks. - https://www.tbsnews.net/node/1353236
- (high, 2023-11-06) Whisper large-v3 (released 6 Nov 2023) was trained on 1M hours weakly labeled + 4M hours pseudo-labeled audio, shows 10-20% error reduction vs large-v2 on Common Voice 15 and FLEURS; CER is used for Chinese, Japanese, Thai, Lao, Myanmar and Korean; Cantonese token added. - https://github.com/openai/whisper/discussions/1762
- (high, 2025-03-26) Whisper large-v3 FLEURS WER (Dolphin paper, 26 Mar 2025): Japanese 4.8, Korean 2.6, Russian 5.4, Indonesian 6.5, Chinese 7.2, Vietnamese 8.9, Arabic 10.5, Hindi 16.5, Thai 19.1, Urdu 21.5, Bengali 47.9. Common Voice: Russian 7.4, Indonesian 7.8, Korean 11.9, Vietnamese 14.8, Japanese 15.2, Arabic 33.5, Hindi 35.2, Urdu 25.1, Bengali 77.0. Dataocean AI real-world set: Indonesian 10.2, Korean 14.1, Vietnamese 18.9, Japanese 19.5, Arabic 21.7, Urdu 25.8, Russian 29.4, Hindi 45.4, Bengali 71.0. - https://arxiv.org/html/2503.20212
- (medium, 2026-08-25) Independent FLEURS run (WhisperNotes, 25 Aug 2026, ~150 sentences/language) for Whisper large-v3-turbo: Spanish 3.62% WER, German 4.05, Korean 4.25 CER, Japanese 5.30 CER, Portuguese 5.44, English 5.49, Russian 5.13, Turkish 5.95, French 5.97, Indonesian 6.31, Vietnamese 8.10, Filipino 9.72, Malay 9.22, Arabic 15.72, Hindi 29.64, Cantonese 36.75 CER. - https://whispernotes.app/blog/qwen3-asr-vs-whisper
- (high, 2026) WhisperX default alignment models: torchaudio for en (WAV2VEC2_ASR_BASE_960H), fr, de, es, it (VOXPOPULI_ASR_BASE_10K_*); Hugging Face for ja, zh, nl, uk, pt, ar, cs, ru, pl, hu, fi, fa, el, tr, da, he, vi, ko, ur, te, hi (theainerd/Wav2Vec2-large-xlsr-hindi), ca, ml, no, nn, sk, sl, hr, ro, eu, gl, ka, lv, tl, sv, id (cahya/wav2vec2-large-xlsr-indonesian). Languages without a default model raise 'No default alignment model for language' and need --align_model. - https://raw.githubusercontent.com/m-bain/whisperX/main/whisperx/alignment.py
- (high, 2025) WhisperX treats Japanese and Chinese as LANGUAGES_WITHOUT_SPACES and aligns them at character level; alignment uses a character-based wav2vec2 phoneme model giving sub-100ms word timestamps. - https://huggingface.co/spaces/Sven33/SATEv1.5/blob/main/transcription/whisperx/alignment.py
- (medium, 2025) Whisper transcribes English loanwords in Hinglish speech into Devanagari (e.g. 'next quarter mein launch karenge' -> 'नेक्स्ट क्वार्टर में लॉन्च करेंगे'), base models can misidentify Hindi as Urdu (Nastaliq script) under auto-detect, so language=hi must be forced; no official mixed-script option exists (Trelis fine-tune adds a |mixedcode| token). - https://convertaudiototext.com/blog/hindi-transcription-code-switching
- (medium, 2023-12) Code-mixed Hindi-English (Hinglish) TTS research uses transliteration of English into Devanagari and single-script training; open-source Hinglish TTS pipelines normalize Roman-script Hindi via IndicXlit and must handle matras, conjunct consonants and schwa deletion. - https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.01103
- (medium, 2025-12) Most Arabic text is unvowelized; TTS front-ends either require or infer tashkeel (diacritics); automatic diacritizers have advanced substantially and some engines treat tashkeel as optional and infer vowels/stress. - https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.00937
- (medium, 2013 (still applies to build requirements)) Without HarfBuzz support, libass renders all Indic scripts and proper Arabic incorrectly; libass uses FriBidi for bidirectional ordering and HarfBuzz as OpenType shaper. - https://bugs.archlinux.org/task/38249
- (medium, 2021-03) FFmpeg's subtitles filter was changed to use automatic font-shaping selection rather than the 'simple' shaper so Indic scripts requiring complex shaping render correctly (March 2021 patch). - https://ffmpeg.org/pipermail/ffmpeg-devel/2021-March/277946.html
- (medium, 2025-04-30) FFmpeg trac #11566 (April 2025, FFmpeg N-119396): burning Arabic SRT with subtitles filter and force_style FontName=Arial draws a box outline in the middle of Arabic words; related KDE bug had persisted ~2 years; unconfirmed, no workaround given. - https://ffmpeg.org/pipermail/ffmpeg-trac/2025-April/073389.html
- (medium, 2026) Mixed RTL/LTR subtitle cues (Arabic with numbers or Latin brand names) misorder without bidi control; standard fix is wrapping each cue in U+202B (RLE) ... U+202C (PDF). - https://videodubbing.com/blog/post/fix-arabic-hebrew-subtitles-rtl-fixer-guide-2026/
- (high, 2023-05) FFmpeg subtitles filter gained a wrap_unicode option (May 2023) that breaks lines by the Unicode Line Breaking Algorithm for CJK text without spaces; requires libass >= 0x01600010 built with libunibreak; enabled by default for non-ASS inputs, must not be used for native ASS. - https://ffmpeg.org/pipermail/ffmpeg-devel/2023-May/310008.html
- (high, v4.0, current) Japanese line breaking follows kinsoku shori (JIS X 4051): certain characters cannot start or end a line; Netflix Japanese timed-text guide limits horizontal subtitles to 13 full-width characters per line (half-width counts 0.5), vertical 11. - https://backlothelp.netflix.com/hc/en-us/articles/215767517-Japanese-Timed-Text-Style-Guide-v4-0
- (medium, 2025) Japanese TTS misreads sentences more than 10% of the time mainly due to kanji heteronyms; context-based readers (e.g. yomikata, BERT-based) output furigana to disambiguate readings. - https://deepgram.com/learn/developers-guide-fixing-tts-pronunciation-errors
- (medium, ongoing) Vietnamese stacks diacritics (tone mark over vowel diacritic); fonts must offset or stack marks to avoid collisions, and not all rendering systems handle two or more marks on one base letter; some Noto variants position diacritics imperfectly on screen. - https://vietnamesetypography.com/design-challenges/
- (medium, current wiki) On Windows, libass/FFmpeg subtitles rendering needs fontconfig environment (FONTCONFIG_FILE/FONTCONFIG_PATH or fontsdir option) and a fonts.xml including C:\Windows\Fonts; Noto script fonts are separate families (e.g. Noto Sans Devanagari, Noto Naskh Arabic) and fontconfig does not automatically prefer them for non-Latin scripts. - https://trac.ffmpeg.org/wiki/Windows_Tips
- (medium, 2025) Saudi Arabia leads the MENA region in YouTube video playbacks, followed by Egypt, Morocco and UAE; Google reports YouTube is the preferred platform for viewers in Saudi Arabia and UAE (Brandcast MENA). - https://blog.google/intl/ar-mena/products/connect-communicate/youtube-brandcast-advertisers-mena-saudi-uae-tv/
- (low, 2025-04-26) Number of Saudi channels earning seven figures in riyals rose 40% YoY as of December 2024; UAE saw a 15% increase in dirham-denominated earnings (YouTube MENA statement). - https://dev.scenenoise.com/Buzz/YouTube-Reports-Surge-in-MENA-Creators-Earning-Seven-Figure-Revenues
- (low, 2025) Vietnam had 62.3M, Turkey 57.5M, Philippines 57.7M, South Korea 43.4M, France 50.4M, Russia ~107M (pre-throttling estimate) YouTube users in 2025 per aggregator compilations of DataReportal data. - https://sqmagazine.co.uk/how-many-people-use-youtube/
- (high, 2025-01-15) VKontakte's monthly reach surpassed YouTube's in Russia for the first time in December 2024 after throttling. - https://meduza.io/en/news/2025/01/15/vkontakte-surpasses-youtube-in-monthly-audience-reach-in-russia-for-first-time

### Open questions

- Does the user's chosen voice-cloning tool return word- or character-level timestamps for all ten languages (and specifically for Hindi, Arabic and Japanese)? This determines whether WhisperX is a fallback or the primary clock.
- Is the FFmpeg build the user ships compiled with libass + HarfBuzz + FriBidi + libunibreak (needed for Arabic/Devanagari shaping and CJK wrap_unicode)? Must be checked with `ffmpeg -buildconf` / a render test on each target machine; the gyan.dev and BtbN Windows builds should be verified rather than assumed.
- Arabic variant decision: MSA narration (region-wide, TTS-friendly, lower engagement) vs Egyptian or Gulf dialect (higher engagement, splits the audience); also whether Gulf-only targeting (Saudi/UAE, higher CPM) is preferable to pan-Arab reach.
- Finance-niche RPM for Japanese, French, Spanish (Spain vs LatAm), Arabic (Gulf vs Egypt) and Korean is not published anywhere credible; the 3-5x general-RPM multiplier is an estimate and should be validated against the user's own AdSense data or Nexlev/vidIQ channel RPM lookups after launch.
- India language split relies on a 2020 Google study (Hindi 54%, English 16%); regional-language share has grown since, so the Hindi audience may be smaller and the Tamil/Telugu/Bengali opportunity larger than modelled.
- Austria, Switzerland, Belgium, Venezuela, Kazakhstan, Angola, Yemen, Syria, Sudan and Francophone Africa YouTube audiences were not in the available tables; German, French, Spanish, Arabic and Russian totals carry ±10-25% uncertainty.
- Should Russian be included at all given zero ad yield for Russia-located viewers and the possibility of a full block, or replaced by Vietnamese/Turkish/Korean which are monetizable?
- Whisper paper appendix tables (per-language FLEURS/Common Voice WER for large-v2, training hours per language) could not be parsed from the PDF in this environment; the Dolphin (Mar 2025) and WhisperNotes (Aug 2026) third-party runs were used instead and show different absolute numbers for Hindi (16.5% vs 29.6%).
- Quality of the WhisperX community alignment models for Hindi (theainerd), Vietnamese, Turkish and Korean has not been benchmarked here; a 20-clip internal test per language is advisable before relying on them.
- YouTube's auto-dubbing (27 languages, available to all creators) may make per-language native production unnecessary for some Tier-B languages; the architect should decide whether Cashflow Studio produces native-language videos or English masters plus uploaded multi-language audio tracks.

### Fact-check

Confirmed claims: 31

- REFUTED: Claim 9: BeMultilingual (2026) CPM compilation: Spanish ~$3.00 avg with US-Hispanic $8-12; Japanese $8-15; Hindi ~$0.53; Arabic $1.80; Indonesian $0.41; Russian $1-3; Korean $2.86. - Fetched 2026-10-09: the page (published 26 Jul 2025, header updated 1 Sep 2026) lists Spanish $0.91, Hindi $0.53, Russian $1.37 (flagged non-monetizable as of Jan 2026) and Indonesian $0.61; it gives NO CPM for Japanese, Arabic or Korean and no US-Hispanic figure. Its stated data source is a single 2022 'My YouTube earnings' post by Brick Experiment Channel (2017-2023 views on a channel with no spoken content), not a multi-channel compilation. Only the Hindi figure matches. Confidence: high that the cited figures are not on the page; possible the researcher saw an earlier revision. Correction: Treat BeMultilingual as a single-channel anecdote: Spanish $0.91, Hindi $0.53, Russian $1.37, Indonesian $0.61 (CPM, 2017-2023 data). Drop the Japanese/Arabic/Korean/US-Hispanic numbers attributed to it. https://www.bemultilingual.ca/blog/most-popular-languages-on-youtube-languages-with-the-highest-cpm-and-what
- REFUTED: Claim 15: Saudi Arabia CPM ~$1-3 vs USA $5-15; UAE is a Tier-2 market with CPM $3-18; some Saudi YouTubers target US audiences because of low regional CPM. - The Digital Information World article (11 Aug 2025) gives point values USA $11.95, Saudi Arabia $2.18, UAE $3.03, sourced from IsThisChannelMonetized (analytics on videos uploaded in 2024, >=1,000 views per country). There is no '$3-18 Tier-2' band and no statement about Saudi creators targeting US audiences; it only says creators generally should target higher-CPM countries. Confidence: high. - REFUTED: Claim 38: On Windows, libass/FFmpeg subtitles rendering needs a fontconfig environment (FONTCONFIG_FILE/FONTCONFIG_PATH or fontsdir) and a fonts.xml including C:\Windows\Fonts. - libass selects font providers in the order CoreText -> DirectWrite -> fontconfig (font_constructors[] in ass_fontselect.c, ASS_FONTPROVIDER_AUTODETECT takes the first that initializes), so a Windows libass built with DirectWrite finds installed system fonts with no fontconfig configuration. BtbN's FFmpeg Windows builds compile libass with -Ddirectwrite=enabled (plus -Dfontconfig=enabled and -Dlibunibreak=enabled). The cited trac wiki page could not be fetched (blocked by Anubis), so its wording could not be checked. fontconfig IS still relevant for the drawtext filter. Confidence: medium-high. Correction: For the subtitles filter on Windows, ship fonts via the filter's fontsdir option (ass_set_fonts_dir) plus explicit FontName in force_style, or rely on DirectWrite finding installed fonts; fontconfig env vars are only needed for drawtext or a libass built without DirectWrite. Verify the bundle with `ffmpeg -h filter=subtitles` (look for wrap_unicode and shaping options). https://raw.githubusercontent.com/libass/libass/master/libass/ass_fontselect.c

Claim 23: the Dolphin paper tables label all figures WER (including Japanese/Korean), not CER as the summary states. Claim 25: exact error string is 'No default align-model for language: {code}'. Claim 26: LANGUAGES_WITHOUT_SPACES = ['ja','zh'] confirmed from the main repo; the 'sub-100ms' claim was not verified.
