Claim 33 confirmed, but that blog's statement that FFmpeg 'defaults to the simple shaper' is outdated since the March-2021 patch (claim 31). The WhisperX GitHub releases fetch returned garbled years (showed 2024 for 3.8.x); PyPI dates (3.8.6 = 25 May 2026) are authoritative. libass README fetches failed (503/garbled); the provider-order finding comes from ass_fontselect.c directly. The ffmpeg.org trac wiki is behind an Anubis challenge and could not be fetched. RANKING IMPACT: none of the refutations change the top-10 membership; the only ordering sensitivity is Indonesian vs Portuguese (Indonesia 151M vs Brazil 150M in late-2025 data). ARCHITECTURE IMPACT: (1) forced alignment against the known script (MMS_FA/ctc-forced-aligner for hi/ar/id, Qwen3-ForcedAligner or WhisperX for en/es/pt/fr/de/ja/ru) should be the guaranteed path because the voice tool is unnamed and may give no timestamps; (2) the July-2025 inauthentic-content policy is the single biggest unaddressed risk for an AI-slideshow product; (3) ship BtbN-style FFmpeg (libunibreak + DirectWrite) rather than assuming fontconfig.

## voice: Voice-cloning TTS providers (non-ElevenLabs) with timestamp support, forced-alignment fallbacks, and a Python voice-provider abstraction for Cashflow Studio (top-10 YouTube languages, Oct 2026)

### Summary

As of Oct 2026 the hosted voice-cloning market has shifted sharply: Play.ht/PlayAI is gone (API dead 26 Jul 2025, platform deleted 31 Dec 2025), Hume is sunsetting its TTS and EVI APIs on 13 Nov 2026, and Resemble pulled its public per-second TTS and $2/$5 clone pricing on 14 Jul 2026 while pivoting to deepfake detection, so none of those three should anchor the design. The strongest cloud candidates for a pipeline that needs clones plus word timing in the top-10 YouTube languages (English, Hindi, Spanish, French, Arabic, Portuguese, Indonesian, German, Japanese, Korean per AIR Media-Tech Apr 2026; Russian and Vietnamese are close alternates) are MiniMax Speech 2.8/2.6 (word-level subtitle timestamps via subtitle_type="word", 40-language language_boost list covering all ten, $60/1M chars turbo, $100/1M HD, $1.50 per rapid clone) and Cartesia Sonic-3.6 (add_timestamps word arrays on WebSocket/SSE, 44 languages covering all ten, roughly $37-50 per 1M characters depending on tier, instant cloning and commercial license from the $5 Pro tier). Fish Audio S2.1 Pro is the cheapest clone-capable API ($15 per 1M UTF-8 bytes, 83 languages, s2.1-pro-free at $0 through 30 Nov 2026) but returns no timestamps, so it needs a forced-alignment stage; Speechify and Inworld return word-level marks cheaply but their current models cover only 7 and about 15 locales respectively, missing Hindi/Arabic/Indonesian (Speechify) or Indonesian (Inworld). Google Chirp 3 Instant Custom Voice ($60/1M, 33+ languages, no timepoints) and Azure Personal Voice ($24/1M plus $600 per 1,000 profiles/month, 91 languages, word-boundary events only on the Phoenix base model) both require allow-list/limited-access approval and a scripted consent recording, which makes them poor fits for many non-developer users. Murf returns wordDurations and is cheap ($10-30/1M) but restricts cloning to Enterprise. On the open-source side, Chatterbox Multilingual (MIT, 23 languages incl. Hindi/Arabic but not Indonesian), Qwen3-TTS (Apache-2.0, 10 languages, 3-second clones) and GPT-SoVITS (MIT, 5 languages, Windows one-click package) are the commercially safe local clones; XTTS-v2 (CPML non-commercial, no licensor since Coqui closed) and F5-TTS (CC-BY-NC weights) are not; IndexTTS-2.5 needs a bilibili commercial agreement; Kokoro is Apache-2.0 but cannot clone. No open model emits word timestamps, so the pipeline must own an aligner: WhisperX (wav2vec2 CTC, pip-installable, CUDA 12.8 or slow CPU int8, HF align models for all ten target languages) is the pragmatic default, with Qwen3-ForcedAligner-0.6B (Apache-2.0, 11 languages, no Hindi/Arabic/Indonesian) as a higher-accuracy option where supported, MFA 3.x for sub-15 ms research-grade alignment but conda-only and no Hindi/Indonesian/Arabic pretrained models, aeneas-ng for a fast CPU-only sentence-level fallback (38 languages, no Hindi/Indonesian/Korean), and stable-ts now archived (development paused 30 May 2026). The recommended abstraction is a capability-declaring VoiceProvider protocol (clone, synthesize, list_voices) returning a normalized SynthResult whose word timings are tagged with their source (provider word, provider char-derived, provider sentence, or aligner), so the FFmpeg timeline and caption burner never care which vendor produced the audio.

### Recommendation

1) Vendor shortlist. Because the user already pays for an (unnamed) cloning tool, design for plug-in adapters and ship three cloud adapters plus one local adapter in this order: (a) MiniMax Speech 2.8-turbo/hd — the only mainstream non-ElevenLabs API that gives native word timestamps AND covers all ten target languages AND clones for $1.50 per voice; (b) Cartesia Sonic-3.6 — word timestamps on WebSocket/SSE, 44 languages, instant clone + commercial license from $5/mo; (c) Fish Audio S2.1 Pro — cheapest ($15/1M UTF-8 bytes, free model until 30 Nov 2026) but timestamp-less, so it exercises the aligner path; (d) local Chatterbox Multilingual (MIT; Hindi+Arabic) with Qwen3-TTS (Apache) as a second local option, both routed through the aligner. Treat Inworld TTS-2 as the fourth cloud adapter if the user's paid tool turns out to be PlayHT-derived. Do NOT build on Play.ht (dead), Hume (sunset 13 Nov 2026), XTTS-v2 / F5-TTS (non-commercial weights), IndexTTS (commercial agreement needed), Kokoro (no cloning) or Murf (clone is Enterprise-only); treat Resemble as high-risk until it confirms commercial generative pricing (publicly withdrawn 14 Jul 2026); treat Google ICV and Azure Personal Voice as 'enterprise only' because both require allow-list/limited-access approval and scripted consent recordings, and Azure Dragon models give no word boundaries anyway. Expected voice cost per 10-minute English video (~1,400 words, ~9K chars): Fish $0.14, Speechify $0.09 (EN/EU only), Inworld Flash $0.14, Cartesia $0.35-0.45, MiniMax turbo $0.54 / HD $0.90, Google ICV $0.54, Azure PV $0.22 + $0.60 per stored profile per month; Devanagari/Arabic/Japanese scripts cost 2-3x on byte-billed Fish.

2) Timestamp strategy. Never let the editor depend on vendor timing semantics. Normalize every synthesis into WordTiming(text, start_s, end_s, confidence, source) where source is one of PROVIDER_WORD (MiniMax, Cartesia, Inworld, Speechify, Murf), PROVIDER_CHAR_DERIVED (Resemble graph_times grouped into words), PROVIDER_SENTENCE (MiniMax sentence mode) or ALIGNED (forced alignment). If a provider returns nothing or only sentence-level data, run a ForcedAligner on the known script, not an ASR pass: default WhisperX align (load_align_model(lang) + align() with one synthetic segment per script sentence spanning the clip) because its torchaudio/HF align models cover all ten languages and it is pip-installable on Windows; use Qwen3-ForcedAligner-0.6B when the language is in its 11 and a GPU is present (better accuracy, Apache-2.0); fall back to aeneas-ng sentence alignment on CPU-only machines where WhisperX is too slow; keep MFA out of the desktop build (conda/Kaldi) and avoid MMS_FA for commercial use. Always verbalize numerals, currency and abbreviations (num2words per language) in the alignment copy while keeping the display copy for captions, because CTC aligners skip non-dictionary tokens. Synthesize per sentence (or per scene) rather than per script so sentence boundaries are known exactly from concatenation offsets even when word alignment is weak; this also stays under the smallest request limits (Resemble 2,000 chars, Hume 5,000, MiniMax 10,000, Fish chunk_length 300 internal).

3) Python interface (what every adapter must implement):

class ProviderCapabilities(BaseModel): provider_id: str; can_clone: bool; clone_min_seconds: float; clone_max_seconds: float; needs_consent_recording: bool; languages: set[str] (BCP-47); timestamp_granularity: Literal['word','char','sentence','none']; timestamps_require_streaming: bool; max_chars_per_request: int; billing_unit: Literal['char','utf8_byte','second','credit']; price_per_unit_usd: Decimal; supports_speed: bool; supports_style: bool; output_formats: set[str]; max_concurrency: int.

class VoiceProvider(Protocol):
  caps: ProviderCapabilities
  def list_voices(self, language: str | None = None) -> list[Voice]
  def create_clone(self, samples: list[Path], name: str, consent: ConsentRecord, language_hint: str | None = None) -> Voice   # ConsentRecord stores who/when/how consent was captured, plus the provider's consent artefact id (Azure/Google require a scripted recording; Fish/MiniMax/Cartesia require you to hold rights)
  def synthesize(self, req: SynthRequest) -> SynthResult   # one call per sentence/scene chunk
  def estimate_cost(self, text: str, language: str) -> Decimal   # must count UTF-8 bytes for Fish, characters elsewhere
  def health(self) -> ProviderHealth   # auth, quota, concurrency, deprecation date (Hume Nov 2026, Fish s1 Dec 2026)

class SynthRequest(BaseModel): text: str (plain text; adapters do their own SSML/markup); voice_id: str; language: str; speed: float = 1.0; style: dict[str, Any] = {} (provider-specific emotion/temperature); seed: int | None; output: AudioSpec(format='wav', sample_rate=48000, channels=1, bit_depth=16) — PCM WAV so FFmpeg never re-decodes lossy audio; pronunciation_overrides: list[tuple[str,str]]; idempotency_key: str = sha256(provider, model, voice, text, params) for the on-disk cache.

class SynthResult(BaseModel): audio_path: Path; sample_rate: int; duration_s: float; words: list[WordTiming]; sentences: list[SentenceTiming]; timing_source: Literal['provider_word','provider_char','provider_sentence','aligned','none']; alignment_confidence: float; cost_usd: Decimal; raw: dict (provider response minus audio, for debugging); model: str; provider_id: str.

class ForcedAligner(Protocol): caps: AlignerCapabilities(languages, device in {'cuda','cpu'}, license); def align(self, audio: Path, text: str, language: str) -> list[WordTiming].

class VoiceService: picks provider by caps.languages ∩ requested language, calls synthesize per sentence with a per-provider semaphore (Fish 5 concurrent on Starter, Inworld clone 2-20 req/min), concatenates WAVs with sample-accurate offsets, shifts word times by chunk offset, invokes ForcedAligner when timing_source in {'provider_sentence','none'} or alignment_confidence < threshold, writes a single timeline.json (sentences + words + sources) that the FFmpeg builder and CapCut-style editor consume; logs cost per video; redacts API keys; keeps consent records with each Voice.

Operational rules: store provider credentials per user in Windows Credential Manager/keyring; cache audio by idempotency_key on the user's local drive; add a 'timestamp QA' step that flags words whose aligned duration is <40 ms or >2 s; expose a manual nudge in the editor since even good aligners drift on names and numbers; schedule periodic health() checks for deprecation dates so users are warned before Hume (Nov 2026) or Fish s1 (Dec 2026) style shutdowns.

### Options

| Option | Pros | Cons | Pricing | API / integration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| MiniMax Speech 2.8 / 2.6 (HD and Turbo) | Native word-level timestamps (subtitle_type='word' or 'word_streaming'); language_boost covers all ten target languages plus Russian/Vietnamese; cheapest cloning ($1.50 per voice, 10 s-5 min sample); 10,000 chars per request; async long-form up to 1M chars; commercial API with pay-as-you-go. | Per-character price is mid-high ($60-100/1M); non-streaming docs are ambiguous about whether word-level subtitles are in the subtitle_file (sentence-level mentioned) — prototype must confirm; Chinese vendor data-residency considerations; consent is contractual (user must hold rights) not enforced by a consent recording. | speech-2.8/2.6-turbo $60 per 1M characters; speech-2.8/2.6-hd $100 per 1M; rapid clone $1.50 per voice; ~ $0.50-0.90 per 10-minute English video (~9K chars). | REST POST /v1/t2a_v2 (hex or URL output; mp3/wav/pcm/flac/opus) and streaming; clone via /v1/files/upload + /v1/voice_clone; official MCP server exists. |
| Cartesia Sonic-3.6 | Word (and phoneme) timestamps as words/start/end arrays on WebSocket and SSE; 44 languages covering all ten targets; instant voice cloning and commercial license from $5/mo Pro tier; Professional Voice Clone slots on Startup+; very low latency; Python/TS SDKs. | Timestamps only on streaming endpoints (WebSocket/SSE), so the adapter must reassemble audio chunks and offsets; credit-based plans rather than pure pay-as-you-go; effective ~$37-50 per 1M characters; PVC consent process documented mostly for enterprise. | Pro $5/mo = 100K credits (~$50/1M chars); Startup $49/mo = 1.25M credits (~$39/1M); Scale $299/mo = 8M credits (~$37/1M); 1 credit per character; ~750-800 credits per audio minute. | WebSocket /tts/websocket and SSE /tts/sse with add_timestamps=true, add_phoneme_timestamps; model_id sonic-3.6 / sonic-latest; voice cloning endpoints; cartesia Python SDK. |
| Fish Audio S2.1 Pro / S2 Pro | Cheapest clone-capable API at $15 per 1M UTF-8 bytes (s2.1-pro-free is $0 through 30 Nov 2026); 83 languages; zero-shot cloning from ~10 s via reference_id or inline references; wav/pcm/mp3/opus output; Plus/Pro tiers include commercial rights. | No timestamps of any kind — requires a forced-alignment stage; billed per UTF-8 byte so Hindi/Arabic/Japanese cost 2-3x; free model has no SLA and may train on requests; free tier is personal-use only; concurrency limit 5 until $100 spent; Hindi/Indonesian presence in the 83 languages should be confirmed on the voice/language list. | $15 / 1M UTF-8 bytes (s1, s2-pro, s2.1-pro); s2.1-pro-free $0; ~ $0.14 per 10-minute English video, ~$0.30-0.40 for Devanagari/Arabic scripts. | REST POST /v1/tts (JSON or MessagePack, model header); voice management API; Python SDK fish-audio-sdk. |
| Inworld Realtime TTS-2 / TTS-2 Flash (replacement for Play.ht) | Word and character timestamps on streaming and non-streaming endpoints with phoneme/viseme detail; instant voice cloning and commercial license included on the free On-Demand plan (70 min free); batch API 20% cheaper; explicit migration path from PlayHT. | ~15 languages (Indonesian missing per third-party list; exact list behind login-gated docs); TTS-2 at $25/1M is pricier than MiniMax turbo per character on low tiers; professional cloning is an add-on from Developer tier. | TTS-2 $25/1M chars On-Demand (down to $12.50 Growth, ~$5 enterprise); TTS-2 Flash $15/1M On-Demand ($7 Growth). | REST/streaming TTS with timestampType WORD/CHARACTER and timestampTransportStrategy SYNC/ASYNC; voice cloning API with per-plan rate limits (2-20 req/min). |
| Speechify API (Simba 3.0 / 3.2) | Word-level speech marks (SSE /v1/audio/stream/with-timestamps or batch /v1/audio/speech); zero-shot cloning self-serve on all paid plans; very cheap ($6-10 per 1M chars) with 500K chars free per month. | Current models cover only English (3.2) and EN/DE/ES/FR/IT/PT-BR (3.0) — no Hindi, Arabic, Indonesian, Japanese, Korean; the 30-language legacy Simba Multilingual is retired from API version 2026-09-21 and is remapped to current models from 2026-11-21, so multilingual coverage is in flux. | Starter $10/mo at $10/1M chars; Pro $99/mo at $8/1M; Scale $499/mo at $6/1M; Free 500K chars/month. | REST + SSE; model ids simba-3.0 (default) and simba-3.2; speech marks {type:'word', start_time, end_time ms, value}. |
| Murf API (Gen2 / Falcon / Falcon 2) | Returns wordDurations (start/end ms per word) in the generate response; 35+ languages, 200+ stock voices; cheap ($10/1M Falcon, $30/1M Gen2) with $10 free credit per month. | Voice cloning is Enterprise-only with no published price — fails the 'clone the user's voice' requirement for self-serve users; architecture aimed at stock voices. | Falcon $0.01 per 1K chars ($10/1M) or $0.01/min; Gen2 $0.03 per 1K chars ($30/1M); 100K-char free trial. | REST POST /v1/speech/generate (audioFile URL or encodedAudio, wordDurations); voices list endpoint. |

Hindi and many Indic languages; Google reliability; en-US clones can transfer to DE/ES/FR/PT; $30/1M Chirp 3 HD stock voices as a fallback with 1M free chars/month. | Allow-list only (contact sales) — unusable for self-serve multi-user rollout; mandatory fixed consent script recording per language; no SSML <mark>, timepoints or word timestamps for Chirp 3 HD / ICV, so alignment is required; $60/1M with no free tier. | Instant Custom Voice $60 per 1M characters (no free tier); Chirp 3: HD $30/1M (1M free/month). | Cloud TTS REST/gRPC v1/v1beta1 with voice cloning key; Python client google-cloud-texttospeech. |
| Azure AI Speech — Personal Voice | 91 languages / 100+ locales with sentence-level auto language detection from one 5-90 s sample; word boundary events available when using PhoenixLatestNeural base; mature Speech SDK for Windows/Python; $24/1M synthesis is reasonable. | Limited Access: API restricted to eligible customers and approved use cases via intake form; verbal consent statement required for every voice; Dragon base models (best similarity) do not emit word boundaries; $600 per 1,000 profiles/month storage fee; SSML-only invocation. | ~$24 per 1M characters + ~$600 per 1,000 voice profiles per month (third-party figures; Azure page shows placeholders without region). | Custom Voice REST API (consent + speaker profile) and Speech SDK/REST synthesis via SSML <mstts:ttsembedding speakerProfileId>; WordBoundary event in SDK (Phoenix only). |

API cloning was enterprise-gated anyway; no Indonesian. | Tiers $0-$150/mo with overage $50-150 per 1M chars (moot after Nov 2026). | REST /v0/tts (deprecated). |
| Resemble AI (hosted) / Chatterbox | API returns character-level timestamps (graph_chars/graph_times) from which word timings can be derived; rapid clone from 10 s; watermarking; Chatterbox Multilingual (MIT, 23 languages incl. Hindi/Arabic) is the open-source sibling. | Public TTS and clone pricing removed 14 Jul 2026 and generative products moved to 'Voice AI Research'; changelog and docs still show live TTS/cloning, so status is contradictory — treat as high vendor risk; 2,000-char request limit; hosted language list not published in docs excerpt. | Previously $0.0005/s (~$1.80 per audio hour) + $2/$5 per clone/month; now unpublished — contact sales. | POST https://f.cluster.resemble.ai/synthesize (sync), WebSocket streaming, clone API; response audio_timestamps. |

| Local: Chatterbox Multilingual (Resemble, MIT) | Commercially free (MIT), zero-shot clone from ~10 s, 23 languages incl. English, Hindi, Spanish, French, Arabic, Portuguese, German, Japanese, Korean, Russian; emotion/intensity controls; built-in watermark; pip installable. | No Indonesian; needs an NVIDIA GPU for practical speed (examples assume CUDA); no timestamps — requires aligner; quality below top hosted models for some languages; large model downloads per user laptop. | $0 (compute on user's PC). | pip chatterbox-tts; ChatterboxMultilingualTTS.generate(text, language_id, audio_prompt_path). |

| Local: GPT-SoVITS (MIT) | MIT; Windows one-click integrated package (go-webui.bat) with bundled Python/CUDA; 5 s zero-shot or 1-minute fine-tune for high similarity; V5 improved vocoder, 48 kHz output. | Only Chinese, English, Japanese, Korean, Cantonese — misses 6 of the 10 target languages; WebUI-centric, API server is secondary; no timestamps. | $0. | api_v2.py HTTP server inside the repo; weights per voice. |
| Local: IndexTTS-2.5 (bilibili license) | Strong emotion/duration control; Arabic and Spanish added Aug 2026; ~6 GB VRAM reported by third parties. | Custom 'bilibili Model Use License Agreement' — commercial use requires contacting bilibili; only ZH/EN/JA/ES/AR; DeepSpeed install pain on Windows. | $0 for non-commercial; commercial terms by agreement. | GitHub index-tts/index-tts Python package. |

Hindi, Arabic, Portuguese, Spanish, Japanese, Korean; F5-TTS: high quality EN/ZH with community weights for more languages. | XTTS-v2 weights are CPML non-commercial with no licensor left to sell a license; F5-TTS weights are CC-BY-NC-4.0 (OpenF5 is Apache but English-focused). Both unsuitable for monetized YouTube output. | $0 but not legally usable commercially. | coqui-tts (idiap fork) / f5-tts packages. |
| Local: Kokoro-82M (Apache-2.0) | Tiny, CPU-friendly, Apache-2.0, 8 languages incl. Hindi, Spanish, French, Portuguese, Japanese; good for stock-voice fallback or previews. | No voice cloning at all; no Arabic/Indonesian/German/Korean; no timestamps. | $0. | pip kokoro; KPipeline(lang_code). |
| Aligner: WhisperX (wav2vec2 CTC forced alignment) | pip install; align models for en, fr, de, es, it (torchaudio) and HF models for hi, ar, id, pt, ja, ko, ru, vi, tr and ~30 more — all ten targets covered; can align a supplied script (whisperx.align with your own segments) instead of transcribing; typical word error well under 100 ms; batched, fast on GPU. | GPU path needs CUDA 12.8 toolkit on Windows; CPU int8 works but is slow for large models (use small/medium for ASR, or skip ASR entirely and only run align); numerals/currency tokens get no timing (verbalize numbers first); HF align model quality varies by language; dependency churn (pyannote/torch). | $0. | whisperx.load_align_model(language_code), whisperx.align(segments, model, metadata, audio, device). |
| Aligner: Qwen3-ForcedAligner-0.6B (Apache-2.0) | Purpose-built forced aligner with LLM-grade robustness; word-level timestamps for arbitrary units up to 5 minutes; Apache-2.0; 11 languages incl. EN, ES, FR, PT, DE, JA, KO, RU; runs via transformers. | No Hindi, Arabic or Indonesian; 0.9B BF16 model needs a GPU for speed; released Jan/Feb 2026 so less battle-tested; 5-minute cap means chunking a 10-minute voiceover. | $0. | transformers AutoProcessor / AutoModelForTokenClassification 'Qwen/Qwen3-ForcedAligner-0.6B-hf' (or qwen-asr package). |
| Aligner: Montreal Forced Aligner 3.x | Research-grade accuracy (mean boundary error <15 ms per Jun 2026 paper); pretrained acoustic models for EN, ES, PT, RU, DE, FR, JA, KO, TR, VI; CPU-only, no GPU needed; Windows supported via conda. | Requires conda environment and Kaldi binaries — hard to ship inside a pip-based desktop app; needs pronunciation dictionaries/G2P per language; no pretrained Hindi, Indonesian or Arabic models; slower setup and OOV handling. | $0 (MIT code, CC BY 4.0 models). | CLI mfa align corpus_dir dictionary acoustic_model out_dir (TextGrid output). |
| Aligner: aeneas-ng (DTW over eSpeak synthesis) | CPU-only, very fast, AGPL-3 maintained fork (1.7.4.0, Dec 2025) with Windows wheels; good at sentence/fragment-level sync; 38 languages incl. EN, ES, FR, DE, PT, AR, RU, JA, TR. | Word-level precision is poor compared with CTC aligners; no Hindi, Indonesian, Korean, Vietnamese; needs eSpeak-ng and FFmpeg on PATH; AGPL license obligations if distributed. | $0. | python -m aeneas.tools.execute_task audio.wav text.txt 'task_language=eng/is_text_type=plain/os_task_file_format=json' out.json. |
| Aligner: stable-ts align() | Simple API to align known text with faster-whisper/openai-whisper backends; CPU OK; Whisper's ~99 languages. | Repository archived — development indefinitely paused 30 May 2026; accuracy inherits Whisper cross-attention approximations (~±50 ms claims); risk of breakage with new torch/whisper versions. | $0. | stable_whisper.load_faster_whisper(...).align(audio, text, language=...). |
| Aligner: faster-whisper word_timestamps (no forced alignment) | Already needed for any ASR tasks; CPU int8 works on laptops; Windows DLL bundles available (Purfview). | Cannot be forced to the known script — it re-transcribes, so mismatches with the script must be fuzzy-matched; word timing is approximate (cross-attention DTW). Only acceptable as last-resort fallback. | $0. | WhisperModel.transcribe(audio, word_timestamps=True, language=...). |
| Aligner: torchaudio MMS_FA (Meta MMS CTC aligner) | 1,100+ languages incl. Hindi, Arabic, Indonesian; ships inside torchaudio (pip, CPU OK); word-level with confidence. | Non-Latin text must be romanized with uroman; MMS weights reported as CC-BY-NC-4.0 so commercial use is doubtful; torchaudio is deprecating parts of its pipelines API. | $0 (license risk). | from torchaudio.pipelines import MMS_FA; bundle.get_model(), get_tokenizer(), get_aligner(). |

### Facts

- (high, 2026-06-03) Play.ht/PlayAI timeline: Meta acquisition confirmed 12 Jul 2025; API stopped accepting requests 26 Jul 2025; complete shutdown and deletion of accounts, clones, audio and API keys on 31 Dec 2025. - https://texttolab.com/blog/play-ht-shutdown-alternatives
- (high, 2026-10-09) Hume docs state: 'Hume is sunsetting the TTS and EVI APIs. Access ends November 13, 2026 at 12:01 a.m. EST'; account data is permanently deleted afterwards. Octave 2 (preview) supports English, Japanese, Korean, Spanish, French, Portuguese, Italian, German, Russian, Hindi, Arabic; feature table lists 'Timestamps (phoneme/word)'. - https://dev.hume.ai/docs/text-to-speech-tts/overview
- (high, 2026-10-09) MiniMax T2A HTTP API supports speech-2.8-hd, speech-2.8-turbo, speech-2.6-hd, speech-2.6-turbo (and older 02/01); subtitle_enable with subtitle_type 'sentence' | 'word' | 'word_streaming'; text < 10,000 chars per request; language_boost list of ~40 languages includes English, Hindi, Spanish, French, Arabic, Portuguese, Indonesian, German, Japanese, Korean, Russian, Vietnamese; 'auto' enables detection. - https://platform.minimax.io/docs/api-reference/speech-t2a-http
- (high, 2026-10-09) MiniMax pay-as-you-go: speech-2.8-hd / 2.6-hd $100 per 1M characters, speech-2.8-turbo / 2.6-turbo $60 per 1M characters; Rapid Voice Cloning $1.50 per voice (charged on first synthesis use); Voice Design $3 per voice; billing unit is characters. - https://platform.minimax.io/docs/guides/pricing-paygo
- (high, 2026-10-09) MiniMax voice clone flow: upload source audio (mp3/m4a/wav, 10 s to 5 min, <=20 MB) via POST /v1/files/upload (purpose voice_clone), then POST /v1/voice_clone with file_id and custom voice_id; optional prompt audio <8 s. - https://platform.minimax.io/docs/guides/speech-voice-clone.md
- (medium, 2026-10-09) Third-party integration docs (Vapi) confirm MiniMax word-level subtitles return a words array with startMs/endMs per word; subtitle metadata is attached to the final audio chunk of each synthesis segment in streaming mode. - https://docs.vapi.ai/providers/voice/minimax
- (high, 2026-10-09) Cartesia WebSocket TTS accepts add_timestamps (word-level) and add_phoneme_timestamps; word timestamps arrive as parallel arrays words[], start[], end[] in seconds; model_id supports sonic-3.6, sonic-3.5, sonic-3, sonic-latest; language codes include en, hi, es, fr, ar, pt, id, de, ja, ko, ru, vi and ~50 total. - https://docs.cartesia.ai/api-reference/tts/tts
- (high, 2026-10-09) Cartesia SSE endpoint also supports add_timestamps=True and emits events of type 'timestamps' containing word_timestamps. - https://docs.cartesia.ai/examples/tts-sse-with-timestamps
- (high, 2026-10-09) Cartesia pricing: Free $0 (20K credits, no commercial license, no cloning), Pro $5/mo (100K credits, instant voice cloning, commercial license), Startup $49/mo (1.25M credits, 2 Professional Voice Clone slots), Scale $299/mo (8M credits, 4 PVC slots); 1 credit = 1 character; ~750-800 credits per minute of audio. Implied cost ~$50/1M chars on Pro, ~$39/1M on Startup, ~$37/1M on Scale. - https://cartesia.ai/pricing
- (high, 2026-10-09) Cartesia Sonic 3.6 speaks 44 languages (English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese, Chinese, Japanese, Hindi and others). - https://docs.cartesia.ai/build-with-cartesia/models/tts
- (high, 2026-10-09) Fish Audio POST /v1/tts models: s1, s2-pro, s2.1-pro (default), s2.1-pro-free, drama-3-preview; voice via reference_id or inline references (zero-shot); formats wav/pcm/mp3/opus; response is chunked audio only — no timestamps, word boundaries or alignment data are documented. - https://docs.fish.audio/api-reference/endpoint/openapi-v1/text-to-speech
- (high, 2026-10-09) Fish Audio API rate card: s2.1-pro $15.00 / M UTF-8 bytes, s2.1-pro-free $0.00, s2-pro $15.00, s1 $15.00 (deprecated, retired 31 Dec 2026); Voice Design $0.01 per request; ASR $0.36/audio hour; concurrency 5 (<$100 paid), 15 (>=$100), 50 (>=$1,000). 1M UTF-8 bytes ~= 180,000 English words. - https://docs.fish.audio/developer-guide/models-pricing/pricing-and-rate-limits
- (high, 2026-06-23) Fish Audio S2.1 Pro supports 83 languages (English, Japanese, Chinese, Korean, Spanish, Arabic, French, German, Portuguese, Russian 'and dozens more'); s2.1-pro-free has no hard character cap under Fair Use, no latency/uptime guarantee, request data may be used to improve models, free access extended through 30 Nov 2026, products >$1M ARR should contact Fish first. - https://fish.audio/blog/s2-1-pro-free-api/
- (medium, 2026-08-18) Fish Audio subscription tiers (third-party summary): Free 8,000 credits no commercial rights; Plus ~$11/mo 250K credits commercial rights, 10 private voice slots; Pro ~$75/mo 2M credits; Max ~$749/mo 25M credits; Fish requires proof of rights for commercial use of cloned voices. Hindi/Indonesian not explicitly listed in Fish's own language quote — verify. - https://texttolab.com/blog/fish-audio-pricing
- (medium, 2026-09-28) Fish Audio bills per UTF-8 byte, so Hindi, Arabic and Japanese scripts (2-3 bytes per character) cost roughly 2-3x the same word count in Latin scripts. - https://smallest.ai/blog/fish-audio-pricing-plans-api-billing-commercial-use-in-2026
- (high, 2026-10-09) Resemble synthesize API (POST https://f.cluster.resemble.ai/synthesize): max 2,000 characters per request; response includes audio_content (base64) and audio_timestamps with graph_chars/graph_times (character-level [start,end] seconds) and phon_chars/phon_times; word timings must be derived by grouping characters. - https://docs.resemble.ai/voice-generation/text-to-speech/synchronous.md
- (medium, 2026-07-14) Resemble packaging change 14 Jul 2026: per-second TTS ($0.0005/s), voice agent and STT rates removed from public pricing; $2 rapid / $5 pro clone add-ons removed; generative products moved to 'Voice AI Research' without commercial pricing; enterprise threshold raised to $2,000/mo; emphasis shifted to detection/watermarking. - https://usagepricing.com/blueprint/activity/resemble-ai-2026-07-14-packaging
- (medium, 2026-06-29) Resemble public changelog still shows active generative-voice work in 2026 (8 May 2026 'first clone free, each additional clone $2'; 29 Jun 2026 API-created voices default to rapid clone), and docs still list TTS and Voice Cloning — so commercial availability is contradictory and must be confirmed with Resemble. - https://www.resemble.ai/changelog
- (high, 2026-10-09) Resemble's current public pricing page lists only Flex ($0 pay-as-you-go), Team ($350/mo), Business ($1,000/mo), Enterprise, with per-unit rates for detection/watermarking; no TTS or clone rates shown. - https://www.resemble.ai/pricing/
- (high, 2026-10-09) Google Chirp 3 Instant Custom Voice: 'Access to Instant Custom Voice is restricted to allow-listed users' (contact sales); mandatory fixed consent script per language; ~10-second reference and consent recordings; 33+ languages incl. English, Spanish, French, German, Chinese, Japanese, Korean, Hindi, Bengali, Tamil, Telugu etc.; no SSML, timepoints or word timestamps documented. - https://docs.cloud.google.com/text-to-speech/docs/chirp3-instant-custom-voice
- (high, 2026-10-09) Google Chirp 3: HD supports 50+ locales and a limited SSML subset for synchronous requests only (no <mark>), plus [pause] markup; docs do not mention timepoints/enableTimePointing. - https://docs.cloud.google.com/text-to-speech/docs/chirp3-hd
- (medium, 2026-06-10) Google Cloud TTS pricing (third-party table, verified Oct 2026): Standard $4/1M (4M free), WaveNet $4/1M, Neural2 $16/1M, Chirp 3: HD $30/1M (1M free/month), Studio $160/1M, Instant Custom Voice $60/1M with no free tier. - https://texttolab.com/blog/google-cloud-tts-pricing
- (high, 2026-09-09) Azure personal voice: 5-90 s sample plus recorded verbal consent statement; speaks 91 languages / 100+ locales with sentence-level auto language detection; API access 'restricted to eligible customers and approved use cases' via intake form; restricted to approved use cases under the transparency note. - https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/speech-service/personal-voice-overview
- (high, 2026-09-09) Azure personal voice SDK features: Word boundary events supported with Phoenix base model (PhoenixLatestNeural) — NOT with Dragon (DragonLatestNeural / DragonHDOmniLatestNeural); base models listed: DragonLatestNeural, DragonHDOmniLatestNeural, MAI-Voice-2, PhoenixLatestNeural; <bookmark> supported only in Phoenix. - https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/speech-service/personal-voice-how-to-use
- (medium, 2026-09-01) Azure pricing page structure: Personal Voice (Limited Access) billed per 1M characters synthesis, voice creation free, plus per-1,000-voice-profiles-per-month storage; F0 free tier 0.5M neural characters/month. Third-party sources put it at $24 per 1M characters + $600 per 1,000 profiles/month; standard neural $16/1M, Neural HD $22/1M (Sep 2026). - https://texttolab.com/blog/azure-text-to-speech-pricing
- (high, 2026-10-09) Murf /v1/speech/generate response includes wordDurations (per-word start/end milliseconds) alongside audioFile and audioLengthInSeconds; parameters include voiceId, style, locale, modelVersion GEN2, pronunciationDictionary, formats MP3/WAV/FLAC/ALAW/ULAW/PCM/OGG. - https://murf.ai/api/docs/api-reference/text-to-speech/generate
- (medium, 2026-08-01) Murf API pricing (third-party): Falcon $0.01 per 1,000 chars ($10/1M), Gen2 $0.03 per 1,000 chars ($30/1M); free trial 100,000 chars; Falcon 2 also quoted at $0.01/min; 35+ languages; voice cloning restricted to Enterprise with no published price. - https://www.costbench.com/software/voice-apis/murf-api/
- (high, 2026-10-09) Speechify models: Simba 3.2 = English only; Simba 3.0 = EN, DE-DE, ES-ES, ES-MX, FR-FR, IT-IT, PT-BR; both support zero-shot cloning on paid plans and speech marks; legacy Simba Multilingual (30+ languages) retired from API version 2026-09-21 and served by current models from 2026-11-21. - https://docs.speechify.ai/build/guides/concepts/models
- (high, 2026-07-23) Speechify POST /v1/audio/stream/with-timestamps streams word-level speech marks ({type:'word', start, end, start_time, end_time (ms), value}) with simba-3.0/3.2; POST /v1/audio/speech returns non-streamed marks on any model. - https://docs.speechify.ai/build/api-reference/v1/audio/stream/with-timestamps
- (medium, 2026-09-15) Speechify API pricing: $10 per 1M chars on $10/mo Starter, $8 on $99/mo Pro, $6 on $499/mo Scale, 500K chars/month free; cloning self-serve on every paid plan. - https://speechify.ai/blog/best-tts-providers-2026-comparison
- (high, 2026-10-09) Inworld TTS timestamps: timestampType WORD or CHARACTER; wordAlignment with words[], wordStartTimeSeconds[], wordEndTimeSeconds[] plus phoneticDetails; available on inworld-tts-2 and inworld-tts-2-flash, streaming and non-streaming, 'in every language the model supports'. - https://docs.inworld.ai/tts/capabilities/timestamps
- (high, 2026-10-09) Inworld pricing: Realtime TTS-2 $25/1M chars On-Demand ($20 Creator, $15 Developer, $12.50 Growth, enterprise to $5); TTS-2 Flash $15/1M On-Demand; On-Demand includes up to 70 min TTS free, instant voice cloning, 100 custom voices and a commercial license; Batch API 20% cheaper. - https://inworld.ai/pricing
- (low, 2026-10-05) Inworld TTS language list (third-party): English, Spanish, French, German, Italian, Portuguese, Dutch, Polish, Russian, Chinese, Japanese, Korean, Hindi, Arabic, Hebrew (15) — Indonesian not listed. - https://www.eesel.ai/blog/inworld-ai-pricing
- (high, 2026-10-09) Chatterbox (Resemble) is MIT licensed; Chatterbox Multilingual covers 23 languages: Arabic, Danish, German, Greek, English, Spanish, Finnish, French, Hebrew, Hindi, Italian, Japanese, Korean, Malay, Dutch, Norwegian, Polish, Portuguese, Russian, Swedish, Swahili, Turkish, Chinese (no Indonesian); all output carries Perth neural watermark; examples assume CUDA. - https://huggingface.co/ResembleAI/chatterbox
- (medium, 2025-09-05) Chatterbox Multilingual zero-shot clones from ~10 s of reference audio; announced Sep 2025 with emotion/intensity control. - https://www.resemble.ai/introducing-chatterbox-multilingual-open-source-tts-for-23-languages/
- (high, 2026-01-22) Qwen3-TTS released 22 Jan 2026 under Apache-2.0; variants 0.6B/1.7B (Base, CustomVoice, VoiceDesign); languages Chinese, English, Japanese, Korean, German, French, Russian, Portuguese, Spanish, Italian; 3-second rapid voice clone; streaming latency ~97 ms. - https://github.com/QwenLM/Qwen3-TTS
- (high, 2026-08-10) IndexTTS-2.5 released 10 Aug 2026; languages Chinese, English, Japanese, Spanish, Arabic; 'bilibili Model Use License Agreement' — commercial usage requires contacting indexspeech@bilibili.com; Windows: DeepSpeed hard to install, CUDA 12.8+ recommended. - https://github.com/index-tts/index-tts
- (medium, 2026-10-09) F5-TTS released weights are CC-BY-NC-4.0 (trained on Emilia), so commercial use is not permitted; OpenF5-TTS-Base is an Apache-2.0 retrain on permissive data. - https://huggingface.co/mrfakename/OpenF5-TTS-Base
- (medium, 2026-01-01) XTTS-v2 weights are under the Coqui Public Model License (non-commercial); Coqui shut down Jan 2024 so no commercial license can be purchased; Idiap fork (idiap/coqui-ai-TTS) released v0.27.5 in Jan 2026; 17 languages, ~6 s reference. - https://localaimaster.com/blog/xtts-coqui-commercial-license
- (high, 2026-10-09) GPT-SoVITS is MIT licensed; languages English, Japanese, Korean, Cantonese, Mandarin; latest V5 (also V2Pro, V4 with native 48 kHz); zero-shot from 5 s, few-shot fine-tune from 1 min; Windows integrated package launched via go-webui.bat; tested Python 3.9-3.12, PyTorch 2.2-2.8. - https://github.com/RVC-Boss/GPT-SoVITS
- (high, 2026-01-01) Kokoro-82M is Apache-2.0, v1.0 released 27 Jan 2025 with 8 languages (en-US/GB, es, fr, hi, it, ja, pt-BR, zh) and 54 fixed voices; it does NOT support voice cloning. - https://texttolab.com/blog/kokoro-tts-review
- (low, 2026-10-09) CosyVoice 3 (Alibaba FunAudioLLM) is Apache-2.0, zero-shot cloning from ~3 s, 9 languages (English, Chinese, Japanese, Korean, German, Spanish, French, Italian, Russian) plus 18 Chinese dialects. - https://tts.ai/voices/cosyvoice3/
- (high, 2026-10-09) WhisperX default alignment models: torchaudio for en, fr, de, es, it; Hugging Face wav2vec2 for ja, zh, nl, uk, pt, ar, cs, ru, pl, hu, fi, fa, el, tr, da, he, vi, ko, ur, te, hi, ca, ml, no, nn, sk, sl, hr, ro, eu, gl, ka, lv, tl, sv, id — covering every top-10 YouTube language. - https://raw.githubusercontent.com/m-bain/whisperX/main/whisperx/alignment.py
- (high, 2026-10-09) WhisperX install: pip install whisperx; GPU needs CUDA toolkit 12.8 (Windows users told to install it first); CPU mode via --compute_type int8 --device cpu; words with non-dictionary characters (e.g. '2014.', '£13.60') get no timings; Whisper large-v3 fp16 ~3 GB VRAM plus ~0.5 GB for the align model. - https://github.com/m-bain/whisperX
- (high, 2026-05-30) stable-ts provides align() and align_words() to align a known transcript at word level using openai-whisper, faster-whisper, HF transformers or MLX backends, runs on CPU or GPU, Windows ffmpeg via Chocolatey/Scoop — but the repository states 'Development on this repository is indefinitely paused' (archived 30 May 2026). - https://github.com/jianfch/stable-ts
- (high, 2026-10-09) faster-whisper exposes word_timestamps=True (Whisper's cross-attention based timing, approximate), supports CPU int8 and CUDA (cuBLAS + cuDNN 9 for CUDA 12; Purfview bundles Windows libraries); it offers no forced alignment of a supplied transcript. - https://github.com/SYSTRAN/faster-whisper
- (high, 2025-12-02) aeneas-ng 1.7.4.0 released 2 Dec 2025 (AGPLv3), Windows x86-64 wheel for Python 3.11, requires FFmpeg and eSpeak/eSpeak-ng; aeneas is confirmed on 38 languages (incl. ENG, SPA, FRA, DEU, POR, ARA, RUS, JPN, TUR) but not Hindi, Indonesian, Korean or Vietnamese. - https://pypi.org/project/aeneas-ng/
- (high, 2026-06-01) Montreal Forced Aligner 3.x installs via conda-forge (conda create -n aligner -c conda-forge montreal-forced-aligner), including Windows via Miniconda; MFA 3.0 reports mean boundary errors below 15 ms across benchmark languages (arXiv 2606.18466, Jun 2026). - https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18466
- (high, 2026-10-09) MFA pretrained acoustic models exist for English (US/UK/Nigeria/India), Spanish, Portuguese, Russian, German, French, Japanese, Korean, Turkish, Vietnamese (CC BY 4.0); none listed for Hindi, Indonesian or Arabic. - https://mfa-models.readthedocs.io/en/latest/acoustic/index.html
- (medium, 2026-10-09) torchaudio.pipelines.MMS_FA is a CTC forced-alignment bundle trained on 23,000 h across 1,100+ languages; non-Latin scripts must be romanized (uroman) first; runs on CPU; produces per-token timestamps with confidence. Third-party notes say the MMS weights inherit CC-BY-NC-4.0 (non-commercial). - https://docs.pytorch.org/audio/stable/tutorials/forced_alignment_for_multilingual_data_tutorial.html
- (high, 2026-02-02) Qwen3-ForcedAligner-0.6B-hf: Apache-2.0, word-level timestamps for arbitrary units within up to 5 minutes of audio in 11 languages (Chinese, English, Cantonese, French, German, Italian, Japanese, Korean, Portuguese, Russian, Spanish); BF16 0.9B params; loads via transformers AutoModelForTokenClassification; paper 2 Feb 2026. - https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-ForcedAligner-0.6B-hf
- (medium, 2026-04-01) AIR Media-Tech ranks YouTube languages by audience size (Apr 2026): English ~1.45B, Hindi ~610M, Spanish 600M+, French 310M+, Arabic 380M, Portuguese 260M, Indonesian 300M, German ~90M, Japanese ~125M, Korean 81M, then Italian, Vietnamese; another ranking by channel count lists Hindi, Spanish, Russian, Vietnamese, German, French, Portuguese, Indonesian, Polish, Filipino after English. - https://air.io/en/audience-growth/what-are-the-most-popular-languages-on-youtube
- (medium, 2026-10-09) MiniMax audio terms require users to hold full legal rights or authorization for any individual's voice in input content and prohibit impersonation and misleading use. - https://platform.minimax.io/docs/api-reference/voice-cloning-intro

### Open questions

- Which voice-cloning tool does the user already pay for? The adapter order and the need for an aligner depend entirely on whether it returns timestamps (MiniMax/Cartesia/Inworld do; Fish does not).
- Do the target users' Windows laptops have NVIDIA GPUs? WhisperX/Qwen3-ForcedAligner/Chatterbox on CPU may take several minutes per 10-minute video; if not, the design should default to MiniMax or Cartesia (native timestamps) and reserve local models for GPU machines.
- MiniMax: confirm with a live test that subtitle_type='word' in non-streaming T2A returns per-word startMs/endMs in subtitle_file (docs mention sentence-level for the file) and whether word subtitles work for Hindi, Arabic and Indonesian.
- Fish Audio: confirm Hindi and Indonesian are in the 83 supported languages and that cloned voices sound acceptable in them; confirm whether s2.1-pro-free output may be used commercially given the Fair Use / personal-use wording.
- Resemble: is hosted TTS/voice cloning still commercially sold after the 14 Jul 2026 packaging change (public rates removed), or only via enterprise contract?
- Cartesia: exact wording of the cloned-voice consent and commercial-license terms for Instant Voice Cloning on the Pro tier, and the maximum transcript length per WebSocket message.
- Inworld: authoritative language list for inworld-tts-2 (docs are login-gated) — specifically Indonesian and Vietnamese.
- Final definition of the 'top 10 YouTube languages' for the product: audience-size ranking (adds Arabic, Indonesian, Korean) vs channel-count ranking (adds Russian, Vietnamese, Polish, Filipino) changes which providers/aligners qualify.
- Consent/ownership policy: will users clone only their own voice, or client voices? Google and Azure require a scripted consent recording; others only require that the user hold rights — the ConsentRecord design should match the strictest provider you intend to support.
- Licensing of the shipped aligner: AGPL (aeneas-ng) and CC-BY-NC (MMS_FA) may be incompatible with a distributed commercial app; confirm WhisperX (BSD-2) + its HF align models' licenses per language before bundling.
- Azure Personal Voice and Google Instant Custom Voice allow-list approval: is the user willing to apply as a business customer, or should these be dropped from the roadmap entirely?

### Fact-check

Confirmed claims: 48

- REFUTED: Claim 33 / Summary / Recommendation: Inworld TTS-2 covers only ~15 locales (English, Spanish, French, German, Italian, Portuguese, Dutch, Polish, Russian, Chinese, Japanese, Korean, Hindi, Arabic, Hebrew) and is 'missing Indonesian', so it is relegated to a conditional fourth adapter. - The cited eesel blog does not contain a language list; the vendor's documentation directly contradicts the 15-locale figure and the 'no Indonesian' conclusion. This changes the vendor ranking: Inworld is the cheapest provider that simultaneously offers clones, native word timestamps and all ten target languages. Correction: Inworld's own multilingual docs (fetched 2026-10-09) state the Realtime TTS-2 models (inworld-tts-2, inworld-tts-2-flash) accept '623 language and locale codes (BCP-47) ... covering 470 languages', organised as Tier 1 (15 languages, highest quality) / Tier 2 (79) / Tier 3 (376). Hindi (hi-IN) and Arabic (ar-SA) are Tier 1; Indonesian (id, id-ID) is Tier 2. Combined with native word/character timestamps 'in every language the model supports', instant cloning from 3-30 s of audio available 'to all users through Portal or via API', a commercial license on the $0 On-Demand tier, $15/1M (Flash) or $25/1M (TTS-2) and a Batch API 'at 20% less per character than realtime synthesis', Inworld should be on the primary shortlist (arguably first), not conditional. The 15-language figure came from a third-party pricing blog (eesel, 2026-10-05) that in fact lists no TTS languages at all. https://docs.inworld.ai/tts/capabilities/multilingual
- REFUTED: Claim 25: Azure Personal Voice costs '$24 per 1M characters + $600 per 1,000 profiles/month' (standard neural $16/1M, Neural HD $22/1M), attributed to texttolab.com/blog/azure-text-to-speech-pricing. - The claim's own source does not contain the numbers attributed to it, and no primary source confirms them. The Azure per-video cost line in the recommendation ($0.22 + $0.60/profile/month) therefore has no verified basis. Correction: The cited texttolab article (dated 2026-09-01, 'last verified October 2026') confirms $16/1M prebuilt neural and $22/1M Neural HD and the 0.5M-char free tier, but it does NOT give any Personal Voice figures; it says only 'Pricing varies and requires application for access'. The official Azure pricing page confirms the billing structure (Personal Voice 'Limited Access': synthesis per 1M characters, voice creation 'Free', storage 'per 1,000 voice profiles per month') but the dollar values are rendered client-side and could not be read. Treat $24/1M and $600/1,000 profiles as unverified; obtain them from the Azure pricing calculator/API for the chosen region before using them in the cost model. https://texttolab.com/blog/azure-text-to-speech-pricing
- MISSING: PROMOTE INWORLD: TTS-2 / TTS-2 Flash cover 470 languages (Hindi & Arabic Tier 1, Indonesian Tier 2), return word + character + phoneme timestamps in every language, clone from 3-30 s of audio on all plans ('Confirm you have the rights to clone the voice' checkbox, no scripted consent recording), commercial license on the $0 On-Demand tier, $15/1M (Flash) / $25/1M (TTS-2) on-demand, Batch API 20% cheaper, voice-clone endpoint rate-limited to 2 req/min (On-Demand) up to 20 req/min (Growth). Sources: https://docs.inworld.ai/tts/capabilities/multilingual , https://docs.inworld.ai/tts/instant-voice-cloning.md , https://docs.inworld.ai/portal/billing.md , https://inworld.ai/pricing (all fetched 2026-10-09).
- MISSING: MINIMAX CLONE EXPIRY: the Voice Clone API doc states 'If a cloned voice is not used within 7 days, the system will delete it.' (https://platform.minimax.io/docs/api-reference/voice-cloning-clone.md). The VoiceService must synthesize (or at least run a preview synthesis) right after cloning and keep a keep-alive/re-clone path; MiniMax subscription plans ($5 Starter: 10 voice slots, RPM 10; up to 800 slots on Business) exist as an alternative to pay-as-you-go (https://platform.minimax.io/docs/guides/pricing-speech.md). voice_id must be 8-256 chars starting with a letter; need_noise_reduction / need_volume_normalization flags exist.
- MISSING: MINIMAX TIMESTAMP DELIVERY DETAIL: in non-streaming HTTP mode subtitles come back as a `subtitle_file` download URL 'valid for 24 hours', described in the doc as 'aligned to sentences (<= 50 characters), timestamped in milliseconds, returned in JSON format'; word-level `subtitle_type='word'` is documented as a request option but the word-level payload is confirmed only for streaming (Vapi notes it is attached to the final audio chunk of each segment, times relative to segment start). The adapter must download and persist the subtitle JSON immediately and the team should empirically confirm word-level output in stream=false mode before relying on it. Sources: https://platform.minimax.io/docs/api-reference/speech-t2a-http , https://docs.vapi.ai/providers/voice/minimax
- MISSING: SPEECHIFY NOW REQUIRES VERIFIED CONSENT RECORDINGS: since 2026-09-23 the API only clones a voice after a two-step consent challenge in which the speaker reads an exact phrase ('I agree to have my voice cloned by Speechify. My verification code is [numbers]') that is transcribed and speaker-matched (https://docs.speechify.ai/build/guides/voice-cloning/consent.md). So Speechify belongs with Google/Azure in the 'needs_consent_recording=True' bucket, not with Fish/MiniMax/Cartesia. Also: /v1/audio/speech is capped at 2,000 chars, /v1/audio/stream and /stream/with-timestamps at 20,000; plan prices Free $0 / Starter $10 / Pro $99 / Scale $499 are confirmed on speechify.ai/pricing but the $10/$8/$6 per-1M rates and 500K free chars could not be verified from a primary page.
- MISSING: CARTESIA CLONE DETAILS: instant clone needs '10 seconds of audio ... up to 60 seconds'; 'Each clone is built from one language' and to speak other languages natively you must 'add languages with the Add Voice Accents API' (relevant for a 10-language product). Overage when monthly credits run out: '$65 per 1M credits' (Pro), '$45' (Startup), '$38' (Scale); unused credits roll over 'up to 2x your monthly rate'; PVC needs 30+ min audio and is Startup/Scale only. The API reference lists 79 language/locale codes (44 languages), not '~50'. Sources: https://docs.cartesia.ai/build-with-cartesia/capability-guides/clone-voices.md , https://cartesia.ai/pricing , https://docs.cartesia.ai/api-reference/tts/tts
- MISSING: WHISPERX HINDI/ARABIC ALIGNMENT QUALITY RISK: the default HF align models are 'theainerd/Wav2Vec2-large-xlsr-hindi' (no license stated on the model card, reported test WER 72.62%), 'jonatasgrosman/wav2vec2-large-xlsr-53-arabic' (Apache-2.0, WER 39.59%), 'cahya/wav2vec2-large-xlsr-indonesian' (Apache-2.0, WER 25.86%), 'kresnik/wav2vec2-large-xlsr-korean' (Apache-2.0, WER 4.74%). Expect weak Hindi word timing and check the unlicensed Hindi model before shipping; consider a different Hindi wav2vec2/CTC model via whisperx.load_align_model(model_name=...). Also `LANGUAGES_WITHOUT_SPACES = ['ja', 'zh']`: WhisperX aligns Japanese/Chinese per character, so the pipeline needs its own tokenizer (e.g. fugashi/MeCab) to regroup characters into caption words. Source: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/m-bain/whisperX/main/whisperx/alignment.py and the HF model cards (fetched 2026-10-09).
- MISSING: AENEAS-NG VS PYTHON 3.12: the only Windows wheel on PyPI is for CPython 3.11 (release 1.7.4.0, 2 Dec 2025, AGPL-3.0). The target machines run Python 3.12, so aeneas-ng needs a separate 3.11 runtime or a source build; AGPL also matters if the app is ever distributed. https://pypi.org/project/aeneas-ng/
- MISSING: CTC-FORCED-ALIGNER: the popular pip package (MahmoudAshraf97/ctc-forced-aligner, BSD-2) is a lighter MMS_FA wrapper but its default model 'MahmoudAshraf/mms-300m-1130-forced-aligner' is CC-BY-NC 4.0 — 'make sure to use a different model for commercial usage'. Confirms the fairseq MMS README: 'The MMS code and model weights are released under the CC-BY-NC 4.0 license.' https://github.com/MahmoudAshraf97/ctc-forced-aligner , https://github.com/facebookresearch/fairseq/blob/main/examples/mms/README.md
- MISSING: LMNT HAS SHUT DOWN: lmnt.com now shows only 'Our speech generation journey has come to an end.' (fetched 2026-10-09). LMNT was the other mainstream API with instant cloning plus per-word durations; do not consider it. https://www.lmnt.com/pricing
- MISSING: VOXCPM2 (missed local option): OpenBMB VoxCPM, Apache-2.0, 'free for commercial use'; VoxCPM2 released April 2026, 2B params, '30 languages' incl. Arabic, Chinese, English, French, German, Japanese, Korean, Russian, Spanish, Vietnamese, 48 kHz output, zero-shot 'Controllable Voice Cloning' and transcript-guided 'Ultimate Cloning'. Check Hindi/Indonesian/Portuguese coverage and laptop VRAM; smaller VoxCPM1.5 (0.6B, Dec 2025) exists. https://github.com/OpenBMB/VoxCPM
- MISSING: CHATTERBOX FAMILY UPDATE: GitHub now lists Chatterbox-Multilingual V3 (500M, '23+' languages: same list as HF, still no Indonesian/Vietnamese), Chatterbox-Turbo (350M, English only, MIT) and Chatterbox-Nano (110M, English, 'Runs on CPU (3x realtime on 8 cores)') plus single-language finetunes incl. Hindi, Brazilian Portuguese, Spain/LatAm Spanish. Resemble's launch blog lists Vietnamese and Czech instead of Danish/Greek/Malay/Swahili — the two lists disagree, so verify the shipped language set at install time. https://github.com/resemble-ai/chatterbox , https://www.resemble.ai/introducing-chatterbox-multilingual-open-source-tts-for-23-languages/
- MISSING: OTHER OPEN MODELS WORTH A LINE: Orpheus multilingual research release includes a Hindi 3B fine-tune (canopylabs/3b-hi-ft-research_release, Apache-2.0, zero-shot cloning) but 3B Llama is heavy for laptops; Higgs Audio v2 is Apache-2.0 but needs a 24 GB GPU and v3 is under a non-commercial research license; NeuTTS Air (Neuphonic, Apache-2.0, ~0.5B, 3 s clone, runs on CPU, Perth watermark, English only with es/de/fr variants) is a CPU fallback for English; Zonos (Apache-2.0, 5 languages) is Linux-first with only an experimental Windows fork; Kyutai TTS is CC-BY-4.0 but en/fr only and cloning is 'restrict[ed] ... to the use of pre-computed voice embeddings'; Microsoft VibeVoice (MIT) has no cloning and Microsoft 'removed the VibeVoice-TTS code'; Fish's open OpenAudio S1-mini is CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0 (not commercial). Sources: huggingface.co/canopylabs/3b-hi-ft-research_release, github.com/boson-ai/higgs-audio, github.com/neuphonic/neutts-air, github.com/Zyphra/Zonos, huggingface.co/kyutai/tts-1.6b-en_fr, github.com/microsoft/VibeVoice, huggingface.co/fishaudio/openaudio-s1-mini
- MISSING: FISH AUDIO GAPS: the official models overview still only says s2.1-pro 'supports 83 languages' with no enumeration (S1 listed 13; none include Hindi/Indonesian), so Hindi/Indonesian/Vietnamese support must be tested, not assumed. Custom voices are created with POST https://api.fish.audio/model from .wav/.mp3/.m4a/.opus clips ('at least 10 seconds per clip; a minute or two ... improves fidelity'), optional transcripts (`texts`), default `enhance_audio_quality`. The free s2.1-pro-free model may use request data to improve models — a privacy concern when the request contains a user's cloned voice. The fish.audio/pricing page returned 404, so the Plus/Pro/Max tier figures rest on the texttolab article only. https://docs.fish.audio/developer-guide/models-pricing/models-overview.md , https://docs.fish.audio/developer-guide/core-features/creating-models.md
- MISSING: SMALLEST.AI LIGHTNING (not considered): Lightning v3.1 Pro lists '31 languages' and cloning; pricing page quotes '~$0.09/minute'; timestamp support not documented in what was fetched — low priority but a legitimate clone-capable hosted option. https://docs.smallest.ai/models/documentation/getting-started/models , https://smallest.ai/pricing
- MISSING: MURF confirmed as Enterprise-only for cloning: 'Instant Voice Cloning is available on the Enterprise plan only', up to 30 s sample, clones 'are multilingual out of the box', no creation fee; Falcon 2 'costs 1 cent per 1000 characters' and the Falcon 2 page does not say whether it returns wordDurations (the /generate reference does). https://murf.ai/api/docs/voices-styles/instant-voice-cloning.md , https://murf.ai/api/docs/text-to-speech-models/falcon-2.md
- MISSING: GOOGLE/AZURE PRICE VERIFICATION: cloud.google.com/text-to-speech/pricing could not be parsed (truncated) and Azure's page renders prices client-side; both pricing claims (22, 25) currently rest on texttolab. Google ICV docs now list 38 languages (not 33+) and Chirp 3: HD 54 locales with SSML only for non-streaming requests and no <mark>/timepoints. https://docs.cloud.google.com/text-to-speech/docs/chirp3-instant-custom-voice , https://docs.cloud.google.com/text-to-speech/docs/chirp3-hd
- MISSING: SPEECHIFY SPEECH MARKS NUANCE: 'All' models return speech marks; if streaming infrastructure cannot produce them the request fails with 400 speech_marks_unsupported, while POST /v1/audio/speech 'returns speech marks for every model' — the adapter should fall back to the batch endpoint. https://docs.speechify.ai/build/guides/text-to-speech/speech-marks.md
- MISSING: AIR MEDIA-TECH CREATOR TABLE: the article's creator-count table (English 12.2M, Spanish 3.1M, Arabic 1.9M, Portuguese 1.8M, Japanese 1M, Indonesian 948K, French 735K, German 419K, Korean 305K, Hindi 246K) does not match the 'Hindi, Spanish, Russian, Vietnamese...' channel-count ordering quoted in claim 52; audience numbers are confirmed (Arabic 380M ranks above French 310M and Indonesian 300M). https://air.io/en/audience-growth/what-are-the-most-popular-languages-on-youtube (updated 01 Apr 2026)

Notes: Method: WebSearch budget was already exhausted (200/200) when this task started, so all checking was done by WebFetch of the cited URLs plus vendor docs/llms.txt indexes on 2026-10-09. Confirmed (48 of 53): claims 1-24, 26-29, 31, 32, 34-51, 53 partially (see below) — core facts matched the cited or a primary source. Minor inaccuracies not counted as refutations: Cartesia API lists 79 language/locale codes not ~50 (claim 7); Google ICV lists 38 languages not 33+ and Chirp 3 HD 54 locales not 50+ (claims 20-21); Resemble rapid-clone default entry is dated 30 Jun not 29 Jun 2026 (claim 18); Qwen3-ForcedAligner paper date is 29 Jan 2026 not 2 Feb (claim 51); WhisperX README gives '<8GB gpu memory for large-v2' rather than the '~3 GB + 0.5 GB' figure (claim 44); MiniMax speech-2.6/02 models are listed under 'Legacy Models' at the same $60/$100 prices (claim 4); Fish 'drama' model has no rate on the rate card (claim 12). Could not verify: claim 53 (MiniMax voice-cloning-intro URL returns 404; the clone guide and clone API pages contain no rights/consent text, so the terms statement is unsourced here); claim 30's per-1M rates ($10/$8/$6) and 500K free characters (plan prices and paid-plan self-serve cloning are confirmed, the cited speechify.ai blog did not render); claim 14 and 22 primary pages (fish.audio/pricing 404; Google pricing page truncated) — the cited third-party pages do say what the researcher reports; claim 39's Idiap fork version (license = Coqui Public Model License confirmed on HF). Play.ht: play.ht no longer resolves (ENOTFOUND), consistent with the shutdown claim, but only the texttolab secondary source was readable. Architecture impact of findings: (1) re-rank Inworld to the top tier (470 languages, word timestamps, cheap, commercial license on free tier, rights checkbox only); (2) move Speechify into the scripted-consent bucket with Google/Azure (mandatory verified consent since 2026-09-23); (3) add MiniMax 7-day unused-clone deletion and 24-hour subtitle_file URL expiry to the adapter's operational rules; (4) budget for Hindi alignment quality work (unlicensed 72% WER wav2vec2 model) and Japanese/Chinese character-to-word regrouping in the aligner stage; (5) aeneas-ng cannot be pip-installed on the users' Python 3.12 Windows machines without a 3.11 runtime; (6) consider VoxCPM2 as a second Apache-2.0 local clone model; (7) re-verify Google and Azure prices from primary consoles before locking the cost model.

## images: AI image generation APIs (Oct 2026) for YouTube storyboard images and thumbnails in Cashflow Studio: model landscape, pricing, text rendering, consistency features, aspect ratios, licensing/content rules, on-image text strategy, competitor-thumbnail template extraction, and Google Flow API status

### Summary

As of 9 Oct 2026 the image-API market has turned over almost completely since 2025: Google retired Imagen 4 on the Gemini API (17 Aug 2026) and on Vertex, and its image line is now the "Nano Banana" Gemini family — Nano Banana 2.1 (gemini-nano-banana-2.1, GA 6 Oct 2026, ~$0.034 per 1K image, 16:9/9:16 native, up to 14 reference images, SynthID), Nano Banana 2 Lite (~$0.034/1K, 1K only), and Nano Banana Pro (gemini-3-pro-image, $0.134 per 1K/2K, $0.24 per 4K, strongest Google text rendering). OpenAI shipped GPT Image 2 (21 Apr 2026) and GPT Image 2.5 Flare/Sunburst (8 Sep 2026), billed only in tokens ($30–32 per 1M image-output tokens; roughly $0.01–$0.21 per 1024² image depending on quality), with custom dimensions up to 3840 px edge and the best independently measured in-image text accuracy (≈98–99%) including CJK/Hindi/Bengali; gpt-image-1 shuts down 23 Oct 2026 and gpt-image-1.5/mini on 1 Dec 2026. Black Forest Labs offers FLUX.2 pro/flex/max/klein (≈$0.014–$0.07 per MP, up to 10 refs, 4 MP) and the brand-new FLUX 3 Image (1 Oct 2026, ~$0.048 at 1K to ~$0.61 at 4K list, 0–1000 bounding-box layout, 15 aspect ratios); third-party hosts (fal.ai, Replicate, OpenRouter) resell most of these at similar or slightly higher prices. Ideogram 4.0 (Jun 2026, open weights under a non-commercial agreement) and 4.5 (30 Sep/5 Oct 2026) cost $0.03/$0.06/$0.10 per image and lead on Latin-script typography and JSON bounding-box layout but are weak on diacritics and non-Latin scripts; Recraft V4/V4.1 ($0.007–$0.04, 16:9 at 1344×768, 1–10 style refs, native SVG) is the cheap design/vector specialist. Midjourney still has no official API and its ToS bans automated access; Leonardo has a pay-as-you-go API but publishes no per-image price list publicly; Stability's API is unchanged SD3.5-era tech at $0.03–$0.08 with no 2026 image-model releases. Across every vendor, headlines (≤ ~25–30 words) render reliably but body copy does not, so the robust pattern for script-driven callouts is to generate clean, text-free scene art and overlay text deterministically with Pillow (stroke_width/stroke_fill, textbbox, Noto fonts; libraqm+fribidi DLL needed on Windows for Arabic/Devanagari shaping) or FFmpeg drawtext/ASS for timed captions, reserving model-rendered text for stylised thumbnail hero words. Competitor-thumbnail templating is practical with a vision model: Gemini returns [ymin,xmin,ymax,xmax] boxes normalised to 0–1000 that map directly onto Ideogram 4 / FLUX 3 bounding-box layout inputs, and open-source projects already deconstruct thumbnails into background/graphics/text JSON for about $0.012 per image. Google Flow (flow.google.com) remains a web app with no public API; the only "Flow API" is a third-party browser-automation wrapper (useapi.net) that runs on the user's Google account and is not sanctioned. YouTube's 2026 rules add context: AI-made thumbnails and script assistance need no disclosure, realistic depictions of real people doing things they did not do must be disclosed (YouTube can auto-label since 27 May 2026), and the YPP "inauthentic content" policy penalises templated mass production — so per-video visual variety matters more than tool choice.

### Recommendation

Design Cashflow Studio around a pluggable ImageProvider interface (generate(prompt, aspect, size, refs[], style) → PNG + metadata) because the user says images will come from a tool they already pay for and the market changes every few months (Imagen 4 retired, three OpenAI models retiring in Q4 2026, Nano Banana 2 deprecated days ago). Ship these adapters first: (1) Google Gemini Nano Banana 2.1 as the default storyboard renderer — native 16:9 and 9:16, 1K at $0.034 (or $0.017 via Batch API, which fits a non-interactive 'render all scenes' step), up to 14 reference images so one 'style sheet' image plus the previous scene keeps a channel's look consistent across 30–40 scenes; (2) Nano Banana Pro or GPT Image 2.5 Flare for thumbnails, where model-rendered hero text is worth paying for (Pro is the predictable-price choice at $0.134 for 2K; GPT Image 2.5 is the accuracy choice for Hindi/Japanese/Korean/Arabic thumbnails); (3) Ideogram 4.x and FLUX 3 as optional 'layout-locked' thumbnail engines fed by the template JSON, since both accept 0–1000 bounding boxes. Do not bake script-driven callouts into the scene images: generate text-free art with negative space, then overlay the callout text, arrows and circles with Pillow (stroke_width/stroke_fill, textbbox auto-fit, Noto Sans + Noto Sans CJK, plus fribidi.dll for Arabic/Hindi shaping on Windows) and keep those overlays as editable timeline layers so the CapCut-like editor can move or retime them; burn captions with FFmpeg's ass/drawtext filters driven by the voice timestamps. For the thumbnail template feature, run a Gemini vision pass that returns strict JSON (subject box, emotion, text blocks with boxes/colours/stroke, shapes, palette) at ~$0.01 per competitor thumbnail, store it as a reusable template, and render by compositing (image model for the text-free subject/background, Pillow for text and shapes) so every language variant reuses one layout; offer the single-shot Ideogram/FLUX-3 route as an alternative. Exclude Midjourney (no API, automation banned) and Google Flow (no API; the useapi.net wrapper is an account-level ToS risk) from the automated pipeline, allowing only manual image import. Keep Recraft V4.1 Flash ($0.007) as the budget fallback and for SVG callout assets. Budget roughly $1–2 in image spend per 10-minute video (≈35 scenes at $0.034 plus 3 Pro thumbnails) and under $0.30 for a Short. For policy: add a 'no real persons' guard in prompts by default (Google forbids deceptive impersonation; YouTube requires disclosure when a real person is depicted doing something they did not), avoid brand logos and realistic currency close-ups unless the user confirms rights, write SynthID/C2PA presence into the project metadata so the altered-content checkbox can be set automatically when realistic imagery is used, and randomise scene composition/style per video to stay clear of YouTube's 'inauthentic content' templated-upload rule. Re-verify prices before release: Google's two pages disagree on the gemini-2.5-flash-image shutdown date, OpenAI publishes only token rates, and BFL/Leonardo hide per-model prices behind logged-in calculators.

### Facts

- (high, 2026-10-09) Gemini API pricing lists gemini-3.1-flash-image (Nano Banana 2) at $0.045 per 0.5K image, $0.067 per 1K, $0.101 per 2K and $0.151 per 4K standard; batch pricing is half ($0.034 per 1K). - https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/pricing
- (high, 2026-10-09) Nano Banana Pro (gemini-3-pro-image) is $120 per 1M image-output tokens, equivalent to $0.134 per 1K/2K image and $0.24 per 4K image; batch is $0.067 / $0.12. No free tier. - https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/pricing
- (high, 2026-10-09) Gemini Nano Banana 2.1 (gemini-nano-banana-2.1) costs $30 per 1M image-output tokens, equivalent to $0.0336 per 1K image, $0.0504 per 2K and $0.113 per 4K; batch is $0.0168 / $0.0252 / $0.0567. - https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/pricing
- (high, 2026-10-09) Gemini 3.1 Flash Lite Image (Nano Banana 2 Lite, gemini-3.1-flash-lite-image) costs $0.0336 per 1K image standard and $0.0168 batch; it supports only 1K output. - https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/pricing
- (high, 2026-10-06) Nano Banana 2.1 reached GA on 6 Oct 2026 with improved text rendering, multi-turn character consistency, and new wide/panoramic ratios (1:4, 4:1, 1:8, 8:1) at 1K/2K/4K; gemini-3.1-flash-image was deprecated the same day (no shutdown date). - https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/changelog
- (high, 2026-10-09) Gemini image models support aspect ratios 1:1, 3:2, 2:3, 3:4, 4:3, 4:5, 5:4, 9:16, 16:9 and 21:9, resolutions 0.5K/1K/2K/4K (Lite = 1K only; 2.1 has no 0.5K), up to 14 reference images for composition/character consistency, 'advanced' legible stylised text, and every output carries a SynthID watermark. - https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/image-generation
- (high, 2026-10-09) Imagen 4 models (imagen-4.0-generate-001, -ultra-, -fast-) were shut down on the Gemini API on 17 Aug 2026 with gemini-nano-banana-2.1 as the named replacement; Imagen 3 shut down 10 Nov 2025. - https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/deprecations
- (medium, 2026-06-24) Imagen 3/4 endpoints were deprecated on Vertex AI on 24 Mar 2026 and Firebase AI Logic shut Imagen down 24 Jun 2026; Google maps imagen-4.0-fast to gemini-3.1-flash-image (thinking MINIMAL), imagen-4.0 standard to gemini-3.1-flash-image (thinking HIGH) and imagen-4.0-ultra to gemini-3-pro-image. - https://firebase.google.com/docs/ai-logic/imagen-models-migration
- (medium, 2026-10-09) gemini-2.5-flash-image (original Nano Banana) is deprecated; the pricing page says shutdown 2 Oct 2026 while the deprecations page lists 15 Mar 2027 — the two Google pages conflict. - https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/deprecations
- (high, 2026-10-09) Gemini API terms: Google does not claim ownership of generated output, provides no indemnification, requires users to be 18+, uses unpaid-tier prompts/outputs for product improvement but not paid-tier data, and requires Paid Services when serving users in the EEA, Switzerland or UK. - https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/terms
- (high, 2026-10-09) Google's Generative AI Prohibited Use Policy bans impersonating an individual (living or dead) without disclosure in order to deceive, misrepresenting provenance as human-made, frauds/scams and misleading claims in sensitive areas such as finance, and sexually explicit content; celebrities and brands are covered only via these broader clauses. - https://policies.google.com/terms/generative-ai/use-policy
- (medium, 2026-04-22) Google Cloud's Generative AI Indemnified Services (last modified 22 Apr 2026) cover GA Gemini and Imagen models used through the Gemini Enterprise Agent Platform API (formerly Vertex AI API) — i.e. indemnification exists on Vertex but not on the consumer Gemini API. - https://cloud.google.com/archive/terms/generative-ai-indemnified-services-20260422
- (high, 2026-10-09) Gemini vision returns object-detection bounding boxes as [ymin, xmin, ymax, xmax] normalised to 0–1000, plus optional polygon segmentation masks; images ≤384 px cost 258 tokens, larger images are tiled into 768×768 crops at 258 tokens each. - https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/image-understanding
- (high, 2026-10-09) OpenAI's current image models are gpt-image-2.5-sunburst (precision editing) and gpt-image-2.5-flare (fast everyday), plus gpt-image-2, gpt-image-1.5, gpt-image-1-mini, gpt-image-1 and chatgpt-image-latest; pricing is token-based: $8/1M image input, $30–40/1M image output, $5/1M text input, with batch at roughly half. - https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/pricing
- (high, 2026-10-09) OpenAI image generation supports recommended sizes 1024×1024, 1536×1024, 1024×1536 and custom dimensions (multiples of 16, aspect 1:3–3:1, max edge 3840 px, 655,360–8,294,400 pixels; above 2560×1440 is experimental), quality levels low/medium/high/xhigh/max/auto, reference-image and mask inpainting edits, multi-turn editing via Responses API, and moderation 'auto' or 'low'. - https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/image-generation
- (high, 2026-10-09) OpenAI deprecations: dall-e-2/3 shut down 12 May 2026; gpt-image-1 shuts down 23 Oct 2026; gpt-image-1.5, gpt-image-1-mini and chatgpt-image-latest shut down 1 Dec 2026; replacements are gpt-image-2.5-sunburst / -flare. - https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/deprecations
- (medium, 2026-04-21) GPT Image 2 was released 21 Apr 2026 as a reasoning-native image model with near-perfect text across Latin, CJK, Arabic, Hindi and Bengali scripts. - https://datanorth.ai/news/openai-launches-gpt-image-2-with-99-text-accuracy
- (medium, 2026-09-16) GPT Image 2.5 (Flare and Sunburst) released 8 Sep 2026 with identical token rates; third-party calculators estimate 1024×1024 output-only cost at ~$0.006 (low), $0.013 (medium), $0.053 (high), $0.094 (xhigh), $0.211 (max); 1536×1024 ~ $0.005–$0.165. - https://www.cometapi.com/gpt-image-2-5-price-api-cost-per-image/
- (low, 2026-10-07) Per-image prices for GPT Image 2 are reported inconsistently: one tracker lists flat $0.03 (1K) / $0.05 (2K) / $0.08 (4K) and up to 16 reference images, while others report ~$0.21 for a high-quality 1024² image and ~$0.28 base; OpenAI itself publishes only token rates. - https://unifically.com/blogs/gpt-image-2
- (medium, 2026-08-30) GPT Image 1.5 per-image prices: low $0.009 (1024²) / $0.013 (1536×1024), medium $0.034 / $0.050, high $0.133 / $0.200; image output tokens $32/1M. - https://aireiter.com/blog/gpt-image-1-5-api-pricing
- (medium, 2026-06-12) Independent June 2026 typography benchmark: GPT Image 2 98.5% text accuracy, Nano Banana Pro 94.8%, Nano Banana 2 91.2%, Seedream 5.0 89.5%; Nano Banana 2 showed 'slight AI-waviness' in the smallest text. - https://www.atlascloud.ai/blog/guides/2026-ai-image-api-benchmark-gpt-image-2-vs-nano-banana-2-pro-vs-seedream-5-0
- (medium, 2026-08-06) August 2026 review of text-in-image models: no model renders body copy reliably ('Headlines yes, paragraphs no', ~25–30 words max); Ideogram 4 wins layout control (JSON bounding boxes, 16 hex colours, 47.9% blind-designer win rate) but fails diacritics/non-Latin scripts; GPT Image 2 wins multilingual scripts; Recraft V4.1 wins editability via native SVG; FLUX.2 flex is best in the Flux family for text. - https://invideo.io/blog/best-ai-model-text-in-images/
- (low, 2025-04-12) OpenAI (Mar–Apr 2025) switched ChatGPT image generation to allow public figures with an opt-out mechanism while keeping bans on sexual deepfakes and imitation of living artists; whether identical rules apply to the API is not stated and this may be outdated. - https://getcoai.com/news/chatgpts-image-generator-now-creates-public-figures-and-controversial-content
- (high, 2025-11-25) FLUX.2 (announced 25 Nov 2025) ships as pro and flex (proprietary), dev 32B (open weights) and klein; supports up to 10 reference images, up to 4 MP editing, and 'complex typography, infographics, memes and UI mockups with legible fine text'. - https://bfl.ai/blog/flux-2
- (medium, 2026-08-06) FLUX.2 API list prices: pro $0.03/MP create and $0.045/MP edit, flex $0.05–0.06/MP, max $0.07/MP, klein 4B/9B $0.014–0.015 per image; FLUX.1 Kontext pro $0.04 and max $0.08 per image; only klein-4B is Apache 2.0 — dev-32B and klein-9B outputs are commercial but self-hosting in a commercial product needs a paid licence. - https://invideo.io/blog/flux-ai-image-generator/
- (medium, 2026-10-02) FLUX 3 Image launched 1 Oct 2026: native up to 4K (~16 MP), up to 10 reference images, bounding-box composition on a normalised 0–1000 grid, per-image pricing roughly $0.041 at 768 px to ~$0.607 at 4K (50% launch discount through 8 Oct 2026); no independent leaderboard entries at launch. - https://kompozy.io/news/flux-3-image-launch

- (medium, 2026-10-09) Ideogram claims no ownership of inputs or outputs and allows commercial use on paid plans; free-tier images are public and non-commercial; API default rate limit is 10 in-flight requests; the API also proxies GPT Image, Nano Banana and Topaz. - https://developer.ideogram.ai/ideogram-api/api-overview

- (medium, 2026-10-09) Leonardo.ai sells a separate pay-as-you-go Production API with a $5 non-expiring starter credit and auto top-ups; per-image API prices are not published on the public docs (calculator requires login); third parties list Lucid Origin at $0.03–$0.08 per image; paid consumer plans include unlimited relaxed generations on first-party models. - https://docs.leonardo.ai/docs/payg-guide
- (high, 2026-10-09) Recraft models and starting API prices: V4.1 Flash $0.007 (~1.3 s), V4.1 $0.035, V4 Styles $0.035 (match style from 1–10 reference images), V4 $0.04, V3 $0.04 (exact text placement/text layout), V2 $0.022; V4 Pro ~$0.21–0.25; prepaid units at $1 = 1,000 units. - https://www.recraft.ai/docs
- (high, 2026-10-09) Recraft 1K models (V4, V4.1, Flash, Styles) support 14 aspect ratios incl. 16:9 = 1344×768 and 9:16 = 768×1344; Pro 2K models give 16:9 = 2688×1536; V2/V3 max 1820×1024 for 16:9; prompts up to 10,000 chars on V4+; style references 1–10 images (64 MB total); vector models output SVG with no fixed pixel size. - https://www.recraft.ai/docs/api-reference/appendix
- (high, 2026-10-09) Recraft terms: paid-plan users own all generated assets with copyright assigned and may use them commercially; free-tier assets are owned by Recraft with no commercial use; API plans are governed by separate Developer Terms; users must hold rights for any referenced third-party brand, trademark or identifiable individual. - https://www.recraft.ai/terms
