- (high, 2026-10-09) Stability AI's 2026 news lists Brand Studio (8 Apr), Stable Audio 3.0 (20 May), Series B funding (25 Aug) — no new image generation model or image API pricing change in 2026, so its image stack is still SD3.5-era. - http://stability.ai/news-updates
- (medium, 2026-07-27) Seedream 5.0 Pro (ByteDance, released 28 Jun 2026) offers precise layout control, multilingual text, up to 2K, default 16:9 at 2048×1152 and any ratio 1:16–16:1; first-party pricing $0.045 per image ≤2.36 MP and $0.09 above. - https://www.atlascloud.ai/blog/guides/seedream-5-pro-platform-access
- (medium, 2026-01-16) Google Flow relaunched 25 Feb 2026 as a unified creative studio merging Flow, Whisk and ImageFX, powered by Nano Banana 2, Nano Banana Pro and Veo 3.1; it became available to Google Workspace Business/Enterprise/Education tiers on 14 Jan 2026. - https://workspaceupdates.googleblog.com/2026/01/flow-available-additional-google-service-workspace.html
- (high, 2026-10-09) Google Flow has no public API: 'Google offers Flow as a web app only, with no public API'; the useapi.net 'Google Flow API' is a third-party wrapper that logs into the user's own Google account via automated browser setup, solves reCAPTCHA, exposes Veo 3.1 / Omni 1.1 Flash / Nano Banana 2 Lite, 2.1 and Pro, and costs $15/month on top of a Google AI plan — it is experimental and not sanctioned by Google. - https://useapi.net/docs/api-google-flow-v1
- (medium, 2026-10-09) Independent FAQ updated 9 Oct 2026 confirms 'Currently, Google Flow does not offer a public API for integration.' - https://linkgo.dev/faq/there-an-api-for-google-flow-and-it-be-integrated
- (high, 2026-10-09) Pillow ImageDraw.text/multiline_text support stroke_width and stroke_fill (outlines), anchor, align (left/center/right/justify), spacing, embedded_color, and OpenType 'features' and 'language' (BCP 47) parameters that require libraqm; textbbox/multiline_textbbox measure text before drawing for auto-fit. - https://pillow.readthedocs.io/en/stable/reference/ImageDraw.html
- (high, 2026-10-09) Pillow wheels since 8.2.0 bundle a libraqm that loads FriBiDi at runtime; on Windows a fribidi.dll (or fribidi-0.dll / libfribidi-0.dll) must be installed separately or RTL/complex-script shaping (Arabic, Devanagari) is unsupported. - https://pillow.readthedocs.io/en/stable/installation/building-from-source.html
- (high, 2026-10-09) FFmpeg drawtext supports fontfile/font, fontsize, fontcolor, box/boxcolor/boxborderw, borderw/bordercolor, shadowx/shadowy, line_spacing, textfile, expression-based x/y, timeline 'enable' expressions and text_shaping via libfribidi; it needs FFmpeg built with libfreetype, libfontconfig, libfribidi and libharfbuzz. - https://ffmpeg.org/ffmpeg-filters.html
- (high, 2026-10-09) Noto fonts cover 150+ writing systems (Latin, Greek, Cyrillic, Arabic, Bengali, Devanagari, Thai, etc.) under the SIL Open Font License; Noto Sans/Serif CJK provide JP, KR, SC and TC variants and are also on Google Fonts. - https://github.com/notofonts/noto-cjk
- (high, 2026-10-09) An open-source Claude/Gemini skill deconstructs YouTube thumbnails into Background → Graphics → Text layers with a JSON schema (face presence/position/emotion, arrows/shapes, word count, typography style, layout pattern, predicted CTR) using a Gemini image model at ~$0.012 per thumbnail (~2,200 tokens), batching 5 images with 2-second delays (136 thumbnails in ~15 minutes). - https://raw.githubusercontent.com/florianrolke/youtube-creator-style-reverse-engineering/master/skills/analyze-thumbnails/SKILL.md
- (high, 2026-10-09) Thumbnail Anatomy Lab (open source) combines tesseract.js OCR with headline/subtext/badge classification, canvas-based 6-colour palette extraction with hex codes, font identification against common YouTube typefaces, and GPT-4o or Gemini Flash vision for face detection, pointing direction, props, background type, effects (glow, rim light, stroke, shadow) and layout patterns. - https://github.com/comicsepoch/thumbnail-anatomy-lab
- (medium, 2026-10-09) YouTube custom thumbnails: videos 3840×2160 recommended (min width 640, 16:9), Shorts 2160×3840 (min height 640, 9:16), JPG or PNG, 2 MB limit on mobile uploads and 50 MB on desktop; verified account required; no nudity, hate, violence or harmful content. - https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/72431
- (high, 2026-10-09) YouTube disclosure rules: realistic AI content that makes a real person appear to say/do something they did not, alters real events/places, or generates realistic scenes that never occurred must be labelled; thumbnails, scripts, animation, colour/lighting edits and clearly unrealistic content are exempt; repeated non-disclosure can lead to removal or YPP suspension; YouTube may auto-label via C2PA or internal detection. - https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/14328491
- (high, 2026-10-09) YouTube's YPP 'inauthentic content' policy (renamed from 'repetitious content' on 15 Jul 2025) prohibits mass-produced, generic, repetitive content including 'AI-generated content made with generic or unoriginal templates' lacking creator perspective, while allowing AI and templates when the result shows creative vision and educational/entertainment value. - https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/1311392
- (low, 2026-05-27) Since 27 May 2026 YouTube can add an AI label itself when a creator does not disclose, with auto-detection targeting significant photorealistic AI. - https://air.io/en/monetization/youtube-monetization-policy-changes-2026-a-complete-dated-timeline

### Open questions

- Which paid image tool does the user actually subscribe to (Midjourney? Leonardo? Google AI Pro/Flow? ChatGPT Plus?) — if it is a subscription without an API (Midjourney, Flow, ChatGPT), the automated pipeline must still buy API images separately, contradicting the near-zero-cost goal.
- Exact per-image cost of GPT Image 2 / 2.5 at 1920×1080 and 1080×1920 at each quality tier — sources conflict ($0.03–$0.28); needs a live test with the OpenAI usage dashboard.
- Official Leonardo Production API credit-per-image rates for Lucid Origin / Lucid Realism (calculator requires login).
- Ideogram 4.0/4.5 character-reference and style-reference surcharges (only 3.0 surcharges are published).
- Whether Google's gemini-2.5-flash-image shutdown is 2 Oct 2026 (pricing page) or 15 Mar 2027 (deprecations page) — relevant only if any legacy code targets it.
- How each vendor's safety filter treats finance-specific imagery: realistic banknotes/coins (counterfeiting filters), stock tickers with real company logos, and recognisable billionaires/CEOs — needs an empirical probe per model with the exact prompts Cashflow Studio will emit.
- Do OpenAI's API moderation settings ('low') permit public-figure likenesses the way ChatGPT does, and how will YouTube's auto-labelling (C2PA/SynthID) interact with images that have overlays re-encoded by FFmpeg?
- Whether FLUX 3 Image's quality and edit consistency hold up on independent leaderboards (none at launch) before relying on it for layout-locked thumbnails.
- Noto font bundling size and the licensing path for shipping fribidi.dll with a Windows installer for multiple non-developer users.
- Whether the Windows laptops have GPUs sufficient for a zero-cost local fallback (FLUX.2 klein-4B Apache 2.0, Ideogram 4 non-commercial weights) — likely not, so the local path may be unrealistic.

## editor: Giving a Python (Windows, multi-user, local) faceless-video pipeline a CapCut-like timeline editor for manual fixes: CapCut draft format and pyJianYingDraft/pyCapCut, interchange formats (OTIO, FCPXML, Premiere XML, Kdenlive, Shotcut MLT), embeddable web timeline editors, Python desktop options, effort estimates and a hybrid architecture (as of 2026-10-09)

### Summary

As of October 2026 there is no drop-in, zero-cost, MIT-licensed "CapCut-class" editor you can embed in a Python app; the realistic path is a hybrid: your pipeline owns a canonical JSON timeline, FFmpeg renders from it, a lightweight embedded web editor (React timeline component + canvas preview, served by the local Python process into a WebView2/pywebview window or the browser) handles the 80% of manual fixes, and one or two export "escape hatches" hand the project to a real NLE for the rest. The CapCut draft format (draft_content.json) is reverse-engineered and writable for CapCut International (unencrypted in every tested version 6.x-9.x), but it is fragile: CapCut 8.4+ refuses drafts whose version markers were not seeded from an app-created project, 10.x builds reject tool-written drafts as corrupted, Chinese JianYing 6.0+ encrypts drafts entirely, and the Python library for International CapCut (GuanYixuan/pyCapCut, 695 stars, active) ships without any license file while pyJianYingDraft (4.5k stars, Apache-2.0) targets only JianYing 5.9. For a free, Windows-capable pro editor hand-off, generating a Kdenlive .kdenlive (MLT XML) is the only interchange route that carries image clips, keyframed zoom/pan (Transform/qtblend keyframes) and embedded title clips; FCPXML 1.9+ carries adjust-transform position/scale keyframes into DaVinci Resolve (titles are dropped; Resolve free 21.1 also removed Python scripting), while OpenTimelineIO (0.18.1, Nov 2025) and Premiere/Resolve OTIO import carry only cuts, tracks and markers, never effects, keyframes or styled text. On the web side the MIT options are @openvideo/timeline (the renamed designcombo engine, npm 1.4.0 Aug 2026, Fabric.js-based), @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor (v1.0.0 Jan 2026), omniclip (MIT, active, but keyframes still on the roadmap) and OpenCut (MIT, 93k stars, but mid ground-up Rust rewrite and not accepting contributions); Remotion's Timeline ($300) and Editor Starter ($600) require a Company License ($25/seat/month plus $100/month automation minimum) once your organization exceeds three people; Twick (Sustainable Use License), Shotstack Studio (PolyForm Shield) and etro (GPL-3.0) carry license constraints. Pure-Python desktop timelines (PySide6/QML, Kivy) have no reusable open-source timeline widget and would be written from scratch, which is why OpenShot itself embeds an HTML/JS timeline inside Qt WebEngine. Honest effort: a basic web editor (reorder, trim, swap image, edit text/caption, change music, synced preview) is 4-6 developer-weeks on top of an existing MIT timeline component (7-9 weeks from scratch with undo/redo, snapping, waveforms); a CapCut-lite with keyframe zoom/pan editing, text-style panel, transitions and ducking adds 8-12 weeks; a full CapCut-class NLE is a 2-4 developer-year product (OpenCut, with 93k stars and 15 months of work, is rewriting from zero; omniclip after three years still lacks keyframes). Treat CapCut draft export as a best-effort convenience for users who already live in CapCut, pinned to a validated CapCut version with auto-update disabled, not as the primary editing surface.

### Recommendation

Adopt a hybrid in three layers. (1) Source of truth: a versioned 'Cashflow Timeline JSON' your pipeline already produces (scenes with image path, in/out times from voice word timestamps, Ken Burns start/end rect, text/popup callouts with style, caption cues, transitions, music with ducking regions, aspect variant 16:9/9:16). FFmpeg (already installed) renders from it: per-image scale+crop keyframed via zoompan or expression-driven crop, xfade between scenes, ASS subtitles via libass for captions/popups, amix + sidechaincompress for ducking. Never make a third-party editor's file format the canonical state. (2) Embedded 'fix-it' editor: a React/Vite single-page app served by the local Python process (FastAPI/uvicorn) and shown in a pywebview 6.2.1 window (WebView2, present on Win10/11) or the default browser; timeline UI from @openvideo/timeline (MIT, npm 1.4.0 Aug 2026) or @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor (MIT, v1.0.0 Jan 2026); canvas preview with PixiJS or Fabric.js 7 that interpolates the same Ken Burns rects and draws captions with the same fonts; wavesurfer.js 8 for the music/voice tracks; features in scope for v1: reorder scenes, trim/ripple to voice boundaries, swap/regenerate image, edit on-image text and caption text/style, change/offset music and ducking, toggle transitions, set a Short's 9:16 crop, 'render preview' button that asks FFmpeg for a low-res proxy of the selected range. Load the artifact-capabilities/engineering effort at 4-6 developer-weeks for this basic editor, 7-9 weeks if built without a timeline component, plus 8-12 weeks for a CapCut-lite tier (keyframe handles on the canvas for zoom/pan, text-style presets, transition picker, music beat snapping, per-language caption tracks). Do not attempt a full CapCut-class NLE: evidence (OpenCut rewriting from scratch after 15 months and 93k stars; omniclip three years in without keyframes) puts that at 2-4 developer-years. (3) Escape hatches for 'full editor' requests, in priority order: Kdenlive .kdenlive export (free on Windows, carries Transform/qtblend keyframes, titles, audio; pin and fixture-test against one Kdenlive version), FCPXML 1.9 export with adjust-transform keyframes for DaVinci Resolve free (captions delivered as SRT/burned-in because Resolve drops titles), and CapCut International draft export as an optional, clearly-labelled best-effort feature only for users already on CapCut: seed every draft from the newest app-created project in %LOCALAPPDATA%\\CapCut\\User Data\\Projects\\com.lveditor.draft, pin the validated CapCut version, disable auto-update, and keep a per-version fixture test; prefer writing your own thin draft writer from the schema docs (or using MIT capcut-cli logic) over depending on pyCapCut until it gains a license. Avoid Remotion (Company License costs once the team exceeds three people), GPL etro, and Twick/Shotstack unless legal clears their source-available terms; treat OpenCut as a 2027 candidate when its Editor API/headless mode ships. Ship OTIO only as a cuts-only secondary export. Verify on real user laptops before building: installed CapCut version, Kdenlive version, WebView2 presence, and that browser-rendered captions match libass output closely enough for non-developers to trust the preview.

### Options

| Option | Pros | Cons | Pricing | API / integration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A. Own JSON timeline + FFmpeg renderer + embedded web editor built on @openvideo/timeline (MIT) or @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor (MIT), Python-served (FastAPI/uvicorn or pywebview), preview via canvas/HTML5 media (RECOMMENDED CORE) | Zero license cost and no per-seat/render fees; works offline on each user's laptop; you control the schema (scenes, images, Ken Burns keyframes, caption cues from word timestamps, music/ducking, 16:9 and 9:16 variants); FFmpeg already installed; browser preview of images+text+audio is cheap (no WebCodecs encode needed because FFmpeg does the final render); @openvideo/timeline is actively published (1.4.0, Aug 2026) and Fabric.js-based; xzdarcy is small, stable, MIT. | You own the UI code: compositing preview (zoom/pan interpolation, caption rendering) must mirror FFmpeg output closely (font rendering differs between browser and libass); @openvideo packages have thin docs and the parent app repo is dual-licensed (confirm the MIT npm packages stand alone); xzdarcy has 42 open issues and is a generic 'animation timeline' not a video NLE; undo/redo, snapping, waveform thumbnails, multi-select are your work. | $0 (MIT). Developer time only: ~4-6 weeks basic, +8-12 weeks CapCut-lite. | Python local HTTP server serves /timeline.json, /media/*, /render (FFmpeg: zoompan or scale+crop per image, xfade, subtitles/ass, amix + sidechaincompress); React app with @openvideo/timeline or @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor, PixiJS/Fabric canvas preview, wavesurfer.js 8 for music track; pywebview 6.2.1 window on Windows (WebView2) or default browser. |
| B. CapCut International draft export (pyCapCut or own writer from the capcut-cli schema docs) | Users who already know CapCut get a fully editable project with image segments, keyframes (position/scale/rotation/opacity), styled text, SRT captions, transitions, animations, audio fades; CapCut International drafts are plain JSON in every tested version (6.x-9.x); CapCut is free; pyCapCut is pure Python and actively pushed (2026-10-01). | Reverse-engineered, unsupported by ByteDance; CapCut 8.4+ rejects drafts unless seeded from an app-created project; 10.x refuses tool-written drafts as corrupted; every CapCut auto-update can break it; pyCapCut has NO license file (cannot safely vendor/redistribute without author permission) and its batch-export is unfinished; JianYing (Chinese) 6.0+ is encrypted and off the table; manual export inside CapCut is still required (no headless render). | $0 software; high maintenance risk. capcut-cli (MIT, Node) is a licensed alternative but adds a Node runtime to the Windows install. | Write draft folder to %LOCALAPPDATA%\CapCut\User Data\Projects\com.lveditor.draft; copy version markers/canvas_config from the newest app-authored draft on that machine; pyCapCut (pip install pycapcut) or a 300-500 line in-house writer using tracks/segments/materials from the schema cheat sheet; keep a per-CapCut-version fixture test. |
| C. Kdenlive .kdenlive (MLT XML) export for lossless hand-off to a free Windows NLE | Only free interchange that carries image clips, keyframed zoom/pan (Transform/qtblend keyframes as '0=...;100=...' strings), embedded title clips (kdenlive_title XML), audio tracks and transitions; Kdenlive runs on Windows, is GPL-free-of-charge and actively developed (25.12 released, 26.04 pending, Microsoft Store build underway); reference Python code exists (Kdenlive MCP server, kdenlive_slideshow_editor, gpapp/mlt-python). | Document format changes per release (version 1.1 since 23.04, not backward compatible) so you need fixtures per Kdenlive version; keyframe system is being refactored (per-parameter keyframes/dopesheet) which may alter the stored syntax; the reference tools are immature (0-star, unlicensed MCP server); Kdenlive UX is less polished than CapCut for non-developers. | $0 (Kdenlive GPL, free download). | Generate MLT XML with profile, chains/producers for PNG/JPG images (duration in frames), playlists per track, tractor, main_bin, kdenlive:docproperties.version=1.1, <filter mlt_service="qtblend"> with keyframed rect for Ken Burns, title producers for popups, avformat chains for voice/music; validate by opening in the pinned Kdenlive version and with melt. |
| D. FCPXML 1.9/1.10 export for DaVinci Resolve (free) with adjust-transform keyframes | Resolve free imports FCPXML Position/Scale/Rotation/Opacity including keyframes, so Ken Burns moves survive; Resolve is the most capable free NLE on Windows; FCPXML is well documented by Apple; no dependency on reverse engineering. | Resolve drops FCP titles, so on-image text/popups and captions must be burned in or delivered as SRT; Resolve free 21.1 removed Python scripting entirely (Studio $295), so no programmatic Resolve project building; Resolve's accepted FCPXML versions (1.3-1.9 as of 2021) need re-verification for Resolve 20/21; OTIO FCPX adapter in Python is effectively unmaintained (last push 2024-06-25) so you hand-write the XML. | $0 (Resolve free). Studio $295 perpetual only if scripting/external control is wanted. | Write <fcpxml version="1.9"> with <asset> per image/audio, <spine> of <video>/<asset-clip> with <adjust-transform><param name="position"/scale"><keyframeAnimation><keyframe time value/></...>; import via File > Import Timeline in Resolve; ship captions as .srt alongside. |
| E. OpenTimelineIO (.otio) export | Industry-standard, Apache-2.0 Python library (0.18.1, Nov 2025); natively imported by Resolve 18.5+, Premiere 25.6+, Kdenlive 25.04+; good for cut lists and markers; could be your archival/interchange layer for clip order and durations. | Cannot carry Ken Burns keyframes, styled text/titles, transitions or effects in any current importer (Kdenlive, Premiere and Remotion docs all confirm effects/text are dropped); image clips without duration semantics vary by importer; the FCPX/FCP7 adapters are separate, low-activity plugins. | $0. | pip install OpenTimelineIO; build otio.schema.Timeline with tracks/clips (ExternalReference to PNG/WAV) and markers; use only as a secondary 'cuts-only' export. |
| F. omniclip (MIT) as embedded editor or fork | MIT, active (last push 2026-10-08), WebCodecs renderer, text/audio/video/image, transitions, filters, undo/redo, 4K export, README says components can be embedded; TypeScript, no framework lock-in to React. | No keyframe/zoom-pan support yet (roadmap); built on the niche @benev/slate framework which complicates deep customization; 2.0 rewrite in progress so APIs may churn; project JSON format undocumented; embedding story unproven. | $0 (MIT). | Fork, host the built bundle from the Python server, exchange project JSON; expect to write the keyframe track yourself. |
| G. OpenCut (MIT, 93k stars) | Largest open-source CapCut alternative, MIT, well funded (fal.ai sponsor), explicit roadmap for Editor API, plugins, headless mode and MCP; Rust core + Tauri desktop will eventually be a strong local editor. | In a complete ground-up rewrite (classic archived 2026-05-17), not accepting contributions, no stable Editor API or documented project format today; unsuitable as a dependency for a 2026 ship date; watch for 2027. | $0 (MIT). | None stable yet; future headless mode/Editor API could consume your JSON timeline. |
| H. Remotion Player + Remotion Timeline ($300) or Editor Starter ($600) | Polished, officially maintained React timeline/editor pieces with Player sync, thumbnails, waveforms, captions; fastest path to a professional-looking editor; Remotion can also render final video (server-side via Node/Chrome). | Free license only for organizations of up to 3 people; your multi-user team likely exceeds this, triggering $25/seat/month and, if you automate renders, $0.01/render with $100/month minimum - contradicts the near-zero running-cost goal; Remotion render needs Node + headless Chromium on each laptop; templates are source copies, not npm packages. | Timeline $300 one-time, Editor Starter $600 one-time; Company License $25/seat/month + $100/month automation minimum for >3 people. | React + @remotion/player; render via @remotion/renderer (Node) or keep FFmpeg for final output and use Remotion only for preview. |
| I. Twick SDK (Sustainable Use License) or Shotstack Studio SDK (PolyForm Shield) | Both are full React/TS editor SDKs with JSON-serializable timelines, image/video/audio/text, Twick adds keyframes and transitions; both render in-browser with WebCodecs; actively pushed (Shotstack 2026-10-08, Twick 2026-06-04); no fees for use inside your own product. | Not OSI open source: Twick forbids reselling as an SDK/competing dev tools; PolyForm Shield forbids products competing with Shotstack (a video-editing API/editor - your product is adjacent, legal review needed); Shotstack's data model is its own Edit JSON; Twick's browser render is Chrome/Edge only and uses ffmpeg.wasm for audio. | $0 for in-product use under their terms; commercial license needed for redistribution as SDK. | @twick/studio + @twick/timeline + @twick/live-player; or @shotstack/shotstack-studio (pixi.js v8) editing Shotstack Edit JSON converted from your timeline. |
| J. etro (GPL-3.0) browser compositor | Mature (since 2018, 1.1k stars, pushed 2026-10-05), layers for text/image/video/audio, keyframed parameters, JSON serialization, GLSL effects - a ready preview compositor. | GPL-3.0 copyleft would force your distributed app's linked web code to be GPL; no timeline UI (only a compositing engine); 74 open issues. | $0 but GPL obligations. | Use only if the whole client is GPL; otherwise avoid. |
| K. Native PySide6/QML (or Kivy) timeline | Single-language Python desktop app, LGPL PySide6 6.12 (Oct 2026), QtMultimedia for playback, no web stack; Kdenlive/Shotcut show QML timelines are feasible in principle. | No reusable open-source Qt/QML timeline component exists (Qt forum 2025, unsolved); Kdenlive/Shotcut timelines are GPL C++; everything (tracks, clips, trimming, snapping, keyframe curves, waveform drawing, text styling panel) is built from scratch; Kivy has only a VideoPlayer widget; PySide6 wheels add ~200 MB to the installer; hiring/outsourcing pool for Qt UI is smaller than React. | $0 licenses; 10-16 developer-weeks for a basic timeline, 2x the web estimate. | PySide6 QML ListView/Flickable-based tracks, QQuickPaintedItem for waveforms, QMediaPlayer for audio; Python model exposes the same timeline JSON. |

### Facts

- (high, 2026-10-09) pyJianYingDraft (GuanYixuan) has 4,508 stars, 677 forks, 51 open issues, Apache-2.0 license, created 2024-07-17, last push 2026-09-26; it is not archived. - https://api.github.com/repos/GuanYixuan/pyJianYingDraft
- (high, 2026-10-09) pyJianYingDraft targets Chinese JianYing (primary support version 5.9); JianYing 7+/6.0+ drafts are encrypted; its auto-export only supports JianYing 6 and below; the international CapCut variant is developed separately in pyCapCut. - https://github.com/GuanYixuan/pyJianYingDraft
- (high, 2026-10-09) pyJianYingDraft/pyCapCut feature set: multi-track video/audio/text/effect/filter tracks, local image/video/audio import with timing and speed, keyframes on position/scale/rotation/opacity (and saturation) for video, text and sticker segments, text fonts/size/color/outline/shadow/background/auto-wrap, SRT import, text animations, audio fades, transitions with custom duration, masks, chroma key, blend modes, intro/outro/loop animations, and template mode (load existing draft, replace materials/text). - https://raw.githubusercontent.com/GuanYixuan/pyCapCut/main/README.md
- (high, 2026-10-09) pyCapCut (international CapCut) has 695 stars, 98 forks, created 2025-08-04, last push 2026-10-01, and GitHub reports NO license (license spdx null; /license endpoint 404) - a legal/compliance risk for redistribution. - https://api.github.com/repos/GuanYixuan/pyCapCut
- (high, 2026-10-09) pyCapCut states generated drafts must still be exported with Windows CapCut, batch/auto-export is 'under migration' from pyJianYingDraft, template mode cannot add segments to imported tracks, curve speed is unsupported, and keyframes are unavailable for effect/filter parameters; no supported CapCut version numbers are stated. - https://github.com/GuanYixuan/pyCapCut
- (high, 2026-10-09) capcut-cli (renezander030) is a TypeScript/Node >=18 MIT tool created 2026-04-13, last push 2026-10-09, 847 stars, that reads/writes CapCut International and JianYing (--jianying) drafts: create drafts, add video/audio/text, transitions, captions (SRT/VTT), effects, long-form to shorts; latest release noted v0.28.0. - https://github.com/renezander030/capcut-cli
- (high, 2026-10-09) capcut-cli version support: CapCut 6.2.8 fixture-tested; 7.9/8.9 (macOS), 8.4.0, 8.7.0 (Windows), 9.2.8-beta4, 9.3.0 reported working when drafts are seeded from real app-created projects; 6.5-8.0 expected compatible but unverified; CapCut 10.x (Mac and Windows) rejects tool-written drafts as corrupted and needs --force-write; JianYing 6.0+ encrypted (cannot decrypt); JianYing 11.4.0 accepts plaintext drafts and upgrades them to encrypted in place. - https://github.com/renezander030/capcut-cli/blob/master/docs/version-support.md
- (medium, 2026-09-17) CapCut 8.4 and later reject drafts with outdated version markers with the error 'This draft comes from an unconventional path and cannot be used'; workaround is to seed generated drafts from a project the installed app created; CapCut International is unencrypted across all tested versions; Windows draft folder is %LOCALAPPDATA%\CapCut\User Data\Projects\com.lveditor.draft; advice: archive the validated installer, back up the draft folder, disable auto-updates. - https://voyager.so/blog/capcut-mcp
- (medium, 2026-10-07) draft_content.json schema: top-level id, name, duration (microseconds), fps, canvas_config, platform, tracks[] (ordered, top of array = top of stack; types video/audio/text/image/sticker/effect/subtitle/filter), materials pools by type, extra_info; segments carry material_id, target_timerange, source_timerange, extra_material_refs[]; text materials are JSON-in-JSON with style ranges in UTF-16LE byte offsets; CapCut 6-9 plain JSON, JianYing 5.9 plain, JianYing 6.0+ encrypted; gist published 2026-05-25 and last updated 2026-10-07; full keyframed clip.transform anatomy is listed as not yet documented. - https://gist.github.com/renezander030/80823f1d47081c312d2c1f9edd20dc22
- (low, 2026) Public 2026 guides describe current CapCut for Windows as v8.5.0+, while capcut-cli reports 10.x builds in the wild; the exact installed version on target laptops must be verified and pinned. - https://www.windowsmode.com/capcut-for-windows/
- (high, 2025-11-09) OpenTimelineIO latest release is v0.18.1 (2025-11-09, wheel fix); v0.18.0 (2025-11-06) added Color support and an 'enabled' flag on Effects and removed OTIOView from core; v0.17.0 (2024-06-24) moved adapters out of core into separately versioned PyPI packages. License Apache-2.0, requires Python >3.9. - https://api.github.com/repos/AcademySoftwareFoundation/OpenTimelineIO/releases?per_page=3
- (high, 2024-06-25) The OTIO FCPX XML adapter supports single/multiple video tracks, audio, gaps, markers, nesting but NOT transitions, audio/video effects, speed effects or image sequence references; the plugin repo (otio-fcpx-xml-adapter) has 7 stars and its last push was 2024-06-25. - https://github.com/OpenTimelineIO/otio-fcpx-xml-adapter
- (high, 2025-04) Kdenlive 25.04 (April 2025) rewrote its OpenTimelineIO import/export on the C++ library (OTIO first added in 23.08); effects, filters and transitions are NOT exported via OTIO; 25.04.2 fixed OTIO track order. - https://ubuntuhandbook.org/index.php/2025/04/kdenlive-25-04-0-released/
- (medium, 2025) DaVinci Resolve natively imports/exports OTIO (.otio and .otioz) from version 18.5 onward. - https://github.com/eric-with-a-c/resolve-otio
- (medium, 2025) Premiere Pro (25.6+) imports and exports OTIO: clip names, cut list with in/out, durations, timecode, fps, multiple tracks and names, linear speed, sequence and clip markers; effects/keyframes/text are not part of the interchange and some sequence settings need manual adjustment on import. - https://helpx.adobe.com/premiere-pro/kb/fixed-issues.html
- (high, 2026) Remotion's own OTIO export documentation confirms that text, shapes, animations, effects, transitions, volume and transform styles cannot be represented in OTIO and must be baked into rendered video; only native video/audio/image clips and track positions survive; it names Resolve 18.5+ and Premiere 25.6+ as importers. - https://www.remotion.dev/docs/export-opentimeline
- (high, 2026) FCPXML expresses keyframed Ken Burns moves with <adjust-transform> containing <param name="position"> and <param name="scale"> each holding <keyframeAnimation><keyframe time="0s" value="-5 10"/>...</keyframeAnimation>; position units are percent of frame, scale is a multiplier. - https://developer.apple.com/documentation/professional-video-applications/animation.md
- (medium, 2021-12-18) DaVinci Resolve imports FCPXML Opacity, Position, Scale and Rotation including keyframed animation, but does not import Final Cut titles (proprietary format); as of Dec 2021 Resolve accepted FCPXML 1.3-1.9 only - version acceptance for Resolve 20/21 should be re-verified. - https://larryjordan.com/articles/round-tripping-projects-between-final-cut-pro-and-davinci-resolve/
- (high, 2026-09-10) DaVinci Resolve free edition v21.1 removed Python scripting entirely ('moved the ability to script in Python to the Studio version'); Studio is a $295 perpetual license. Programmatic Resolve timeline building therefore requires Studio; FCPXML/OTIO file import remains the free route. - https://www.pugetsystems.com/blog/2026/09/10/how-davinci-resolve-free-v21-1-scripting-changes-affect-puget-bench/
- (high, 2025-11-19) Premiere Pro had a bug (reported 2025-09-14, through v25.5) where XML-imported timelines lost motion properties/keyframes; Adobe marked it fixed in 25.6.1 (2025-11-19). Premiere (FCP7 xmeml) is a paid product and a weak target for a zero-cost pipeline. - https://community.adobe.com/t5/bug-premiere-pro/p:-imported-xml-timeline---motion-properties-disappeared/idi-p/15504185
- (high, 2026) Kdenlive project files are MLT XML (document version 1.1 since 23.04); they store references to media plus 'position, duration and edits of clips in the timeline, with applied effects and their parameters including keyframes'; only title and color clips are embedded; newer-format files do not open in older Kdenlive versions (manual 26.08). - https://docs.kdenlive.org/en/project_and_asset_management/file_management/project_files.html
- (medium, 2026) Kdenlive dev docs: structure is profile -> producers/chains -> playlists -> tractors -> main_bin -> final tractor; kdenlive:* properties; effects are MLT <filter> elements with keyframes stored as property strings like '0=...;100=...'; title clips are embedded as kdenlive_title XML in a producer property. - https://github.com/KDE/kdenlive/blob/master/dev-docs/fileformat.md
- (high, 2026) Kdenlive's Transform effect (MLT qtblend) supports keyframes for position/size (X,Y,W,H), rotation and compositing and is maintained on Linux/Windows/macOS; the separate 'Zoom Pan' effect (avfilter zoompan) has no keyframes and the manual recommends using Transform instead. - https://docs.kdenlive.org/en/effects_and_filters/video_effects/transform_distort_perspective/transform.html
- (high, 2026-04-18) State of Kdenlive (2026-04-18): releases 25.04/25.08/25.12 with 26.04 upcoming; MLT is being ported to MSVC for Microsoft Store distribution; a keyframe-system refactor with per-parameter keyframes and a dopesheet is in progress; 25.12 improved the titler's SVG/image handling. - https://planet.kde.org/kdenlive-2026-04-18-state-of-kdenlive-2026/
- (medium, 2026-09-29) A Python Kdenlive MCP server (12bijaya/MCP_Server_Kdenlive) writes .kdenlive files directly with 124 tools incl. push/pull/pan camera moves on easing curves, generic keyframing, beat-synced audio, transitions; but it was created 2026-08-19, has 0 stars, no license, and no stated Kdenlive version targets - useful as reference code, not as a dependency. - https://glama.ai/mcp/servers/12bijaya/MCP_Server_Kdenlive
- (medium, 2026) kdenlive_slideshow_editor (michalfapso) is a Python3/PyQt5 MIT tool that generates Kdenlive slideshows with Ken Burns pan/zoom and beat-synced transitions, using madmom and a patched OpenTimelineIO. - https://github.com/michalfapso/kdenlive_slideshow_editor
- (high, 2026) Shotcut loads generated MLT XML only if Shotcut-specific annotations are present: tractor property shotcut=1, shotcut:projectAudioChannels, playlist shotcut:name/audio/video, producer shotcut:caption/hash, filter shotcut:filter and shotcut:animIn/animOut, a 'main bin' playlist, and a 'background' black playlist as the first track; since 21.05.01 avformat producers are <chain> elements. - https://shotcut.org/notes/mltxml-annotations/
- (high, 2026-10-08) omniclip is an MIT TypeScript browser video editor (WebCodecs rendering) with 1,465 stars, created 2023-11-05, last push 2026-10-08; README claims components can be embedded in other web projects; supports trim/split, text, audio, video, images, transitions, filters, 4K export, undo/redo; keyframe animation/zoom-pan is on the roadmap and NOT implemented; omniclip 2.0 is in development. - https://github.com/omni-media/omniclip/blob/main/README.md
- (high, 2026-09-24) OpenCut (OpenCut-app/OpenCut) is MIT, 93,274 stars, 9,153 forks, created 2025-06-22, last push 2026-09-24; it is undergoing a complete ground-up rewrite around a Rust core with Tauri desktop, the classic codebase is archived (opencut-classic archived, last push 2026-05-17), external contributions are not accepted during the rewrite, and Editor API / plugins / headless mode / MCP are roadmap items only. - https://github.com/OpenCut-app/OpenCut

The older @designcombo/timeline stopped at 5.5.8 on 2024-12-10 with no license field. - https://registry.npmjs.org/@openvideo/timeline

- (high, 2026) Remotion free license covers individuals, teams of up to 3 people and non-profits; organizations with 4+ people need a Company License: $25/month per person writing Remotion code, and/or $0.01 per automated render with a $100/month minimum; using the Remotion Player or automation counts. - https://www.remotion.dev/docs/license/faq

### Open questions

- Which CapCut International build is actually installed on the users' Windows laptops (8.x vs 10.x), can auto-update be disabled per policy, and will ByteDance keep International drafts unencrypted (JianYing already encrypts since 6.0)?
- pyCapCut has no license file: will the author add one (pyJianYingDraft is Apache-2.0), or must the project write its own draft writer / port capcut-cli's MIT logic to Python?
- Can @openvideo/timeline and @openvideo/core (MIT on npm) be used standalone by an organization with more than 3 employees, given the parent react-video-editor repo's dual-license statement?
- How many people will operate the tool (the Remotion free threshold is 3; this also matters for any other per-seat licenses)?
- How closely must the browser preview match the FFmpeg/libass render (font fallback for the top-10 YouTube languages incl. Hindi, Arabic RTL, Japanese, Korean, Russian) before non-developer users trust the editor?
- Which Kdenlive version will be pinned for .kdenlive export, and does the in-progress keyframe-system refactor (per-parameter keyframes) change the stored keyframe syntax in 26.x?
- Does DaVinci Resolve 20/21 still accept FCPXML 1.9 (the 2021 source said 1.3-1.9), and do image clips with adjust-transform keyframes import with correct duration?
- Will omniclip 2.0 or OpenCut's rewrite expose a documented embeddable API/JSON format in time to replace the in-house editor, and is it worth tracking both for 2027?
- Does the project need in-browser rendering at all (WebCodecs) or is FFmpeg-only final rendering with proxy previews acceptable for all users?
- How should per-user local data (drafts, generated images, voice files) be structured so an edited timeline JSON can be re-rendered or re-exported later without re-paying for voice/images?

### Fact-check

Confirmed claims: 38

- REFUTED: Claim 43: OpenShot (Python/PyQt5) implements its drag-and-drop timeline in HTML5/JavaScript hosted in QtWebEngine - a precedent for the Python-shell + web-timeline hybrid. - GitHub release notes for v4.0.0 (2026-08-30) and v4.0.1 (2026-09-27) describe the 'Redesigned native Qt timeline'; the directory listing at https://api.github.com/repos/OpenShot/openshot-qt/contents/src/windows/views and the raw source at https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OpenShot/openshot-qt/develop/src/windows/views/timeline.py show a QWidget backend; Wikipedia (the cited source) does not mention HTML/JS/QtWebEngine at all. Correction: Outdated as of 2026-08-30. OpenShot 4.0.0 (released 2026-08-30) and 4.0.1 (2026-09-27) replaced the HTML/AngularJS/QtWebEngine timeline with a 'Redesigned native Qt timeline'. On the develop branch, src/windows/views/ contains timeline.py (293 KB) plus a timeline_backend/ package (geometry/, paint/, qwidget/, snap.py, state.py, theme.py); timeline.py imports 'from .timeline_backend.qwidget import TimelineWidget' and logs 'Timeline backend: QWidget'. There is no webview.py and no src/timeline/ HTML directory any more. OpenShot is therefore a counter-precedent (web timeline -> native Qt), and it also means a pure-Python, open-source Qt timeline widget now exists (but under openshot-qt's GPL-3.0-or-later, so it is reference material, not a dependency for a non-GPL product). https://api.github.com/repos/OpenShot/openshot-qt/releases?per_page=4
- REFUTED: Claim 45: pywebview latest is 6.2.1 (2024-12-19), BSD-3-Clause, Python >=3.8. - PyPI release history and GitHub releases (https://api.github.com/repos/r0x0r/pywebview/releases?per_page=5, tag 6.2.1 published_at 2026-04-15T09:01:26Z) both give April 2026. Correction: Version, license and Python floor are correct, but the date is wrong by ~16 months: pywebview 6.2.1 was released 2026-04-15 (6.2 on 2026-04-13, 6.1 on 2025-10-21, 6.0 on 2025-08-08, 5.4 on 2025-01-27). This is good news for the architecture: the project is actively maintained, not stale since late 2024. https://pypi.org/project/pywebview/#history
- REFUTED: Claim 10: Public 2026 guides describe current CapCut for Windows as v8.5.0+ (citing windowsmode.com). - WebFetch of https://www.windowsmode.com/capcut-for-windows/ returned no version string; Uptodown shows 9.4.0.4015 (2026-09-05); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/GuanYixuan/pyCapCut/main/README.md cites 9.5.0.4050. Correction: The cited windowsmode.com page (updated 2026-03-24) states no CapCut version number at all. Current evidence: Uptodown lists CapCut for Windows 9.4.0.4015 dated 2026-09-05; the pyCapCut README references 'CapCut 9.5.0.4050' when documenting blend-mode types; capcut-cli's version-support doc reports 10.x builds (Mac and Windows) rejecting tool-written drafts. So the installed base on Windows in Oct 2026 is 9.4-9.5 with 10.x appearing, not '8.5.0+'. The architectural conclusion (verify and pin the installed version) stands, but the version figure and attribution are wrong. https://capcut.en.uptodown.com/windows
- REFUTED: Claim 2 / summary: pyJianYingDraft targets Chinese JianYing with primary support version 5.9 and 'targets only JianYing 5.9'. - README fetched 2026-10-09 explicitly discusses support for version 10.8 and describes which features degrade on newer versions. Correction: Partially stale. The current README says 5.9 is the main tested version but that newer JianYing versions, up to 10.8, are receiving increasing feature support; it notes that in new versions draft_content.json is 'often not directly readable plaintext JSON' (affecting template loading on 10.8+), that auto-export relies on old visible controls that JianYing 7+ no longer provides, and that batch export supports JianYing 6 and below. The library is not limited to 5.9; its export automation is. The summary's 'targets only JianYing 5.9' overstates the limitation. (Irrelevant to the recommended path since the user is on International CapCut, but the architect should not dismiss the library on that basis.) https://raw.githubusercontent.com/GuanYixuan/pyJianYingDraft/main/README.md
- REFUTED: Claim 5: pyCapCut ... no supported CapCut version numbers are stated. - Direct fetch of the README on 2026-10-09 shows the 9.5.0.4050 reference; the GitHub page fetch (https://github.com/GuanYixuan/pyCapCut) shows the same. Correction: The pyCapCut README does state a version: it documents blend modes against 'CapCut 9.5.0.4050'. It still gives no formal compatibility matrix, so the practical advice (pin and fixture-test) is unchanged, but the statement that no version numbers appear is incorrect. https://raw.githubusercontent.com/GuanYixuan/pyCapCut/main/README.md
- MISSING: Kdenlive's current release is 26.08.x, not 26.04 'upcoming': GitHub mirror tags show v26.08.2, v26.08.1, v26.08.0 (plus v26.04.3), and the manual is versioned 26.08 (https://api.github.com/repos/KDE/kdenlive/tags?per_page=6; https://docs.kdenlive.org/en/project_and_asset_management/file_management/project_files.html, fetched 2026-10-09). Pin the .kdenlive export fixture test to 26.08.x. Microsoft Store availability (promised 'soon' in the April 2026 State of Kdenlive post) could not be verified.
- MISSING: OpenShot 4.0 (2026-08-30) ships a native Qt timeline written in Python (src/windows/views/timeline_backend: geometry, paint, qwidget, snap.py) - the only open-source pure-Python NLE timeline widget found. It is GPL-3.0-or-later (openshot-qt), so usable only as design reference unless the product is GPL. Separately, libopenshot is LGPL-3.0 with Python bindings, cross-platform incl. Windows, with Bezier/linear/constant animation curves (https://github.com/OpenShot/libopenshot, 1.6k stars); OpenShot .osp project files are JSON. This gives a second free-Windows-NLE hand-off route (.osp) and a possible LGPL render path. Confidence medium: Python bindings are bundled with the OpenShot installer rather than pip-installable, and spatial keyframe details were not verified in this pass.
- MISSING: Diffusion Studio Core (https://github.com/diffusionstudio/core, MPL-2.0, ~1.2k stars) is a browser video compositing engine on WebCodecs with keyframe animation, TextClip, ImageClip with masks, audio ramps - a missed candidate for the canvas preview/local export layer alongside PixiJS/Fabric. MPL-2.0 is file-level copyleft (usable in a proprietary app if modified files stay MPL). Maintenance recency not verified.
- MISSING: License split in the openvideo ecosystem: the openvideodev/react-video-editor repo (NOASSERTION) says 'Free for individuals, non-profits, and organizations with up to 3 employees. Company License: Required for organizations with more than 3 employees' (https://github.com/openvideodev/react-video-editor), while the @openvideo/timeline and @openvideo/core npm packages are MIT (1.4.0, 2026-08-24, https://registry.npmjs.org/@openvideo/timeline). Use only the MIT npm packages; do not copy UI code from the react-video-editor repo without checking that license. Also note @openvideo/timeline depends on fabric ^6.3.0, so pairing it with Fabric.js 7.4.0 (2026-05-18) for the preview canvas would ship two fabric majors - choose one.
- MISSING: Remotion nuance (https://www.remotion.dev/docs/license/faq): 'Previews in the Remotion Studio or Remotion Player do not count as Renders', so Player-only use does not trigger the $0.01/render or $100/month automation minimum - but any Remotion use (including the Player) in an organization of 4+ people still requires the $25/seat/month Company License. The threshold counts people in the organization, not developers.
- MISSING: WebCodecs detail: VideoEncoder is in Safari since 16.4, but AudioEncoder only since Safari 26 (https://caniuse.com/mdn-api_videoencoder; https://caniuse.com/mdn-api_audioencoder). Irrelevant inside WebView2 (Chromium), but matters if the editor is ever opened in a non-Chromium browser; Firefox has both since 130 on desktop only.
- MISSING: OpenTimelineIO's README states supported Python is '3.9 - 3.12' (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/AcademySoftwareFoundation/OpenTimelineIO/main/README.md), not an open-ended '>3.9'; fine for the Python 3.12 target, but 3.13+ is unverified. The otio-fcp-adapter (FCP7 XML) is actively maintained (pushed 2026-04-08) and otio-aaf-adapter (pushed 2026-10-08) exist, whereas the FCPX XML adapter is stale (2024-06-25); FCP7 XML via otio-fcp-adapter is the realistic OTIO-based route into Premiere <25.6 and Resolve (https://api.github.com/orgs/OpenTimelineIO/repos?per_page=100).
- MISSING: Shotcut (free, GPL, Windows) is a viable third free-NLE hand-off target via MLT XML with Shotcut annotations (claim 27 was confirmed at https://shotcut.org/notes/mltxml-annotations/) but it is absent from the Options Considered list; its Size/Position/Rotate filter is keyframable and MLT XML is simpler than .kdenlive. Worth listing as an alternative to Kdenlive export, since both are MLT-based and a single MLT writer could target both.
- MISSING: DaVinci Resolve free-tier constraints beyond scripting: the product page lists free as 8-bit, up to 60 fps, up to UHD 3840x2160; Studio is $295 and adds 10-bit/120fps/beyond-4K plus AI features (https://www.blackmagicdesign.com/products/davinciresolve, current major version 21). Acceptable for 1080p/4K YouTube output, but note it if 10-bit HDR or 4K60+ is ever required. The cited resolve-otio repo (claim 14) does not actually state native OTIO import from 18.5; that fact is instead corroborated by Remotion's export docs (https://www.remotion.dev/docs/export-opentimeline naming Resolve 18.5+ and Premiere 25.6+).
- MISSING: FFmpeg specifics the renderer must respect (https://ffmpeg.org/ffmpeg-filters.html): xfade requires inputs with the same resolution and frame rate and constant frame rate, so every scene clip must be normalised (scale/pad/fps) before crossfading; zoompan documents z/x/y/d/s/fps options. The well-known zoompan jitter at small zoom steps and the common workaround (pre-upscale the still, e.g. scale=8000:-1, or drive scale+crop per frame with expressions) is practitioner knowledge not sourced in this pass - treat as low-confidence until tested on real images.
- MISSING: OpenCut classic is confirmed archived (archived=true, pushed 2026-05-17, 266 stars, https://api.github.com/repos/OpenCut-app/opencut-classic), so there is no stable 'classic' fallback; OpenCut remains a 2027 watch item only.
- MISSING: The CapCut draft schema gist (claim 9) shows last update 2026-10-04 rather than 2026-10-07 - trivial, but it and capcut-cli v0.28.0 (2026-10-04) move fast; whatever draft writer is built needs a per-CapCut-version fixture test as recommended.

Notes: Method: loaded WebSearch/WebFetch; all GitHub-hosted facts checked against api.github.com (repo metadata, releases, tags, contents), npm facts against registry.npmjs.org, PyPI facts against pypi.org JSON/history, and vendor claims against the cited pages. The WebSearch budget was exhausted by this session before my searches ran, so 'newer 2026 news' sweeps were limited to WebFetch of known URLs (Puget Systems 2026-09-10 article on Resolve 21.1, Blackmagic product page, Uptodown, Kdenlive tags, OpenShot releases).

Fully confirmed (38): claims 1, 3, 4, 6, 7, 8, 9, 11, 12, 14 (fact confirmed via Remotion docs; cited source does not state it), 16, 17 (Apple DocC JSON endpoint; position units are percent of project height), 18, 19, 20, 21, 23 (Transform 'Keyframes: Yes'; Zoom Pan page says 'Keyframes: No' and 'Until this is fixed use the Transform effect'), 24 (accurate as of 2026-04-18 but now stale, see missing), 25 (0 stars, no license, created 2026-08-19; the '124 tools' figure not verified), 26, 27, 28, 29, 30, 31 (5.5.8 has no license field; its 2024-12-10 date not verifiable from registry metadata), 32, 33, 34 (Timeline $300 GitHub-repo delivery, Editor Starter $600 included in Enterprise), 35, 36, 37 (first-publish date not verified), 38, 39, 40, 41, 42, 44, 46 (page ms.date 2024-06-27, updated_at 2026-10-02).

Partially verifiable (3, not counted as confirmed or refuted): claim 13 - the 25.04 C++ OTIO rewrite is confirmed by the State of Kdenlive 2026 post, but 'first added 23.08', '25.04.2 fixed track order' and 'effects/filters/transitions not exported' could not be checked (kdenlive.org pages returned 404/unreadable); claim 15 - Adobe helpx pages return HTTP 403 to WebFetch; Premiere 25.6+ OTIO import is corroborated only by Remotion's docs; claim 22 - the dev-docs structure and kdenlive:* properties are confirmed, but the exact effect keyframe string format and kdenlive_title embedding were not in the fetched excerpt.

Refuted/corrected (5): 43 (material: OpenShot 4.0 moved to a native Qt timeline), 45 (date only), 10 (attribution and version figure), 2 (stale scope), 5 (minor). None of the refutations overturn the core recommendation (own JSON timeline + FFmpeg + embedded MIT web editor + Kdenlive/FCPXML/CapCut escape hatches); the OpenShot finding weakens the 'web timeline inside a desktop shell is the proven pattern' argument and shows a mature Python project chose the opposite direction in 2026, which the architect should weigh when deciding between pywebview+React and a native PySide6 timeline.

## ui: Python-first UI frameworks for a SaaS-grade desktop dashboard (Cashflow Studio) with an embedded custom JS timeline/video-preview and live job progress, plus Windows multi-user distribution and FFmpeg shipping, as of 2026-10-09

### Summary

As of October 2026 the pure-Python UI field has consolidated around three actively shipped frameworks (Reflex 0.10.0, Flet 1.0.x, NiceGUI 3.18) alongside the older data-app tools (Streamlit 1.65, Gradio 6.30, Dash 4.4, Panel 1.9.4). For Cashflow Studio's hard requirements - a polished dark SaaS-style dashboard, an embeddable custom JavaScript timeline with an HTML5 video preview, websocket-pushed progress from long FFmpeg/API jobs, multi-page navigation, and a native Windows window shipped to non-developers without Electron - NiceGUI is the only option that satisfies all of them natively: it is FastAPI + socket.io on the back end, Vue 3.5 + Quasar 2.18 + Tailwind 4 on the front end, custom components are a plain .js/.vue file declared with `class Timeline(ui.element, component='timeline.js', dependencies=[...], esm={...})` with two-way `self.on()` / `run_method()` / `emitEvent` bridging, long jobs run via `run.cpu_bound` / `run.io_bound` and update the browser over the existing socket, `ui.sub_pages` gives SPA routing, `ui.run(native=True)` opens a pywebview/WebView2 window, and `nicegui-pack` wraps PyInstaller; it is MIT-licensed, has 16.3k stars and was pushed on 2026-10-07. Flet 1.0 (Sept 2026) is excluded because its WebView control is unsupported on Windows/Linux and custom controls require writing Dart/Flutter. Reflex is the strongest React-ecosystem alternative (28.9k stars, wraps any npm React component, background events, `rx.script`), but it compiles the frontend with Bun (VC++ redist needed on Windows, WSL recommended), requires Python 3.11+, and has no documented desktop/offline packaging path. Streamlit's new Components v2 (shadow-DOM, no build step) is good, but its rerun model, OIDC-only `st.login`, and Electron-only desktop route (stlite) disqualify it; Gradio and Dash both need a Node/Svelte or Node/React build toolchain for custom components and Dash's production background callbacks need Celery/Redis; Panel's ESM `JSComponent` is excellent and it documents a pywebview desktop recipe, but its Bokeh-based look and 5.8k-star community make a Nexlev-grade UI more work. For distribution, PyInstaller 6.22.3 (GPL with bundling exception) in --onedir mode plus an Inno Setup 6.7.3 installer or Velopack (MIT, Python SDK, delta auto-updates) is the mainstream path; Nuitka 4.2.2 (now AGPLv3 with a runtime exception for compiled output) is the faster-startup alternative; `uv tool install` from a wheel URL is a near-zero-effort channel for power users. WebView2 is preinstalled on Windows 11 and "the vast majority" of Windows 10 devices, with a ~2 MB bootstrapper fallback, so an Electron-free window costs nothing. FFmpeg can be shipped as a BtbN or gyan.dev build dropped into the onedir (`--add-binary`), installed at setup time via `winget install Gyan.FFmpeg` (9.0.2 is in winget-pkgs), or fetched on first run by `static-ffmpeg` 3.0 (FFmpeg 8.0); note that x264/x265 only exist in GPL builds, and the licence of the binaries inside `imageio-ffmpeg`/`static-ffmpeg` is not documented.

### Recommendation

Primary stack: NiceGUI 3.18 (MIT) running as a native pywebview/WebView2 window (`ui.run(native=True, reload=False, storage_secret=...)`), Python 3.12. Build the dashboard shell with Quasar layout components (header/left drawer/pages) themed via `ui.colors`/`ui.dark_mode(None)` plus Tailwind 4 utility classes, and route with `ui.sub_pages` so Research -> Script -> Storyboard -> Voice -> Edit -> Export is one SPA. Implement the CapCut-like editor as ONE custom component: `class Timeline(ui.element, component='timeline.js', dependencies=['vendor/wavesurfer.min.js'], esm={...})` that renders a canvas/DOM multi-track timeline plus an HTML5 `<video>`/`<img>` preview, exposing `run_method('load', edl)` / `run_method('seek', t)` and emitting `change`/`split`/`trim` events to Python; take design cues (not code) from omniclip (MIT) rather than adopting designcombo's React stack. Run FFmpeg/yt-dlp/voice/image jobs with `run.io_bound` (subprocess) and `run.cpu_bound` (Pillow/caption rendering) inside asyncio tasks, parse ffmpeg `-progress pipe:1` and push to `ui.linear_progress`/`ui.log` over the existing socket (no polling). Per-laptop users need no login; if several people share one machine or a shared PC, reuse the official AuthMiddleware + `app.storage.user` pattern. Packaging: `nicegui-pack --onedir --windowed` (PyInstaller 6.22, --onedir avoids onefile startup and AV issues), ship ffmpeg.exe/ffprobe.exe in the onedir from BtbN 'latest' (gpl variant if libx264 is required; lgpl if licensing must stay LGPL), wrap with Inno Setup 6.7 that (a) checks the WebView2 `pv` registry key and runs the ~2 MB Evergreen bootstrapper if absent and (b) optionally runs `winget install --id Gyan.FFmpeg --silent` as the zero-size alternative. Add Velopack (MIT, Python SDK) or tufup for auto-update from GitHub Releases; keep `uv tool install <wheel URL>` as the developer/tester channel. Fallback stack (if the team prefers hand-written web UI or NiceGUI's Vue model becomes limiting): FastAPI + Jinja2 + htmx 4 (hx-sse for progress) + Alpine.js + Tailwind v4/DaisyUI, served to a pywebview 6.2 window, same PyInstaller/Inno/Velopack pipeline; the timeline component is reused unchanged. Choose Reflex only if the team decides to adopt a ready-made React editor (designcombo/OpenVideo is free only for <=3-employee organisations) and accepts the Bun build step, Python 3.11+, and building its own pywebview desktop wrapper. Do not use Flet (no Windows WebView, Dart for custom controls), Streamlit (rerun model, Electron-only desktop, OIDC-only auth), Gradio or Dash (Node/Svelte or Node/React component toolchains, Celery for production background jobs).

### Options

| Option | Pros | Cons | Pricing | API / integration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| NiceGUI 3.18 (FastAPI + socket.io + Vue 3.5/Quasar 2.18/Tailwind 4) in native pywebview window | Meets every hard criterion natively: SaaS-grade Quasar components (tables, drawers, dialogs, AG Grid, ECharts, Plotly) with Tailwind 4 utility styling and `ui.dark_mode`/`ui.colors`; custom JS/Vue component = one .js/.vue file via `component=`/`dependencies=`/`esm=` with `run_method`, `self.on`, `ui.run_javascript`, `emitEvent` (no Node build step); websocket push is built in, `run.cpu_bound`/`run.io_bound`/`ui.timer`/event system handle long jobs; `ui.sub_pages` SPA routing; official auth middleware example with `app.storage.user`; `ui.run(native=True)` opens a WebView2 window and `nicegui-pack` wraps PyInstaller; MIT; 16.3k stars, 60 open issues, pushed 2026-10-07, 3.x releases roughly every 3-4 weeks; backed by Zauberzeug (robotics) with production usage. | Smaller community than Reflex/Streamlit; Vue (not React) so React-only timeline editors (designcombo) would need adaptation or an iframe; Quasar look needs theming effort to escape the Material default; native mode depends on WebView2 + .NET 4.6.2 being present (blank window if missing); `run.cpu_bound` pickles arguments (use free functions); Tailwind Play-CDN approach means the full Tailwind runtime in the page. | Free, MIT. | Python API; FastAPI app underneath (mount extra routes, APIRouter); socket.io; pywebview native window; `nicegui-pack` PyInstaller wrapper. |
| FastAPI + Jinja2 + htmx 4 (SSE/WS extensions) + Alpine.js + Tailwind v4, hosted in a pywebview 6.2 window | Maximum control and zero framework lock-in: any Tailwind/DaisyUI/Flowbite template gives a Nexlev-grade look; the timeline is just a JS module on the page; sse-starlette or websockets stream job progress; auth, multi-page and theming are standard web patterns; pywebview (BSD-3, 6.2.1 Apr 2026) gives the native window and `WEBVIEW2_RUNTIME_PATH` bundling; packages fine with PyInstaller/Nuitka. | You hand-build everything NiceGUI gives for free (component library, state sync, reconnect handling, auth plumbing); more JS/HTML written by the team; htmx 4 (Aug 2026) is a fresh major with changed defaults; no 'pure Python' story for non-frontend developers. | Free (MIT/BSD components); Tailwind free. | FastAPI routes + SSE/WebSocket endpoints; htmx attributes; Alpine for client state; pywebview js_api for native dialogs. |
| Reflex 0.10 (Python -> React frontend compiled with Bun, FastAPI backend, WebSockets) | Largest pure-Python community (28.9k stars, pushed 2026-10-09); wraps any npm React component (`rx.Component` library/tag) so React timeline editors (designcombo/OpenVideo, Twick) can be reused; `rx.script` for arbitrary JS; background events with `yield` progress; 60+ Radix-based components with good default styling and dark mode; `reflex export` yields a static frontend; single-port prod builds since 0.9. | Frontend build needs Bun (VC++ 2015 redist on Windows; docs recommend WSL), Python 3.11+ only; no documented desktop/offline packaging - you would pre-export the frontend, serve it from the backend and wrap in pywebview yourself; heavier runtime (node_modules, compile step); enterprise components (AG Grid etc.) show Reflex branding unless on a paid tier; company focus is Reflex Cloud/AI builder; 396 open issues. | Framework Apache-2.0 free; reflex-enterprise free with branding, paid tier removes it; Reflex Cloud optional. | Python State/event API; React component wrapping; FastAPI backend; `reflex export` static bundle. |
| Flet 1.0.4 (Flutter-rendered UI) | Pixel-identical native rendering on Windows via Flutter; 1.0 declared production-ready (Sept 2026); 150+ controls, Material design, built-in packaging (`flet build windows` or PyInstaller-based `flet pack`); Apache-2.0; 17.3k stars, pushed 2026-10-09. | Disqualifying for this project: WebView control is unsupported on Windows/Linux and custom controls require Dart/Flutter code, so a custom JS timeline/HTML5 video preview cannot be embedded on Windows; `flet build windows` needs Visual Studio C++ workload and Developer Mode on the build machine; not a web stack (no Tailwind/HTML). | Free, Apache-2.0. | Python control tree; Flutter engine; `flet build` / `flet pack`. |
| Streamlit 1.65 (Snowflake) | Huge community (45.9k stars), fast iteration; Components v2 (`st.components.v2.component`, shadow DOM, no build step) finally allows frameless bidirectional custom JS; custom light/dark themes; `st.Page`/`st.navigation` multipage; `st.fragment` partial reruns. | Full-script rerun model fights long-running jobs and rich editors (community reports of state loss on 30-40 min jobs); `st.login` is OIDC-only (needs external IdP, no offline); no native window - desktop only via stlite + Electron; external PRs paused; custom-component return values are rerun-bound, awkward for a stateful timeline. | Free, Apache-2.0. | Script-rerun API; Components v2 JS bridge; OIDC auth. |
| Panel 1.9.4 (HoloViz, Bokeh server) | `JSComponent`/`ReactComponent` ESM components need no Node (import maps from esm.sh), two-way param sync and events; built-in OAuth/basic auth; async callbacks and periodic callbacks; official pywebview desktop how-to and 'distribute as uv tool' guidance; BSD-3; release every ~2 months (1.9.0 May 2026, 1.9.4 Aug 2026). | Bokeh-centric look and smaller community (5.8k stars) make a Nexlev-style UI more custom CSS work; dark mode support is uneven across widgets; Bokeh/Tornado server is heavier and less familiar than FastAPI; fewer SaaS-style layout components. | Free, BSD-3-Clause. | Param-based reactive API; Bokeh server; `pn.serve`; ESM components. |
| Gradio 6.30 | Very active (6.30.0 2026-10-08, 43.7k stars), Apache-2.0; theme engine; `Blocks.route()` multipage; simple `launch(auth=...)`; queue with progress bars for long functions; FastAPI underneath. | Optimised for ML demos, not dashboards; custom components require Node 20+ and the Svelte `gradio cc` build/publish cycle; pages cannot interact with each other; limited layout control for a CapCut-like editor; no native window story. | Free, Apache-2.0. | Blocks API on FastAPI; Svelte custom components. |
| Plotly Dash 4.4 (Flask/FastAPI + React) | MIT, 24.4k stars, monthly 4.x releases in 2026; WebSocket callbacks (4.2), streaming callbacks and shared_storage (4.5rc); multi-backend (Flask/FastAPI/Quart); dark mode via dash-bootstrap-components; `dash.pages` multipage. | Custom components are React packages built from dash-component-boilerplate (Node toolchain); production background callbacks require Celery + Redis (diskcache only for dev); callback graph model is clumsy for a stateful editor; no native window packaging story; much of the polished tooling is Dash Enterprise. | Open source MIT; Dash Enterprise commercial. | Declarative callbacks; React components; background callback managers. |
| Distribution: PyInstaller 6.22 --onedir + Inno Setup 6.7 (or Velopack for auto-update) | Mature, largest hook ecosystem, GPL-with-exception permits closed-source bundles; `nicegui-pack` wraps it; Inno Setup 6.7.3 (May 2026) produces a familiar signed Setup.exe and can run the WebView2 bootstrapper and `winget install Gyan.FFmpeg`; Velopack (MIT, Python SDK, delta updates, `vpk pack` on the onedir) adds Squirrel-style auto-updates; tufup 0.10 (MIT, TUF-secured) is the Python-native alternative. | --onefile has ~2 s extraction startup and antivirus false positives; bytecode is decompilable; Velopack Python SDK is young (dev builds on PyPI); tufup has a single maintainer. | All free/open source. | CLI build pipeline; Velopack UpdateManager / tufup client in-app. |
| Distribution: Nuitka 4.2 standalone/onefile | Compiled C output, ~0.08 s startup vs ~1.8 s PyInstaller onefile, some IP protection; Windows onefile fixes and VS 2026 support in 4.0 (Apr 2026); compiled binaries exempt from AGPL via runtime exception. | Long compile times, MSVC/MinGW/Zig toolchain on the build machine, more package-config edge cases; Nuitka itself is AGPLv3 (fine to use, but note for CI vendors); obfuscation features need Nuitka Commercial (EUR 250/yr). | Free (AGPLv3 + runtime exception); Commercial EUR 250/yr. | `nuitka --standalone --onefile --windows-console-mode=disable`. |
| Distribution: uv tool install from a wheel URL (power users / internal testers) | One PowerShell line installs uv (no Python needed), then `uv tool install https://.../cashflow_studio-x.y.whl` creates an isolated env, manages Python and puts the launcher on PATH; `uv tool upgrade` is the update story; zero packaging work; Panel docs explicitly recommend this route. | Not friendly for non-developers (terminal, PATH, no Start-menu icon unless you add a shortcut), antivirus/IT policy may block scripts; Python runtime download on first install; no code protection. | Free. | uv CLI; GitHub Releases as wheel host. |
| Distribution: Briefcase 0.4 / PyApp / pytauri | Briefcase builds signed MSI with .NET detection and ARM64 support (2026); PyApp gives a tiny self-bootstrapping binary with self-update; pytauri uses Tauri's WebView2 host without Electron and `pytauri-wheel` avoids Rust. | Briefcase is best for mobile/Toga and adds friction for a web-UI app; PyApp's last release is Oct 2025 and needs internet on first run; pytauri is small (1.4k stars) and adds a Rust/Tauri mental model. | Free. | CLI build tools. |
| FFmpeg shipping: BtbN/gyan.dev build in the onedir, winget Gyan.FFmpeg at install, or static-ffmpeg/imageio-ffmpeg from Python | BtbN autobuilds offer explicit lgpl vs gpl variants with a stable 'latest' URL; gyan.dev essentials is ~34 MB and is the winget-packaged build (9.0.2 in winget-pkgs) so the installer can run `winget install --id Gyan.FFmpeg --silent`; static-ffmpeg 3.0 downloads FFmpeg 8.0 on first run with one Python call; imageio-ffmpeg bundles ffmpeg 7.1 inside a pip wheel for fully offline installs. | x264/x265 encoders exist only in GPL builds (LGPL builds lose libx264 H.264 encoding); binary licences of static-ffmpeg and imageio-ffmpeg are undocumented; imageio-ffmpeg wheels are ~60 MiB and need a PyInstaller spec fix; winget requires Windows 10 1809+ and may prompt for elevation. | Free; FFmpeg LGPL/GPL. | subprocess ffmpeg/ffprobe; `static_ffmpeg.run.get_or_fetch_platform_executables_else_raise()`; `imageio_ffmpeg.get_ffmpeg_exe()`. |

### Facts

- (high, 2026-10-07) NiceGUI v3.18.0 was published on 2026-10-07 (GitHub API published_at 2026-10-07T13:34:47Z); highlights include a security fix for ui.upload, new ui.codemirror signals, and fixes for sub_pages navigation with FastAPI 0.141+. - https://api.github.com/repos/zauberzeug/nicegui/releases/latest
- (high, 2026-10-09) NiceGUI repo metrics via GitHub API: 16,274 stars, 958 forks, 60 open issues, last push 2026-10-07T13:29:51Z, license MIT, created 2021-05. - https://api.github.com/repos/zauberzeug/nicegui
- (high, 2026-10-09) NiceGUI 3.18.0 on PyPI requires Python >=3.10,<4 and is MIT licensed. - https://pypi.org/pypi/nicegui/json
- (high, 2025-10-03) NiceGUI v3.0.0 was published 2025-10-03 (GitHub API). It upgraded to Tailwind CSS 4, Vue 3.5.22 and Quasar 2.18.5, added script mode, a `root` parameter for ui.run, an Event system for long-lived objects, observable props/classes/style, dropped Python 3.8, and listed 13 breaking changes (shared auto-index page removed, table.rows pattern, ui.html sanitisation, etc.). - https://github.com/zauberzeug/nicegui/releases/tag/v3.0.0
- (high, 2025-10-27) NiceGUI is built on FastAPI + Uvicorn (backend), Vue.js + Quasar (frontend), socket.io for real-time client-server communication, and Tailwind CSS; maintained by Zauberzeug, a German robotics company that uses it in production. - https://talkpython.fm/episodes/show/525/nicegui-goes-3.0
- (high, 2026-10-09) NiceGUI custom components: `ui.element.__init_subclass__(cls, *, component: str|Path|None, dependencies: list[str|Path], esm: dict[str,str]|None, default_classes, default_style, default_props)`; `component` points to a .js/.vue file registered via register_vue_component(), `dependencies` are JS libraries registered with register_library() and exposed to the component, and `esm` supports ES-module imports. Python->JS: `run_method(name, *args, timeout=1) -> AwaitableResponse`; JS->Python: `on(type, handler, args=None, *, throttle=0.0, js_handler='(...args) => emit(...args)')`. - https://raw.githubusercontent.com/zauberzeug/nicegui/main/nicegui/element.py
- (high, 2026-10-09) NiceGUI official custom_vue_component example: `class Counter(ui.element, component='counter.js')`, props via `self._props['title'] = title`, Vue events received with `self.on('change', on_change)`, Python calls Vue methods with `self.run_method('reset')`; the JS side is a plain Vue options object using `this.$emit('change', value)`. Hot reload of .js/.vue via `ui.run(uvicorn_reload_includes='*.py,*.js,*.vue')`. - https://github.com/zauberzeug/nicegui/blob/main/examples/custom_vue_component/counter.py
- (high, 2026-10-09) NiceGUI JS<->Python bridge: `ui.run_javascript(code, timeout=1.0)` returns the JS result when awaited (supports `return await new Promise(...)`), and arbitrary browser JS can call `emitEvent('name', data)` which Python receives with `ui.on('name', handler)`; `.on(..., js_handler=...)` runs handlers purely client-side; events support throttle= and key modifiers. - https://nicegui.io/documentation/generic_events
- (high, 2026-10-09) NiceGUI long-running work: `run.cpu_bound()` executes in a separate process (state is pickled; prefer free functions; set `run.process_pool_start_method='spawn'`), `run.io_bound()` runs in a thread; `ui.timer(interval, callback)`, async event handlers, `@ui.refreshable`, `app.on_startup`/`app.on_connect`/`app.on_exception`; UI updates are pushed to the browser automatically over the websocket. - https://nicegui.io/documentation/section_action_events
- (high, 2026-10-09) NiceGUI `ui.sub_pages({'/': main, '/other': other})` provides SPA routing without full page reloads, parameterised routes like '/item/{item_id}', nested sub_pages, `ui.navigate.to()`, and works with the `ui.run(root)` entry point. - https://nicegui.io/documentation/sub_pages
- (medium, 2026-10-09) NiceGUI theming: `ui.dark_mode()` (None = follow OS), `ui.colors(primary=..., secondary=..., dark=..., dark_page=...)`, Quasar props via `.props()`, Tailwind utility classes via `.classes()`, `.style()`, `ui.add_css()`, `ui.add_head_html()`; Quasar `!important` classes were moved to a lower CSS layer so Tailwind can override. The styling page still links the Tailwind v3 Play CDN docs even though 3.0 moved to Tailwind 4 (possible doc lag). - https://nicegui.io/documentation/section_styling_appearance
- (high, 2026-10-09) NiceGUI ships ui.table (Quasar), ui.aggrid (AG Grid community/enterprise), ui.echart (Apache ECharts), ui.plotly, ui.matplotlib/pyplot, ui.altair and ui.anywidget (anywidget integration), ui.leaflet, ui.scene (three.js), ui.tree, ui.log, ui.editor, ui.json_editor, ui.linear_progress/circular_progress/spinner; Highcharts is excluded from the core package for licence reasons (`pip install nicegui[highcharts]`). - https://nicegui.io/documentation/section_data_elements
- (high, 2026-10-09) NiceGUI native mode: `ui.run(native=True, window_size=(1024,786), frameless=..., fullscreen=...)`, `app.native.window_args` passes pywebview parameters, `app.native.main_window` exposes the window; native mode auto-picks a free port. PyInstaller packaging uses the `nicegui-pack --onefile|--onedir --windowed --name myapp main.py` wrapper; requirements: `reload=False`, a root/@ui.page function, and `freeze_support()` first in the main guard when native. - https://nicegui.io/documentation/section_configuration_deployment
- (high, 2026-10-09) NiceGUI storage/auth: `storage_secret` enables `app.storage.user` (server-side dict keyed by a browser session cookie) and `app.storage.browser`; storage path via NICEGUI_STORAGE_PATH; reconnect_timeout default 3.0 s. The official authentication example implements a Starlette `AuthMiddleware` that lets through `/login`, `/favicon.ico` and `/_nicegui*` and otherwise redirects unless `app.storage.user.get('authenticated')`; login sets `app.storage.user.update(username=..., authenticated=True)`, logout calls `app.storage.user.clear()`; the example notes production should use OAuth2/Authlib. - https://github.com/zauberzeug/nicegui/blob/main/examples/authentication/main.py
- (medium, 2025-01-01) Known NiceGUI native-mode pitfalls on Windows: blank window when the WebView2 runtime is missing; a multiprocessing 'SemLock created in a fork context' error combination with pywebview; PyInstaller+native support was fixed in 1.2.1 (older issues, may be outdated). - https://github.com/zauberzeug/nicegui/issues/3815
- (high, 2026-10-09) Reflex 0.10.0 was released on PyPI 2026-10-08 (0.9.13 on 2026-10-09, 0.9.12 on 2026-09-22); requires Python >=3.11,<4.0; Apache-2.0. - https://pypi.org/project/reflex/#history
- (high, 2026-10-09) Reflex GitHub API: 28,940 stars, 1,791 forks, 396 open issues, pushed 2026-10-09T06:48:05Z, Apache-2.0. - https://api.github.com/repos/reflex-dev/reflex
- (high, 2026-10-09) Reflex compiles Python to a React frontend and a FastAPI backend communicating over WebSockets; 60+ built-in components based on Radix UI; it can wrap npm React components via `class X(rx.Component): library='pkg@ver'; tag='Name'; lib_dependencies=[...]`, with `NoSSRComponent` and `on_change: rx.EventHandler[...]` for events; it warns against wrapping libraries that do not expose React components. - https://reflex.dev/docs/wrapping-react/overview/
- (high, 2026-10-09) Reflex `rx.script("inline js")` / `rx.script(src='/my-custom.js')` injects arbitrary JavaScript via next/script (afterInteractive by default) and can be conditionally rendered from state; `add_custom_code()` and `add_hooks()` add page-level JS and React hooks to wrapped components. - https://reflex.dev/docs/library/other/script/
- (high, 2026-10-09) Reflex background tasks use `@rx.event(background=True)` on async State methods; state may only be mutated inside `async with self:`; progress is pushed to the UI by `yield`ing inside that block; multiple instances can run concurrently and the developer must guard against duplicates; tasks are tracked in `app.background_tasks`. - https://reflex.dev/docs/events/background-events/
- (high, 2026-10-09) Reflex self-hosting: `reflex run --env prod` compiles an optimised static frontend and serves it with the backend on one port (Uvicorn or Granian); `reflex export` yields frontend.zip (static HTML/JS/CSS) and backend.zip; production does not need Node/Bun once built; the docs do not address desktop/offline deployment. - https://reflex.dev/docs/hosting/self-hosting/
- (high, 2026-10-09) Reflex installation: Python 3.11+ required; on Windows WSL is recommended 'for optimal performance'; Bun (used to build the frontend) requires the Microsoft Visual C++ 2015 Redistributable on native Windows. - https://reflex.dev/docs/getting-started/installation/
- (medium, 2026-04-30) Reflex 0.9.0 (migration guide dated 2026-04-30) introduced an optional database extra, single-port production builds, an auto-setter change and removed APIs; 2026 company focus includes Reflex Build (AI app builder), Reflex Cloud BYOC, security scanning, and the Rust-based 'xy' charting library. - https://reflex.dev/blog/
- (medium, 2026-10-09) reflex-enterprise (AG Grid, AG Charts, maps, drag-and-drop) is free to use but shows Reflex branding unless on a paid tier; AG Grid Enterprise features additionally need an AG Grid licence key (AG Grid Enterprise is $999 per developer licence). - https://reflex.dev/docs/enterprise/overview
- (high, 2026-10-08) Flet 1.0.0 was published on PyPI 2026-09-14 (blog post 'Flet 1.0' dated 2026-09-15); latest is 1.0.4 (2026-10-08); Python >=3.10; Apache-2.0; the 1.0 post cites 16,700+ GitHub stars and 9M PyPI downloads and says 1.0 'means it's ready for building production apps'. - https://pypi.org/project/flet/#history
- (high, 2026-10-09) Flet GitHub API: 17,297 stars, 720 forks, 282 open issues, pushed 2026-10-09; Apache-2.0; built on Flutter. - https://api.github.com/repos/flet-dev/flet
- (high, 2026-10-09) Flet's WebView control is 'Supported only on the following platforms: iOS, Android, macOS, and Web' - NOT Windows or Linux desktop; it offers run_javascript() but no built-in two-way messaging bridge. This blocks embedding a custom JS timeline in a Flet Windows desktop app. - https://flet.dev/docs/controls/webview
- (high, 2026-10-09) Building a custom Flet control/extension requires writing Dart/Flutter code with a configured Flutter SDK (`flet create --template extension`, `flet build`), i.e. no plain-JS component path. - https://flet.dev/docs/extend/user-extensions
- (high, 2026-10-09) `flet build windows` requires Visual Studio 2022/2026 with the 'Desktop development with C++' workload and Windows Developer Mode enabled on the build machine; `flet pack` is a PyInstaller-based alternative that needs no Visual Studio or Flutter toolchain. - https://flet.dev/docs/publish/windows
- (high, 2026-10-02) Streamlit 1.65.0 was released 2026-10-02 (1.64.0 on 2026-09-15); Python >=3.10; Apache-2.0. 1.65 added side-drawer dialogs, `required` inputs, query-param binding for expanders/tabs and `theme.dataframeHeaderTextColor`. - https://docs.streamlit.io/develop/quick-reference/release-notes
- (high, 2026-10-09) Streamlit GitHub: 45.9k stars, 4.4k forks, Apache-2.0; the README states that pull requests from external contributors are currently paused. - https://github.com/streamlit/streamlit
- (medium, 2026-10-09) Streamlit Custom Components v2 was announced in 1.51.0 (2025-10-31) as 'frameless custom UI with bidirectional data flow'; API `st.components.v2.component(name, *, html=None, css=None, js=None, isolate_styles=True)` mounts in a shadow DOM (no iframe, no build step), JS receives `component.parentElement/data/setStateValue/setTriggerValue`, triggers invoke Python callbacks while state persists in the ComponentResult; content is not sanitised.
