The API reference page states 'Requires Streamlit v1.65.0 or later' (API may have changed since 1.51). - https://docs.streamlit.io/develop/api-reference/custom-components/st.components.v2.component
- (high, 2026-10-09) Streamlit `st.login()`/`st.logout()` require an external OpenID Connect provider (Google, Entra ID, Okta, Auth0...) configured in secrets.toml [auth] (redirect_uri, cookie_secret, client_id, client_secret, server_metadata_url); it cannot work offline without an IdP. - https://docs.streamlit.io/develop/concepts/connections/authentication
- (medium, 2026-04-01) Streamlit's full-script rerun model is a recurring 2026 pain point for long-running jobs: community threads report 30-40 minute jobs losing state on auto-reload and `st.fragment` blocking other reruns; the Reflex blog (April 2026, competitor source) calls fragments 'workarounds layered on a rerun-first architecture'. - https://discuss.streamlit.io/t/streamlit-rerun-after-40-minutes/111883
- (high, 2026-10-09) Streamlit desktop packaging goes through @stlite/desktop (Pyodide/Wasm Streamlit bundled with Electron and electron-builder) - i.e. not Electron-free. - https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@stlite/desktop@0.98.1/README.md
- (high, 2026-10-08) Gradio 6.30.0 released 2026-10-08 (6.29.1 2026-10-02, 6.26.0 2026-08-24); Python >=3.10; Apache-2.0; GitHub 43.7k stars; stack is FastAPI + Svelte + Vite + Tailwind. - https://pypi.org/project/gradio/#history
- (high, 2026-10-09) Gradio custom components require Python 3.10+, Node.js 20+, npm 9+ and the `gradio cc create/dev/build/publish` workflow (Svelte-based); a build step is mandatory to produce an installable wheel. - https://www.gradio.app/guides/custom-components-in-five-minutes
- (high, 2026-10-09) Gradio multipage apps use `demo.route('Page', '/url')` on Blocks with an auto navbar; all pages share one backend/queue but 'multipage apps do not support interactions between pages'. - https://www.gradio.app/guides/multipage-apps
- (high, 2026-09-21) Dash 4.0.0 was released 2026-02-03; 4.2.0 (2026-06-01) added WebSocket callbacks and FastAPI/Quart backends alongside Flask; 4.4.1 (2026-07-21) is the latest stable and fixed background-callback security issues; 4.5.0rc0 (2026-09-21) adds streaming (async generator) callbacks, `dash.ctx.shared_storage`, partial pattern matching and experimental React 19. MIT licence; Python >=3.9. - https://github.com/plotly/dash/releases
- (high, 2026-10-09) Dash GitHub: 24.4k stars, 2.3k forks, MIT; built on Plotly.js, React and Flask. - https://github.com/plotly/dash
- (high, 2026-10-09) Dash background callbacks need a manager: DiskcacheManager (`pip install dash[diskcache]`, 'not recommended for production') or CeleryManager with Redis (`dash[celery]`, recommended for production); decorator supports `background=True`, `running=`, `progress=`/`set_progress`, `cancel=`. - https://dash.plotly.com/background-callbacks
- (high, 2026-10-09) Custom Dash components are React components built from the dash-component-boilerplate (Node/React toolchain); props must be JSON-serialisable and Python classes are auto-generated via react-docgen; Dash 3+ 'hooks' plugins are an alternative extension path. - https://dash.plotly.com/plugins
- (medium, 2024-01-01) Dash dark mode is typically done with dash-bootstrap-components >=1.5 `data-bs-theme` plus dash-bootstrap-templates figure templates (community guidance, not core). - https://community.plotly.com/t/light-and-dark-color-modes-with-dash-bootstrap-components-1-5-0/78810
- (high, 2026-08-17) Panel v1.9.4 was published 2026-08-17 (GitHub API) and v1.9.0 on 2026-05-21; 1.9.x focused on ReactiveESM/React component robustness, OAuth hardening, Param 2.4 typing, wildcard routes; BSD-3-Clause; Python >=3.10; GitHub 5.8k stars / 638 forks. - https://api.github.com/repos/holoviz/panel/releases/latest
- (high, 2026-10-09) Panel `JSComponent`/`ReactComponent`/`AnyWidgetComponent` (panel.custom) take an `_esm` string or file exporting `render({model, el})`, load CDN modules through `_importmap`, sync parameters two-way via `model.on(...)`/`model.send_event(...)`, and require no Node or bundler (hot reload with --dev). - https://panel.holoviz.org/reference/custom_components/JSComponent.html
- (high, 2026-10-09) Panel officially documents a desktop recipe: run `pn.serve(..., show=False)` in a daemon thread, open `webview.create_window('http://localhost:{port}')` + `webview.start()`, package with PyInstaller `--noconsole --icon` on Windows (py2app on macOS), or 'distribute the app as a uv tool'. - https://panel.holoviz.org/how_to/desktop_or_mobile/pywebview.html
- (medium, 2026-08-28) htmx 4.0.0 was released 2026-08-28 with rebuilt SSE (`hx-sse:connect`) and WebSocket (`hx-ws:connect`) extensions, opt-in attribute inheritance and new extensions (hx-multipart, hx-live, hx-download, ...); Datastar is an SSE-first alternative that merges htmx+Alpine roles. - https://four.htmx.org/announcements/2026-08-28-htmx-4.0.0-is-released
- (medium, 2026-05-15) FastAPI + HTMX + Alpine.js + Tailwind dashboards with live updates typically use sse-starlette for Server-Sent Events and htmx's sse extension; starter templates exist (e.g. Achronus/fastapi-quickstart with Jinja2/Tailwind/Flowbite/HTMX/Alpine; volfpeter/fastapi-htmx-tailwind-example IoT dashboard with DaisyUI). - https://github.com/volfpeter/fastapi-htmx-tailwind-example
- (medium, 2026-10-09) FastHTML (AnswerDotAI) is a Python-first HTMX framework on Starlette/Uvicorn, 7.1k stars, Apache-2.0, 1,648 commits; release cadence not verified in this research. - https://github.com/AnswerDotAI/fasthtml
- (high, 2026-04-15) pywebview 6.2.1 was released 2026-04-15 (6.2 on 2026-04-13: DPI-aware Screen, min Python 3.8, WebView2 1.0.3856.49, CoreCLR fallback; 6.1 on 2025-10-21 added `webview.settings['WEBVIEW2_RUNTIME_PATH']` to bundle a WebView2 runtime). BSD-3-Clause, 6.1k stars; two-way JS<->Python API, DOM support, native dialogs. - https://pywebview.flowrl.com/changelog.html
- (high, 2026-10-09) pywebview on Windows defaults to EdgeChromium (WebView2), requiring .NET Framework 4.6.2 and the Edge/WebView2 Runtime; MSHTML (IE11) is a deprecated fallback; CEF is optional; select with `webview.start(gui=...)` or PYWEBVIEW_GUI. PyInstaller is the recommended freezer on Windows/Linux (`pyinstaller main.py --add-data index.html:. --onefile`), Nuitka also works. - https://pywebview.flowrl.com/guide/web_engine.html
- (high, 2026-10-02) WebView2 Evergreen Runtime is part of Windows 11 and 'the vast majority of Windows 10 devices have the WebView2 Runtime installed already'; the Bootstrapper is ~2 MB and downloads the matching runtime (`MicrosoftEdgeWebview2Setup.exe /silent /install`); a Standalone Installer exists for offline; Fixed Version binaries are >250 MB; presence is detected via the EdgeUpdate\Clients\{F3017226-...} `pv` registry key. - https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-edge/webview2/concepts/distribution
- (high, 2026-09-12) PyInstaller v6.22.3 was published 2026-09-12; 6.21.0 (2026-06-13) supports Python 3.8-3.15; licence is GPL 2.0 with a special exception that allows bundling commercial/closed-source applications with no attribution obligation (modifications to PyInstaller itself stay GPL). - https://api.github.com/repos/pyinstaller/pyinstaller/releases/latest
- (high, 2026-09-22) Nuitka 4.2.2 was released 2026-09-22 (4.0 on 2026-04-22 with --project/pyproject support, Zig fallback compiler, Windows onefile fixes, VS 2026 support; 3.14 support 'not yet ready' at 4.0). Nuitka is now licensed AGPLv3+ 'with the runtime exception granted in LICENSE-RUNTIME.txt' so compiled binaries are not bound by AGPL; Nuitka Commercial costs EUR 250/yr (obfuscation, encrypted tracebacks). - https://pypi.org/project/Nuitka/
- (medium, 2026-01-01) Nuitka vs PyInstaller (2026 comparisons): PyInstaller bundles bytecode (decompilable, largest hook ecosystem, --onefile extraction ~1.8 s startup) while Nuitka compiles to C with ~0.08 s startup and faster execution; for desktop-only apps PyInstaller/Nuitka/cx_Freeze beat Briefcase. - https://blog.thoughtparameters.com/post/nuitka_vs_pyinstaller_python_packaging/
- (medium, 2026-03-31) Briefcase 0.4.0 shipped March 2026 (isolated dev-mode dependencies); 2026 Windows work includes MSI privilege fix CVE-2026-33430, ARM64 Windows installers, .NET detection and custom MSI banners; BeeWare recommends Briefcase mainly when mobile targets are needed. - https://beeware.org/news/buzz/2026/march-2026-status-update/
- (high, 2026-10-09) uv standalone Windows installer: `powershell -ExecutionPolicy ByPass -c "irm https://astral.sh/uv/install.ps1 | iex"`; `uv self update`; `uv tool install <pkg>` creates an isolated env and puts the console script on PATH (`uv tool upgrade`, `--python` to pin interpreter, `uvx` for ephemeral runs); tools can be installed from PyPI, git URLs, or direct `https://` URLs to a .whl/.tar.gz. - https://docs.astral.sh/uv/getting-started/installation/
- (medium, 2026-01-01) uv tool vs pipx (2026): both isolate a tool in its own venv; `uv tool install` is several times faster, adds managed Python, lockfiles and PEP 723 scripts; 'new users can skip pipx and just use uv'. - https://pydevtools.com/handbook/explanation/how-do-uv-tool-and-pipx-compare/
- (high, 2025-10-15) PyApp (ofek) wraps a Python app in a Rust binary that bootstraps python-build-standalone at first run (or embeds it), installs via pip/uv, and offers self-update commands; latest release v0.29.0 on 2025-10-15 - no 2026 release observed. - https://api.github.com/repos/ofek/pyapp/releases/latest
- (high, 2025-10-04) tufup 0.10.0 (MIT, Python >=3.9) was released 2025-10-04; it provides TUF-secured archive+patch updates for stand-alone (e.g. PyInstaller) Python apps, focused on Windows/macOS, and replaces the archived PyUpdater. - https://pypi.org/pypi/tufup/json
- (medium, 2026-02-24) Velopack is an MIT-licensed installer + auto-update framework (2.4k stars) with delta updates and a Python SDK: call `velopack.App().run()` at entry, use `UpdateManager` to check/download/apply, build with PyInstaller `--onedir` then `vpk pack --packId ... --packVersion ... --packDir ... --mainExe ...`; PyPI package `velopack` 1.2.161 (dev builds uploaded 2026-02). - https://docs.velopack.io/getting-started/python
- (medium, 2026-05-26) Inno Setup 6.7.3 was released 2026-05-26 alongside Inno Setup 7.0.1 beta (64-bit and 32-bit editions, long-path support). - https://jrsoftware.org/isdl.php
- (medium, 2026-10-09) pytauri (Tauri bindings for Python via PyO3) is Apache-2.0 with 1.4k stars; `pytauri-wheel` removes the Rust compiler requirement; packaging via tauri-cli; uses the system WebView2 on Windows - an Electron-free alternative but a smaller ecosystem. - https://github.com/pytauri/pytauri
- (medium, 2025-01-01) FFmpeg licensing: core libraries are LGPL v2.1+; GPL parts (libx264, libx265, etc.) are only enabled with --enable-gpl, making the resulting binary GPL; distributing a closed-source app that uses an LGPL build requires LGPL compliance (dynamic linking / separate binary). - https://wip.co/posts/ffmpeg-license-can-we-legally-sell-closed-source-apps-that-use-it-ap32zk
- (high, 2026-10-09) BtbN/FFmpeg-Builds publishes gpl, lgpl, nonfree (+ -shared) variants for win64/win32/linux64/linuxarm64 daily at 12:00 UTC with a stable 'latest' tag; the lgpl variant excludes libx264 and libx265; repository scripts are MIT but bundled libraries carry GPL/LGPL licences. - https://github.com/BtbN/FFmpeg-Builds
- (high, 2026-09-19) gyan.dev Windows builds: release 9.0.2 dated 2026-09-19 (previous 8.1.2); 'essentials' is 34 MB (.7z) / 109 MB (.zip), 'full' adds most remaining libraries; standard builds are GPLv3 (an LGPLv3 shared variant exists only for release-full). - https://www.gyan.dev/ffmpeg/builds/
- (high, 2026-10-09) The winget package Gyan.FFmpeg exists in microsoft/winget-pkgs with versions up to 9.0.2 (plus Essentials and Shared variants); winget ships with App Installer on Windows 11 and Windows 10 version 1809+ and supports silent installs. - https://github.com/microsoft/winget-pkgs/tree/master/manifests/g/Gyan/FFmpeg
- (high, 2026-01-16) static-ffmpeg 3.0 (PyPI, 2026-01-16, MIT wrapper) downloads ffmpeg+ffprobe 8.0 'with all plugins and codecs' on first run from the zackees/ffmpeg_bins repo for win32/darwin/darwin_arm64/linux/linux_arm64; API `run.get_or_fetch_platform_executables_else_raise()` and `static_ffmpeg.add_paths()`; the licence of the binaries is not stated. - https://pypi.org/project/static-ffmpeg/
- (medium, 2025-01-16) imageio-ffmpeg 0.6.0 (2025-01-16, BSD-2 wrapper, Python >=3.9) ships platform wheels containing an ffmpeg binary (~60 MiB; Windows x86-64 wheel 31.2 MB; imageio-binaries holds ffmpeg-win-x86_64-v7.1.exe); `get_ffmpeg_exe()` honours IMAGEIO_FFMPEG_EXE; ffprobe binaries in the repo are only v4.1; binary licence/build source is not documented; PyInstaller needs an explicit path fix for imageio_ffmpeg.binaries (issue #8664). - https://github.com/imageio/imageio-binaries/tree/master/ffmpeg
- (high, 2024-01-01) Bundling ffmpeg.exe with PyInstaller is done with `--add-binary "ffmpeg.exe;."` on Windows (or by placing it in the --onedir output). - https://gist.github.com/Shreyaan/84458e718f41e51407a1effd2e93efdb
- (high, 2026-03-05) designcombo/react-video-editor (OpenVideo) is a client-side CapCut/Canva-style editor: Next.js 15, PixiJS v8, @openvideo/core + @openvideo/timeline, Zustand, Tailwind v4, Radix; multi-track drag/split/trim/snap timeline; 1.8k stars; dual-licensed - free for individuals, non-profits and organisations with up to 3 employees, commercial licence otherwise (updated 2026-03-05). - https://github.com/designcombo/react-video-editor
- (high, 2026-10-09) omniclip is an MIT-licensed browser video editor (1.5k stars) built with TypeScript web components (<omni-timeline>, <omni-media>) and WebCodecs, fully client-side; 'Omniclip 2.0 is in development'. - https://github.com/omni-media/omniclip
- (medium, 2026-10-09) Twick is a React SDK for timeline-based video editors (@twick/timeline, @twick/canvas on Fabric.js, @twick/studio; WebCodecs + FFmpeg.wasm), 537 stars, under the Sustainable Use License v1.0 (free to use/self-host/modify, not resellable as an SDK). - https://github.com/ncounterspecialist/twick
- (medium, 2026-10-09) wavesurfer.js v7 (7.10.x) is a TypeScript rewrite with official Regions and Timeline plugins (draggable/resizable regions, time ruler) suitable for an audio/caption track in a custom timeline. - https://wavesurfer.xyz/docs/plugins/
- (low, 2026-06-01) Comparative 2026 star counts cited by third parties: Reflex ~28.7k, Flet ~16.4k, NiceGUI ~16.1k; 'Pick NiceGUI for quick tools, Reflex for production web apps' is a common framing. - https://www.bitdoze.com/best-python-web-frameworks/

### Open questions

- Does the BtbN LGPL build include any H.264 encoder (e.g. openh264 or hardware h264_nvenc/qsv/amf) usable for YouTube MP4 export, or must the GPL build with libx264 be shipped? If GPL ffmpeg.exe is distributed inside a proprietary installer, get a licensing opinion (subprocess invocation is common practice but not formally settled).
- What licence do the binaries inside static-ffmpeg 3.0 and imageio-ffmpeg 0.6.0 carry (neither project documents it)?
- Measured size and cold-start time of a `nicegui-pack --onedir` build with WebView2 on a low-end Windows 10 laptop (not benchmarked in this research).
- Do the target laptops all have the WebView2 Evergreen runtime and .NET Framework 4.6.2? Confirm per machine or always ship the 2 MB bootstrapper in the installer.
- Does uv require a system git for `uv tool install git+https://...` on a clean Windows machine? (Unverified - use wheel URLs from GitHub Releases to sidestep.)
- Streamlit's API page says Components v2 'requires 1.65.0 or later' while it was announced in 1.51.0 - the API may have changed between Oct 2025 and Oct 2026; irrelevant if NiceGUI is chosen.
- When exactly did Nuitka switch from Apache-2.0 to AGPLv3 + runtime exception (observed on 4.2.2, Sept 2026), and does that matter for any CI/build-service terms?
- Breaking changes in Reflex 0.10.0 (released 2026-10-08) were not reviewed.
- Is 'multi-user authentication' meant as one login per laptop (no auth needed) or a shared machine/LAN server with several accounts? This decides whether the AuthMiddleware pattern and `storage_secret` are needed from day one.
- Velopack's Python SDK maturity on PyPI (stable 1.2.161 vs frequent .dev uploads) should be validated with a test release before committing to it over tufup or plain Inno Setup re-installs.
- FastHTML release cadence in 2026 could not be verified from PyPI JSON (fetch parse failed); only relevant to the fallback stack.

## ytdlp: yt-dlp-based competitor research for Cashflow Studio (Oct 2026): channel listing without downloads, YouTube anti-bot landscape, transcripts, thumbnails, fallbacks, and an outlier-score design

### Summary

As of 2026-10-09, yt-dlp 2026.08.19 (verified locally on this Windows 10 machine from a residential IP) lists a whole channel tab without downloading anything: `--flat-playlist -J` on `/@handle/videos` returned all 450 Veritasium videos in 12.2 s (about 15 continuation requests of 30 items) with id, title, url, duration, a ROUNDED view_count (1,200,000 where the exact value is 1,246,453), one hqdefault 480x270 thumbnail, plus channel-level channel_follower_count, channel_id and playlist_count; dates are absent unless `--extractor-args youtubetab:approximate_date` is passed, which yields a day-granularity `timestamp` parsed from "2 weeks ago" text. The `/shorts` tab gives id, title, url, view_count and thumbnails but no duration, and a bare channel URL returns a playlist of tab-playlists (Videos, Shorts). Exact numbers (view_count, like_count, comment_count, upload_date, 41 tags, chapters, 157 auto-caption languages in vtt/json3/srv1-3/ttml/srt, the full thumbnail ladder up to maxresdefault) come from a per-video `-J --skip-download` call that took about 3 s (2 s with `youtube:player_skip=js;skip=hls,dash`), and these worked WITHOUT a JavaScript runtime, which only produced a deprecation warning about missing formats. The 2025-2026 anti-bot picture is: YouTube requires PO tokens for web-client streams and subtitles (wiki revised 2026-07-12), yt-dlp has required an external JS runtime (Deno recommended, bundled EJS scripts, announced 2025-09-23) for the n/sig challenge, guest sessions are rate-limited to roughly 300 videos per hour (about 1000 webpage/player requests), datacenter/VPN IPs get "Sign in to confirm you're not a bot", and Chrome's app-bound encryption has broken `--cookies-from-browser chrome` on Windows since 2024; mitigations are residential IPs, the `-t sleep` preset, Firefox or the "Get cookies.txt LOCALLY" extension for a private-window cookie export, and the bgutil PO-token provider (2.0.2, 2026-10-07, needs Node 22+/Deno 2+) as a fallback. Auto-generated English subtitles downloaded fine as json3 (word-level `segs[].tOffsetMs`) without any PO-token provider in this test. Thumbnails: `maxresdefault.jpg` (1280x720) exists only when the uploader provided an HD custom thumbnail (404 for a 9-day-old video, 200 for a 17M-view video); `--write-thumbnail --skip-download` picked maxresdefault.webp 1280x720 and `--convert-thumbnails jpg` produces a JPEG. Fallbacks are weak: youtube-transcript-api 1.2.4 works from residential IPs but is blocked from cloud IPs; scrapetube 2.6.0 returned zero channel videos (IndexError) in this test while its search still works; public Invidious instances are down to 5 with only one advertising the API (and that one rejected the request via Anubis), and all three probed Piped API hosts failed (525, 502, DNS). The official Data API now documents a default of 100 search.list calls/day plus 10,000 units/day for everything else, with playlistItems.list and videos.list at 1 unit per 50 items, but captions.download needs edit rights so it cannot fetch competitor transcripts. Nexlev's outlier score is verifiably views divided by the channel's average views over the analysed videos (61,000,000 / 8,632,143 = 7.07 shown as "7.1x"); 1of10 describes outliers as 3x+ of the channel's average over the same publication window (5x strong, marketing says 10x-100x); ViewStats and vidIQ publish no formula (vidIQ calls it a "breakout score" and also exposes views-per-hour).

### Recommendation

1) Make yt-dlp the single research engine, embedded via the Python API, pinned and auto-updated monthly (`pip install -U "yt-dlp[default,curl-cffi]"`), running on each user's laptop from their residential IP. Install Deno with the app (`winget install DenoLand.Deno`) so downloads work, but note research itself (flat listing, metadata, subtitles, thumbnails) was verified to work without it. 2) Two-tier fetching to stay under the ~300 video-requests/hour guest limit: Tier A, one flat call per tab: `yt-dlp --flat-playlist -J --extractor-args "youtubetab:approximate_date" --sleep-requests 0.75 "https://www.youtube.com/@HANDLE/videos"` and the same for `/shorts` (Python: `YoutubeDL({'extract_flat':'in_playlist','skip_download':True,'quiet':True,'sleep_interval_requests':0.75,'extractor_args':{'youtubetab':{'approximate_date':['']}}}).extract_info(url, download=False)`), cache entries in local SQLite keyed by video id with fetched_at. Tier B, exact metadata only for the top 20-50 outlier candidates: `yt-dlp -J --skip-download --extractor-args "youtube:player_skip=js;skip=hls,dash" --sleep-requests 1 URL` (~2 s each; gives exact views, likes, comments, upload_date, tags, chapters, description, caption languages). 3) Outlier score, replicating Nexlev/1of10 but more robust: separate long-form (/videos) from Shorts (/shorts); baseline = median of flat view_count over the channel's videos whose approximate timestamp lies within a window around the candidate (10 before and 10 after by position, or +/-90 days), excluding videos younger than 7 days from the baseline; score = views / baseline (Nexlev uses mean of the last N; add that as a second column); add age normalisation views_per_day = views / max(age_days, 1) and vpd_ratio = vpd / median_vpd of the window; add sub_ratio = views / channel_follower_count; rank by score with labels >=3x notable, >=5x strong, >=10x "1-of-10"; recompute the top candidates with exact Tier-B numbers before showing them. 4) For the chosen 1-3 videos: `yt-dlp --skip-download --write-auto-subs --write-subs --sub-langs "LANG.*,-live_chat" --sub-format json3 --write-thumbnail --convert-thumbnails jpg -o "%(id)s.%(ext)s" URL`; parse json3 events/segs for the transcript (word offsets are available) and keep the 1280x720 JPEG as the thumbnail template; if maxres is missing fall back to sddefault 640x480 (HEAD-check i.ytimg.com/vi/ID/maxresdefault.jpg -> sddefault.jpg -> hqdefault.jpg). 5) Failure handling: on "Sign in to confirm you're not a bot" or "This content isn't available, try again later" back off (exponential, >=10 min), then offer the user two opt-in fallbacks: (a) a cookies.txt exported from a private Firefox/Chrome window via "Get cookies.txt LOCALLY" (never `--cookies-from-browser chrome` on Windows; store per user, never in the shared GitHub repo), (b) the bgutil PO-token provider (needs Node 22+/Deno). Use youtube-transcript-api as the transcript fallback. Do not build on scrapetube, public Invidious or Piped (all failed on 2026-10-09). 6) Offer an optional "Data API key" setting for power users: uploads playlist via playlistItems.list + videos.list (1 unit per 50 videos) gives exact counts and dates cheaply; avoid search.list (100 calls/day cap). 7) Multi-language: pass `--extractor-args "youtube:lang=xx"` to prefer translated titles/descriptions when scoring non-English competitors, request `--sub-langs "xx.*"` and prefer the original-language auto track; keep all per-user data on the local drive as required.

### Options

| Option | Pros | Cons | Pricing | API / integration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| yt-dlp (CLI or embedded Python) run on each user's laptop | Verified working 2026-10-09 from a residential IP with no key, no login, no JS runtime for metadata: whole-channel flat listing (450 videos in 12 s), exact per-video stats in 2-3 s, auto-subs json3 with word timings, max-res thumbnails, search with filters; actively maintained (release 2026-08-19); pip-installable with the app; ~0 running cost. | Flat view counts are rounded (3 significant digits) and have no dates unless approximate_date; Shorts have no duration in flat mode; ~300 video/player requests per hour per guest IP; needs Deno for downloads (not for research); PO-token/cookie fallbacks add Node/Deno complexity; YouTube changes can break it between releases so auto-update is mandatory. | Free (Unlicense); optional Deno runtime free | `pip install "yt-dlp[default,curl-cffi]"`; `yt_dlp.YoutubeDL(opts).extract_info(url, download=False)`; CLI `yt-dlp --flat-playlist -J --extractor-args youtubetab:approximate_date URL/videos` |
| bgutil-ytdlp-pot-provider (PO-token plugin for yt-dlp) | Auto-detected by yt-dlp >=2025.05.22; supplies gvs/player/subs tokens so web-client streams and subtitles keep working on flagged IPs; 2.0.2 released 2026-10-07. | Requires Node.js >=22 or Deno >=2 and a local HTTP server (port 4416) or slower script mode; does not guarantee bypassing bot checks or IP login walls; extra install surface for non-developer users. | Free (open source) | `pip install bgutil-ytdlp-pot-provider`; `--extractor-args "youtubepot-bgutilhttp:base_url=http://127.0.0.1:4416"` |
| youtube-transcript-api 1.2.4 | Simplest transcript fetch (manual + auto, translation, list of languages); verified working from residential IP; good as a fallback when yt-dlp's subtitle path fails. | Blocked from cloud IPs (RequestBlocked/IpBlocked); cookie auth broken; sentence-level timings only (no word offsets); last release 2026-01-29. | Free (MIT) | `YouTubeTranscriptApi().fetch(video_id, languages=['en'])`, `.list(video_id)`, `transcript.translate('es').fetch()` |
| scrapetube 2.6.0 | Tiny dependency, generator API with videos/shorts/streams and sort_by popular. | get_channel returned 0 videos / IndexError for username, URL and channel ID in the 2026-10-09 test; last release 2025-09-16; raw InnerTube renderer dicts (viewCountText strings). Not recommended. | Free (MIT) | `scrapetube.get_channel(channel_url=..., content_type='videos', sort_by='popular')` |
| Invidious / Piped public instances | Clean JSON (viewCount, published, lengthSeconds, captions URLs) when they work; no key. | Public Invidious list is down to 5 HTTPS instances, 4 disable the API and the fifth blocked the request with Anubis; all three probed Piped API hosts failed (525/502/DNS) on 2026-10-09; self-hosting needs invidious-companion and rotating proxies - conflicts with zero-cost local-only requirement. | Free but effectively unavailable / self-host costs | GET /api/v1/channels/:ucid/videos?continuation=; GET /channel/:id (Piped) |
| YouTube Data API v3 (user's own free key) | Exact statistics, ISO durations, exact publishedAt, 50 videos per unit via playlistItems.list + videos.list; stable and legal; 10,000 units/day covers hundreds of thousands of videos per day. | Requires each user to create a Google Cloud project/key (non-developer friction); search.list capped at 100 calls/day; no competitor captions (captions.download needs edit rights); no thumbnail-template or outlier logic. | Free tier: 10,000 units/day + 100 search.list calls/day | channels.list(part=contentDetails) -> uploads playlist 'UU...' -> playlistItems.list(maxResults=50) -> videos.list(part=statistics,contentDetails,snippet, id=50 ids) |
| Paid outlier tools (Nexlev, vidIQ, 1of10, ViewStats) via API/MCP | Pre-computed outlier scores, niche databases, VPH, thumbnail similarity; Nexlev's formula is simply views / channel average views and can be replicated. | Subscription cost (vidIQ MCP charges credits per call); proprietary windows undisclosed (ViewStats, vidIQ); contradicts the near-zero running-cost goal; can be used only as an optional cross-check. | Paid plans (1of10 free tier shows 5 outlier results) | Nexlev MCP youtube_channel_outliers(channel_id, max_videos, min_outlier_threshold); vidIQ MCP vidiq_outliers(channelIds, minOutlierScore, minVph) |

### Facts

- (high, 2026-10-09) Local test (yt-dlp 2026.08.19, Windows 10, residential IP): `--flat-playlist` on https://www.youtube.com/@veritasium/videos returned 450 entries in 12.2 s; every entry had duration, 445/450 had view_count, 0 had timestamp; top-level dict included channel_follower_count=21,300,000, channel_id, playlist_count=450, tags, description, thumbnails. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/blob/master/yt_dlp/extractor/youtube/_tab.py
- (high, 2026-10-09) Flat /videos entry keys are exactly: _type, id, ie_key, url, title, duration, view_count, thumbnails (one hqdefault 480x270 with sqp signature), live_status, availability, channel_url, uploader_url, creators, __x_forwarded_for_ip; live_status/availability were None for normal videos and timestamp/upload_date/description/like_count are absent. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/blob/master/yt_dlp/extractor/youtube/_tab.py
- (high, 2026-10-09) Flat-mode view_count is parsed from display text ('1.2M views') and is therefore rounded: flat gave 1,200,000 for video ONAuUyml0yI whose full extraction reported 1,246,453 views. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/blob/master/yt_dlp/extractor/youtube/_tab.py
- (high, 2026-10-09) `--extractor-args youtubetab:approximate_date` adds a day-granularity `timestamp` derived from relative text ('2 weeks ago'); in the test it gave 2026-10-01 00:00 UTC for a video actually uploaded 2026-09-30 19:19 UTC, and `upload_date` stayed None on the flat entries. README: 'Extract approximate upload_date and timestamp in flat-playlist. This may cause date-based filters to be slightly off'. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/blob/master/README.md#extractor-arguments
- (high, 2026-10-09) Flat /shorts tab entries (shortsLockupViewModel) contain id, title, url (https://www.youtube.com/shorts/ID), view_count and 2 thumbnails but NO duration; durations for Shorts require per-video extraction. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/blob/master/yt_dlp/extractor/youtube/_tab.py
- (high, 2026-10-09) A channel URL without a tab (https://www.youtube.com/@veritasium) returns a playlist whose entries are the tab playlists ('Veritasium - Videos', 'Veritasium - Shorts'); request /videos, /shorts, /streams explicitly. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/blob/master/yt_dlp/extractor/youtube/_tab.py
- (high, 2026-10-09) Per-video `-J --skip-download` (no JS runtime installed) took 3.0 s and returned exact view_count 17,570,501, like_count 133,895, comment_count 6,800, upload_date 20260921, timestamp, duration, channel_follower_count, categories, language, 36 tags, 10 chapters, availability, live_status, 157 automatic_captions languages with formats vtt/json3/srv1/srv2/srv3/ttml/srt, manual subtitles, 42-entry thumbnail ladder and 53 formats. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/blob/master/README.md#output-template
- (medium, 2026-10-09) Adding `--extractor-args "youtube:player_skip=js;skip=hls,dash,translated_subs"` cut per-video metadata time from 3.0 s to 2.0 s with all metadata fields unchanged (formats dropped 53->36); in this test the automatic_captions language count stayed at 157 despite skip=translated_subs, so do not rely on that flag to shrink the caption list. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/blob/master/README.md#extractor-arguments
- (high, 2026-10-09) Without a JS runtime yt-dlp 2026.08.19 only warns: '[youtube] No supported JavaScript runtime could be found. Only deno is enabled by default; to use another runtime add --js-runtimes RUNTIME[:PATH] ... YouTube extraction without a JS runtime has been deprecated, and some formats may be missing' - metadata, flat listings and json3 auto-subtitles still extracted successfully. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/wiki/EJS
- (high, 2026-10-09) README (fetched 2026-10-09): default player clients are `visionos,web`; 'If no JavaScript runtime/engine is available, then web is omitted'; with logged-in cookies `web_embedded,tv_downgraded,web` (free) or `web_creator,tv_downgraded,web` (premium). Available clients: web, web_safari, web_embedded, web_music, web_creator, mweb, ios, visionos, android, android_vr, tv, tv_downgraded, tv_simply. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/blob/master/README.md#extractor-arguments
- (high, 2026-07-12) PO Token Guide (last edited 2026-07-12): PO tokens are required for `web`/`web_safari` Subs and GVS, `mweb`/`web_music`/`web_creator`/`tv_simply` GVS, `android`/`ios` GVS or Player; NOT required for `web_embedded` (embeddable videos only), `android_vr`, `tv` (DRM'd without cookies). Three contexts exist: gvs, player, subs. Manual syntax: `--extractor-args "youtube:po_token=web.gvs+TOKEN,web.subs+TOKEN"`; `fetch_pot=auto|always|never`. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/wiki/PO-Token-Guide
- (high, 2026-10-07) bgutil-ytdlp-pot-provider 2.0.2 (PyPI release 2026-10-07) is the recommended PO-token provider: `pip install -U bgutil-ytdlp-pot-provider`, HTTP server mode on port 4416 (Docker or native, needs Node.js >=22 or Deno >=2.0) or script mode; requires yt-dlp >=2025.05.22; auto-detected (check `yt-dlp -v` for 'bgutil providers'); README warns tokens 'do not guarantee bypassing 403 errors or bot checks' and cannot bypass IP-based login restrictions. - https://github.com/Brainicism/bgutil-ytdlp-pot-provider
- (high, 2025-09-23) yt-dlp issue #14404 (2025-09-23) announced that YouTube extraction requires an external JavaScript runtime (Deno 2.0+, Node 20+, Bun, QuickJS 2023-12-9+); the EJS wiki now lists Deno >=2.3.0 (recommended, enabled by default), Node >=22.0.0, QuickJS/QuickJS-NG, Bun deprecated; options `--js-runtimes deno:/path`, `--js-runtimes node`, `--remote-components ejs:github|ejs:npm`. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/issues/14404
- (high, 2026-10-09) The Windows yt-dlp.exe bundles the yt-dlp-ejs challenge scripts ('No additional action required') but NOT a JS runtime; pip users should install `pip install -U "yt-dlp[default]"` (includes yt-dlp-ejs 0.8.0, released 2026-03-17). Deno on Windows: `irm https://deno.land/install.ps1 | iex` or `winget install DenoLand.Deno`; 'Deno is a single binary executable with no external dependencies'. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/wiki/EJS
- (high, 2026-08-19) yt-dlp latest release is 2026.08.19 (published 2026-08-19T23:48:43Z): added visionos client, removed android_vr from defaults, 'always extract channel metadata' in the tab extractor; 2026.07.04 raised the minimum recommended Python to 3.11. `yt-dlp[default]` does not include curl_cffi; install `pip install "yt-dlp[default,curl-cffi]"` to remove the 'no impersonate target is available' warning seen in the test. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/releases
- (high, 2025-06-11) Extractors wiki ('Common YouTube Errors', edited 2025-06-11): 'the rate limit for guest sessions is ~300 videos/hour (~1000 webpage/player requests per hour). For accounts, it is ~2000 videos/hour (~4000 webpage/player requests per hour)'; the symptom is 'This content isn't available, try again later'; it recommends a 5-10 s delay between downloads or `-t sleep`, which README defines as `--sleep-subtitles 5 --sleep-requests 0.75`. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/wiki/Extractors
- (high, 2025-06-11) Cookie export procedure (Extractors wiki): log into YouTube in a new private/incognito window, navigate to https://www.youtube.com/robots.txt in the same tab, export youtube.com cookies with an extension, then close the private window so the session is never reopened (YouTube rotates cookies on open tabs); do NOT combine `--cookies FILE --cookies-from-browser BROWSER` for this. FAQ recommends 'Get cookies.txt LOCALLY' (Chrome) and 'cookies.txt' (Firefox), Netscape format. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/wiki/Extractors#exporting-youtube-cookies
- (high, 2024-09-05) Issue #10927 (2024-09-05): Chrome's app-bound cookie encryption means `--cookies-from-browser chrome` 'will likely mean ... permanently broken on Windows' (seproDev); workarounds: use Firefox, or export with 'Get cookies.txt LOCALLY' and pass `--cookies file.txt` (confirmed working by users even with Chrome open). - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/issues/10927
- (medium, 2024-06-10) Issue #10128 (June 2024, still the reference thread): 'Sign in to confirm you're not a bot' is IP-reputation driven and 'affecting almost all apps that access Youtube, including the official Youtube web app' (bashonly); users may need to wait for a new ISP IP; commenters warn against passing personal account cookies because of ban risk (#10085, #10108). - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/issues/10128
- (high, 2026-10-09) Auto-subtitle download worked in the test without any PO-token provider: `writeautomaticsub=True, subtitleslangs=['en'], subtitlesformat='json3', skip_download=True` produced ONAuUyml0yI.en.json3 with 249 events; json3 carries word-level timing via `events[].segs[].tOffsetMs` (e.g. 'We' 0 ms, 'are' +200 ms, 'launching' +320 ms) plus `tStartMs`/`dDurationMs` per event. CLI equivalent: `yt-dlp --write-auto-subs --sub-langs "en.*" --sub-format json3 --skip-download URL`. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/blob/master/README.md#subtitle-options
- (high, 2026-10-09) Thumbnail ladder on i.ytimg.com: `/vi/ID/maxresdefault.jpg` (1280x720) returned 404 for a recent 5-minute video but 200 (313,946 bytes) for a 17M-view video; `hq720.jpg` mirrors maxres; `sddefault.jpg` (640x480) and `hqdefault.jpg` (480x360) always existed; webp variants live under `/vi_webp/ID/`. `--write-thumbnail --skip-download` saved maxresdefault.webp (ffprobe: 1280x720, 172 KB) and with `--convert-thumbnails jpg` (FFmpegThumbnailsConvertor) a .jpg was produced. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/blob/master/README.md#thumbnail-options
- (high, 2026-10-09) `ytsearch5:QUERY` with extract_flat returns richer entries than channel tabs (channel, channel_id, channel_is_verified, description, duration, view_count, live_status, timestamp keys) in about 2 s, and a results URL with YouTube's `sp=` filter parameter (e.g. upload date = this month, type = video) also works in flat mode. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp/blob/master/README.md
- (high, 2026-01-29) youtube-transcript-api v1.2.4 (2026-01-29, Python >=3.8,<3.15) worked from this residential IP: `YouTubeTranscriptApi().list(id)` returned manual + auto English; `fetch(id, languages=['en'])` returned 106 snippets with text/start/duration. README: cloud-provider IPs are mostly blocked (RequestBlocked/IpBlocked), rotating residential proxies via WebshareProxyConfig/GenericProxyConfig are the fix; cookie auth is currently broken. - https://github.com/jdepoix/youtube-transcript-api
- (high, 2026-10-09) scrapetube 2.6.0 (last release 2025-09-16) failed channel listing in the test on 2026-10-09: get_channel with channel_username, channel_url and channel_id all returned 0 videos (IndexError), while get_search still returned results; its API offers content_type videos/shorts/streams, sort_by newest/oldest/popular, sleep and proxies. - https://scrapetube.readthedocs.io/en/latest/
- (high, 2026-10-09) Invidious: docs say 'The list of public instances is short due to the recent YouTube issues' and 'please host Invidious at home instead of using a public instance'; api.invidious.io listed 5 HTTPS instances with only invidious.f5.si advertising api:true, and that instance answered the channel-videos API call with an Anubis 'Access Denied' page on 2026-10-09. Self-hosting now requires invidious-companion for streams. - https://docs.invidious.io/instances/
- (high, 2026-10-09) Piped: documentation lists 18 public instances (pipedapi.* API hosts) but probes on 2026-10-09 failed: pipedapi.kavin.rocks HTTP 525, pipedapi.leptons.xyz HTTP 502, api.piped.yt DNS not found; API offers /channel/:id and /nextpage/channel/:id with relatedStreams items (url, title, thumbnail, views, duration, uploadedDate). - https://docs.piped.video/docs/api-documentation/
- (medium, 2026-10-08) YouTube Data API v3 docs (getting-started updated 2026-09-14; quota page updated 2026-10-08): 'default quota allocation of 100 search.list calls, 100 videos.insert calls, and 10,000 units per day combined for all other endpoints'; playlistItems.list, videos.list, channels.list, commentThreads.list cost 1 unit; captions.list 50; playlistItems.list maxResults 0-50 using the channel's uploads playlist (contentDetails.relatedPlaylists.uploads, 'UU...' id). This per-method cap on search.list appears to be a 2026 change from the older '100 units per search' model - verify in the Cloud console. - https://developers.google.com/youtube/v3/determine_quota_cost
- (high, 2026-09-15) captions.download (200 units) 'requires the user to have permission to edit the video' (OAuth youtube.force-ssl or youtubepartner), so the official API cannot fetch competitor transcripts; yt-dlp or youtube-transcript-api are the only no-login routes. - https://developers.google.com/youtube/v3/docs/captions/download
- (high, 2026-10-09) Nexlev outlier score (verified via its youtube_channel_outliers tool on Veritasium, max_videos=30): channel `avgViews` = 8,632,143; video 'The Closest Thing We Have to Alien Technology' viewCount 61,000,000 -> `viewRatio` 7.0666 -> `outlierScore` '7.1x', i.e. views / mean views of the analysed recent videos; tool threshold semantics: 'A value of 2.0 means videos with 2x the average views'; Nexlev's faceless-outlier filter documents 'video views / channel average views. >= 1 above average, >= 2 strong, >= 3 exceptional'. - https://www.nexlev.io/
- (medium, 2026-06-10) 1of10: landing page defines outliers as 'videos that perform 10x to 100x better than a channel's average'; its blog (2026-06-10) says 'a video performing at 3x or more of its channel's average over the same publication window is worth noting. A video at 5x or above is a strong signal'; the exact baseline window and mean-vs-median choice are not disclosed. Free plan: 'Outlier search - 5 results'. - https://1of10.com/blog/how-to-find-viral-youtube-videos/
- (low, 2026-10-09) ViewStats publishes no formula; its Outliers feature is described only as 'Find content that's outperforming related to where it should be'. vidIQ (via its MCP tool schema, Oct 2026) returns a 'breakout score showing how much each video exceeds its channel's typical performance' and exposes `minVph` (views per hour) and `minOutlierScore` filters; vidiq.com blocked direct fetches (HTTP 403). - https://www.viewstats.com/
- (high, 2026-10-09) Python API pattern verified: `yt_dlp.YoutubeDL({'quiet': True, 'skip_download': True, 'extract_flat': 'in_playlist', 'playlist_items': '1:300', 'extractor_args': {'youtubetab': {'approximate_date': ['']}}}).extract_info('https://www.youtube.com/@handle/videos', download=False)` returns `info['entries']` as a list of flat dicts; per-video: `YoutubeDL({'skip_download': True, 'extractor_args': {'youtube': {'player_skip': ['js'], 'skip': ['hls','dash']}}}).extract_info(watch_url, download=False)`. - https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp#embedding-yt-dlp

### Open questions

- Will YouTube start enforcing PO tokens for guest subtitle (timedtext) requests on the clients yt-dlp uses by default? On 2026-10-09 json3 auto-subs downloaded without a provider; re-test after each yt-dlp release and keep the bgutil fallback ready.
- The Data API quota page now reads '100 search.list calls ... and 10,000 units per day combined for all other endpoints' (updated 2026-10-08) - confirm the real per-project numbers in a fresh Google Cloud console before promising users an API fallback.
- How well do ISP/CGNAT IPs of the target users (multiple countries, top-10 YouTube languages) behave? Shared mobile/CGNAT ranges can be flagged like datacenters; needs a field test per user with the back-off logic.
- Exact baseline windows used by 1of10 ('same publication window'), ViewStats and vidIQ are undisclosed; the proposed median-of-neighbours + age-normalised design is a reasoned equivalent, not a reproduction.
- Flat view_count is rounded to ~3 significant digits and approximate_date is day-level and coarser for old videos ('1 year ago'); decide whether Tier-B exact fetches are needed for all videos in the scoring window (cost: ~2 s and 1 player request each) or only for the top candidates.
- Shorts durations are not available in flat mode and Shorts view counts on the /shorts tab are rounded; decide if a Tier-B pass is needed for Shorts outliers.
- Whether `youtube:skip=translated_subs` actually reduces network work on current versions (the automatic_captions count stayed 157 in the test) and which caption track name carries the original language (yt-dlp uses an '-orig' suffix pattern) for non-English channels.
- Deno/Node distribution to non-developer Windows users (installer step vs. bundled binary) and the security posture of running the bgutil HTTP server locally (unauthenticated, bind to 127.0.0.1 only).

## zero-cost-llm: Running the LLM "brain" of Cashflow Studio at near-zero marginal cost (Claude subscription vs headless Claude Code vs MCP vs Agent SDK vs API vs local LLMs), as of 2026-10-09

### Summary

The single most important finding is two days old: Anthropic's help center (updated 2026-10-07) says Claude Max 5x and Max 20x plans now include $100 and $200 per month of Claude API credits, claimed by linking a Claude Console organization to the plan; the credits cover the Messages API, Message Batches, the Agent SDK, Managed Agents and `claude -p` run with that org's API key, work with any model, are spent first, do not roll over, and do not cover interactive Claude Code sessions or Bedrock/Vertex/Foundry. Combined with Claude Haiku 5.5 (released 2026-10-07 at $0.10/$0.50 per MTok, $0.05/$0.25 in batch) and Sonnet 5.5 ($2/$10, $1/$5 batch), the LLM share of a 10-minute faceless video is roughly $0.01-0.03 (Haiku 5.5) to $0.15-0.30 (Sonnet 5.5) per video, so a Max 5x user's monthly credit covers hundreds to thousands of videos at zero incremental cost, and the user's own "$3-5 per video" estimate is correctly dominated by images and voice, not the LLM. On policy: the Consumer Terms (effective 2025-10-08) ban automated access to the consumer service "except when you are accessing our Services via an Anthropic API Key or where we otherwise explicitly permit it"; Anthropic explicitly permits subscribers to run `claude -p`, `claude setup-token` long-lived OAuth tokens for CI/scripts, GitHub Actions, routines and scheduled tasks on their own subscription, and the Agent SDK help article (updated 2026-10-07) says "You can still use the Claude Agent SDK, `claude -p`, and third-party apps with your subscription limits." What is prohibited, per the Claude Code legal page and the AUP, is third-party developers offering claude.ai login, routing other users' requests through Free/Pro/Max credentials, collecting or intermediating claude.ai tokens, or tools that misrepresent their client identity; this is what the January-February 2026 OpenCode episode enforced, and Anthropic says it may route third-party-tool usage to pay-as-you-go usage credits instead of subscription limits. Weekly limits (rolled out 2025-08-28 alongside the 5-hour window) remain in force and are shared across claude.ai, Claude Desktop and Claude Code; Anthropic no longer publishes hour figures, and the July 2025 numbers (e.g. Pro 40-80 h Sonnet 4/week) are outdated. Exposing the Python pipeline as a local MCP server for Claude Desktop/Claude Code is squarely "ordinary use of native Anthropic applications", works on Pro, and is well documented for Windows, but it is human-in-the-loop, capped at 25k tokens per MCP tool result by default, and draws on the same session/weekly limits. The Agent SDK itself is API-key only for anything distributed to other people, and is heavier than a deterministic pipeline needs. Local open-weight models have improved sharply (Gemma 4 31B scores 1452 on Arena vs Sonnet 5.5's 1476 and Haiku 4.5's 1414; Qwen3.5 claims 201 languages, Gemma 4 140), and on a 32 GB laptop the Gemma 4 26B MoE (3.8B active, 16-19 GB) or Qwen3.8-27B (18 GB, Apache 2.0) are viable, with Gemma 4 12B or Qwen3.5 9B (7-8 GB) on 16 GB machines; but CPU-only laptops generate long scripts slowly, Q4 quantization and Ollama's 4,096-token default context need care, and no independent data yet shows them matching Claude on long-form scripts across ten languages. A hybrid is sensible only as an offline/zero-plan fallback and for bulk low-stakes preprocessing; with Max credits, Claude via the API is both cheaper in effort and essentially free in dollars. Several facts are only days old and should be re-verified in the Console before the architecture is frozen.

### Recommendation

Build the brain on the plain Claude API, not on Claude Code internals. Each user links a Claude Console organization to their own Max subscription (claude.ai Settings > Billing), creates an API key, and pastes it into Cashflow Studio, which stores it locally per user and never shares or proxies it. The Max 5x plan's $100/month (Max 20x: $200/month) API credit, live since 2026-10-07, covers Messages and Batches on any model, so at the estimated $0.01-0.03 per video on Haiku 5.5 or $0.15-0.30 on Sonnet 5.5 the marginal LLM cost is zero for any realistic monthly output, and because the API key is the explicitly permitted automation path there is no terms-of-service exposure for a multi-user distributed app. Default routing: claude-sonnet-5-5 for the viral-title rewrite and the 1,500-word script in each of the ten target languages (consider claude-opus-5-5 only for English hero scripts), claude-haiku-5-5 at effort low/medium for storyboard JSON with on-image callouts, SEO keywords/tags, thumbnail briefs and translation passes; use structured outputs for all JSON, put the framework/style guide behind a 1-hour cache_control breakpoint (the 512-token minimum is easily met), and run non-urgent or multi-language jobs through the Message Batches API for a further 50% off. Add a per-user Console spend limit and surface the Console usage page in the app so credit exhaustion is visible before 'API requests stop'. Provide two fallbacks: (a) for Pro-only users, a 'run inside Claude' mode where the pipeline is exposed as a local stdio MCP server for Claude Desktop/Claude Code (ordinary native-app use under their own login, Windows-supported) and, optionally, non-bare `claude -p --output-format json --json-schema` invoked only against the user's own login with the binary unmodified, accepting that this draws on their 5-hour/weekly limits and that Anthropic may bill such third-party-tool usage to usage credits; (b) an offline/zero-plan mode using Ollama or LM Studio's OpenAI-compatible endpoint with Gemma 4 26B MoE (32 GB machines) or Gemma 4 12B / Qwen3.5 9B (16 GB machines), num_ctx raised to 16k+, used for drafts, translation QA, keyword clustering and transcript cleanup rather than final scripts. Do not use the Agent SDK for the core pipeline (API-key only, heavier, less deterministic), and never pool subscriptions, embed setup-token OAuth tokens, or spoof the Claude Code client identity. Re-verify three young facts before freezing the design: the Max monthly API credit terms (2 days old), Haiku 5.5's long-form multilingual quality (2 days old, not yet on leaderboards), and the AUP version dated 2026-11-12.

### Options

| Option | Pros | Cons | Pricing | API / integration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Claude API (Messages + Batches + prompt caching) paid from the Max plan's monthly API credit, via an API key of the Console org linked to each user's own Max subscription | The API key is the one path the Consumer Terms explicitly carve out for automation, and the only one Anthropic recommends for third-party tools, so a distributed Python app is unambiguously within terms. Max 5x/20x include $100/$200 per month of API credit (since 2026-10-07) covering Messages and Batches on any model, so marginal cost is $0 until the credit is exhausted; estimated $0.01-0.03 per video on Haiku 5.5 or $0.15-0.30 on Sonnet 5.5, i.e. hundreds to thousands of videos per month. No 5-hour/weekly gating, structured outputs (`output_config.format`) for storyboard JSON, 50% batch discount for overnight runs, cache reads at $0.01-$0.10 per MTok, per-user Console spend limits, authoritative billing in the Console. | Only Max and Team plans get the credit; Pro users get nothing and must buy credits (which expire after one year). Credits do not roll over and 'API requests stop until your next monthly credits arrive' unless the user adds funds. A freshly linked Console org may start in the Evaluation tier with reduced rate limits. Each user must do a one-time Console link and paste their own key into the app; the app must store it locally and never pool keys. Program is two days old, so details may change. | Included: $100/month (Max 5x, $100/mo plan) or $200/month (Max 20x, $200/mo plan) of API credit. List rates after that: Haiku 5.5 $0.10/$0.50 per MTok (batch $0.05/$0.25); Sonnet 5.5 $2/$10 (batch $1/$5); Opus 5.5 $4/$20 (batch $2/$10). | Python `anthropic` SDK: client.messages.create / client.messages.batches.create with cache_control on the framework prefix; model claude-haiku-5-5 for storyboard/SEO/translation, claude-sonnet-5-5 (or claude-opus-5-5) for scripts; ANTHROPIC_API_KEY per user. |
| Headless Claude Code (`claude -p --output-format json --json-schema ...`) running under the user's own subscription login (non-bare mode) or a `claude setup-token` CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN | Works on Pro as well as Max at no extra dollar cost; Anthropic documents `-p`, setup-token, GitHub Actions, routines and scheduled tasks as supported subscription use, and the Agent SDK help article (2026-10-07) says you can still use `claude -p` and third-party apps within subscription limits. Structured JSON output and session continuation are built in; subscribers get a 1-hour cache TTL automatically. | Draws from the same 5-hour and weekly limits as the user's chat use, and 'a large workflow fanout can exhaust the weekly allowance'; Claude Code adds system-prompt/tool overhead tokens; `total_cost_usd` is only an estimate and irrelevant to plan billing. Shipping an app that shells out to `claude -p` makes Cashflow Studio a platform 'running Claude Code in your products', which requires agreeing to the Commercial Terms, leaving the binary unmodified and never touching the user's credentials; Anthropic may route third-party-tool traffic to usage credits at its discretion. `--bare` (API-key only) 'will become the default for -p in a future release', so subscription-auth scripts will need to pass flags explicitly or break. Not covered by the Max monthly API credit unless run with the linked org's API key. | $0 beyond the subscription while within limits; overflow at standard API rates via usage credits if enabled. | subprocess.run(['claude','-p',prompt,'--output-format','json','--json-schema',schema,'--max-budget-usd','1']) on the user's machine; the user runs `claude /login` themselves; optional `--mcp-config` to expose the app's tools; never pass --bare if subscription auth is intended. |
| Expose the Python pipeline as a local MCP server (stdio) and let the user drive it from Claude Desktop or Claude Code conversationally | Squarely 'ordinary use of Claude Code and other native Anthropic applications' under the user's own login, so no third-party-auth risk and it works on Pro. Claude Desktop runs on Windows 10+, local stdio servers are configured in %APPDATA%\Claude\claude_desktop_config.json or `claude mcp add --transport stdio ... -- python server.py`, desktop extensions support Python servers and are available to all Desktop users, and each tool call gets a permission prompt (good for non-developer users). Desktop scheduled tasks (1-minute minimum) can even run the flow on a timer locally. | Human-in-the-loop and conversational, not a fire-and-forget batch; each run consumes session/weekly subscription usage; MCP tool results default to a 25k-token cap (configurable) and stdio servers don't auto-reconnect; output quality/determinism depends on the chat model and prompt rather than a fixed pipeline; users must install Claude Desktop or Claude Code; usage from the pipeline competes with the user's own chatting. | $0 beyond the subscription; usage credits at API rates if limits are exceeded and enabled. | Python MCP SDK (FastMCP) over stdio exposing tools like research_channel, write_script, build_storyboard, render_video; registered in claude_desktop_config.json or via `claude mcp add`. |
| Claude Agent SDK (claude-agent-sdk for Python) embedded in the app | Full Claude Code harness (agent loop, built-in Read/Write/Bash/WebSearch, MCP, hooks, subagents) with Python-native message objects; now covered by the Max/Team monthly API credit; governed by Commercial Terms so distribution to other users is clean when each user supplies their own API key. | API-key only for third-party products ('Anthropic does not allow third party developers to offer claude.ai login or rate limits'); heavier and less deterministic than direct Messages calls for a fixed content pipeline; agent overhead tokens; cost fields are estimates; bundles a native binary per platform (ARM64 Windows needs separate install). | Standard API rates (same as option 1), drawn first from Max/Team monthly credits. | pip install claude-agent-sdk; query(prompt, ClaudeAgentOptions(allowed_tools=[...], mcp_servers=...)); ANTHROPIC_API_KEY. |
| Local open-weight LLM via Ollama or LM Studio on the user's Windows laptop (Gemma 4 26B MoE / 31B or Qwen3.8-27B on 32 GB; Gemma 4 12B, Qwen3.5 9B or Gemma 4 E4B on 16 GB; gpt-oss 20b borderline on 16 GB) | Truly $0 per token, offline, private, no rate or weekly limits, no terms-of-service exposure. Quality has closed much of the gap: Gemma 4 31B scores 1452 on Arena vs 1476 for Sonnet 5.5-xhigh and above Haiku 4.5 (1414); Gemma 4 claims 140 languages and Qwen3.5 201 languages/dialects; Gemma 4, Qwen3.8 and gpt-oss are Apache 2.0. Both runtimes expose OpenAI-compatible local endpoints (localhost:11434/v1, localhost:1234/v1) with JSON mode, so one client abstraction can switch between local and Claude. | Still clearly below Opus 5.5 (1507) and Sonnet 5.5 on preference benchmarks, and no independent evidence yet for long-form, multi-language YouTube scriptwriting; Q4 quantization (what fits on laptops) loses further quality; dense 27-31B models on CPU-only laptops produce a 2,700-token script in many minutes (speed figures are community rules of thumb, not sourced); Ollama's default 4,096-token context must be raised; 16 GB machines are limited to ~12B-class models; model files are 7-20 GB downloads per user; the architect must own prompt engineering/QA per model; Gemma 3 is under Gemma Terms of Use rather than Apache. | $0 per token; one-time hardware/time cost. Downloads: Gemma 4 12B 7.7-8.0 GB, 26B 16-19 GB, 31B 19-20 GB; Qwen3.5 9B 6.6-7.6 GB; Qwen3.8 27B 18 GB; gpt-oss 20b 14 GB. | Ollama REST/OpenAI-compatible API (http://localhost:11434/v1) or LM Studio server (http://localhost:1234/v1); set num_ctx >= 16k; use JSON mode for storyboard output. |
| Hybrid: Claude (Haiku 5.5 / Sonnet 5.5 via API credits) for scripts, storyboards and SEO; local model for bulk, low-stakes preprocessing and as an offline/no-plan fallback | Puts quality where it matters (viral-title rewrite, script, callouts, thumbnail brief) while transcript cleaning, dedupe, keyword clustering, tag expansion and translation QA can run locally at $0; gives Pro-only or plan-less users a working (lower-quality) path; a single OpenAI-style provider interface keeps the switch cheap. | Two prompt stacks to maintain and evaluate; with Max credits Claude is already free at this volume, so the local tier saves nothing for Max users and adds support burden on 16 GB machines; inconsistent style between tiers must be controlled by shared templates. | Claude portion ≈ $0.01-0.30 per video (covered by Max credits); local portion $0. | Provider abstraction with two backends: anthropic SDK (Messages/Batches) and OpenAI-compatible local endpoint; per-user config chooses backend and model. |
| Pro-plan-only user path (no monthly API credit): Claude Desktop MCP or non-bare `claude -p` under subscription limits, or buy API credits | Keeps the app usable for $20/month users without any API bill; Pro includes Claude Code and routines. | Subscription limits are the only budget (5-hour and weekly, shared with chat); heavy multi-language fan-out can burn a weekly allowance; no batch discount; if Anthropic routes third-party-tool traffic to usage credits the user pays API rates anyway. | $20/month plan; overflow at standard API rates via usage credits if enabled. |  |
| Not recommended: pooling one subscription for many users, embedding setup-token OAuth tokens in the app, spoofing Claude Code client headers, or proxying claude.ai sessions | None beyond short-term cost avoidance. | Directly prohibited by the Consumer Terms (account sharing), the Claude Code legal page (no intermediating credentials, no routing others' requests through Pro/Max credentials) and the AUP ('services that route requests through consumer subscriptions or misrepresent the product or client being used'); Anthropic enforced this against OpenCode in Jan-Feb 2026 and reserves the right to enforce without notice, which would strand every user of the app. | n/a |  |

### Facts

- (high, 2025-10-08) Anthropic Consumer Terms (shown as 'Effective October 8, 2025') prohibit, 'Except when you are accessing our Services via an Anthropic API Key or where we otherwise explicitly permit it, to access the Services through automated or non-human means, whether through a bot, script, or otherwise'; they also prohibit sharing account credentials or making your account available to anyone else, and reselling the Services. - https://www.anthropic.com/legal/consumer-terms
- (high, 2026-10-09 (fetched)) Claude Code legal page (official): 'OAuth authentication is intended exclusively for purchasers of Claude Free, Pro, Max, Team, and Enterprise subscription plans and is designed to support ordinary use of Claude Code and other native Anthropic applications.' and 'Advertised usage limits for Pro and Max plans assume ordinary, individual usage of Claude Code and the Agent SDK.' - https://code.claude.com/docs/en/legal-and-compliance
- (high, 2026-10-09 (fetched)) Same page: 'Anthropic does not permit third-party developers to offer Claude.ai login into their own applications, or to route requests through Free, Pro, or Max plan credentials on behalf of their users. Moreover, developers may not collect, store, or intermediate Claude.ai credentials or session tokens — sign-in to a Claude account must complete through Anthropic's own flow.' Anthropic 'reserves the right to take measures to enforce these restrictions and may do so without prior notice.' - https://code.claude.com/docs/en/legal-and-compliance
- (high, 2026-10-09 (fetched)) Same page: preinstalling or running Claude Code inside your own product requires agreeing to the Commercial Terms and (a) the Claude Code binary must not be modified and no built-in auth method removed, (b) 'Each end user must authenticate with their own Anthropic API key, Claude subscription plan credentials, or 3P inference provider credential', and customers 'may not pay for, resell, or intermediate Claude usage on their end users' behalf'. It adds this does not 'prevent an end user from signing in to the unmodified Claude Code binary with their own Claude subscription, including where a platform hosts Claude Code'. - https://code.claude.com/docs/en/legal-and-compliance
- (high, 2026-10-09 (fetched)) Agent SDK docs (overview and quickstart): 'Unless previously approved, Anthropic does not allow third party developers to offer claude.ai login or rate limits for their products, including agents built on the Claude Agent SDK. Use the API key authentication methods described in the Quickstart instead.' The SDK is governed by the Commercial Terms of Service; supported credentials are ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, Bedrock, Claude Platform on AWS, Vertex (Agent Platform) and Foundry. - https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/overview
- (high, 2026-10-07) Help-center article 'Use the Claude Agent SDK with your Claude plan': June 15, 2026 note: 'We've paused the previously-announced changes to Claude Agent SDK usage. For now, nothing has changed: Claude Agent SDK, `claude -p`, and third-party app usage still draw from your subscription limits.' October 7, 2026 note: 'Claude Max and Team plans now include monthly API credits, which cover the Claude Agent SDK, `claude -p`, the Claude API, and Claude Managed Agents.' and 'You can still use the Claude Agent SDK, `claude -p`, and third-party apps with your subscription limits.' - https://support.claude.com/en/articles/15036540-use-the-claude-agent-sdk-with-your-claude-plan
- (high, 2026-10-07) Monthly API credits (official help center): Max 5x gets $100/month and Max 20x $200/month of Claude API credit; Team Standard seats $20/seat and Premium $100/seat, pooled with a $500/month cap. 'To claim your credit, you link a Claude Console organization to your plan' (Settings > Billing). Credits refresh each billing cycle, 'Doesn't roll over. Unused credits expire at the end of each billing cycle', are 'Used first' before purchased credits, and 'work with any available Claude model on the Claude Platform'. They cover the Messages API and Message Batches API, Claude Managed Agents, the Claude Agent SDK, and `claude -p` 'when run with your linked organization's API key'; they do not cover interactive Claude Code sessions or usage on Bedrock, Vertex AI or Microsoft Foundry. 'If the organization has no other credits, API requests stop until your next monthly credits arrive.' Pro is not listed as receiving monthly API credits. - https://support.claude.com/en/articles/17154008-monthly-api-credits-for-max-and-team-plans
- (high, 2026-10-09 (fetched)) Help-center 'Log in to your Claude account': 'The preferred way to access Anthropic services using third-party software, tools, or services ("third-party tools"), including open-source projects, is through API key authentication through Claude Console or a supported cloud provider.' Anthropic 'may at its discretion allow paid subscribers who have enabled usage credits to use certain third-party tools', prohibits tools that 'misrepresent their identity to Anthropic's servers' or attempts to 'route third-party traffic against subscription limits', and 'reserves the right to draw use of such third-party tools from usage credits rather than subscription limits'; 'Users are responsible for any usage credit charges incurred this way.' - https://support.claude.com/en/articles/13189465-log-in-to-your-claude-account
- (medium, 2026-11-12 (page-stated effective date; verify)) Anthropic Usage Policy, 'Do Not Abuse Our Platform': prohibits 'Resell, proxy, or otherwise provide access to Claude through unauthorized means, including services that route requests through consumer subscriptions or misrepresent the product or client being used', plus bypassing product guardrails/limits and spam or coordinated inauthentic content networks. Note: the page displays 'Effective November 12, 2026', which is after today's date (2026-10-09), so this appears to be a forthcoming version posted in advance; treat the current in-force text as the previous version linked from that page. - https://www.anthropic.com/legal/aup

- (high, 2026-10-09 (fetched)) Official API price table (fetched 2026-10-09), per MTok input/output: Claude Haiku 5.5 $0.10/$0.50 for prompts up to 100k tokens ($0.50/$2.50 above 100k; cache read $0.01); Haiku 4.5 $1/$5; Sonnet 5.5 $2/$10 (cache read $0.10); Sonnet 5 $2/$10 (introductory price made permanent); Sonnet 4.6 $3/$15; Opus 5.5 $4/$20 (cache read $0.20); Opus 5/4.8/4.7/4.6 $5/$25; Fable 5.1 $10/$50. Batch API is 50% off both input and output (Haiku 5.5 $0.05/$0.25; Sonnet 5.5 $1/$5; Opus 5.5 $2/$10). Cache writes cost 1.25x (5-min) or 2x (1-hour) base input; cache reads 0.1x base (0.05x on Opus 5.5/Sonnet 5.5). 'Batch API and prompt caching discounts can be combined.' Models 4.7+ use a tokenizer producing ~30% more tokens for the same text. - https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing

Anthropic says it 'now costs around 75% less to run' than Haiku 4.5 and is 'designed for high-volume, cost-sensitive tasks', citing GDPval-AA 1620 vs 735 and HLE 45.9% vs 10.2% versus Haiku 4.5. All current Claude models list 'multilingual capabilities'. - https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/haiku-5-5/overview

Start-tier batch limits: 1,000 RPM, 200,000 requests in the processing queue. - https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/batch-processing

Up to 4 explicit breakpoints; 5-minute TTL by default, 1-hour at extra cost; lifetime measured from the start of the request that writes/reads the cache. - https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching

- (high, 2026-10-09 (fetched)) Anthropic's cost-optimization guide reports prompt caching cut agent-loop cost 2.7x-5.3x (typical loops read 79-90% of input from cache), batch is the 'second-largest free lever' for unattended work, `medium` effort matched default accuracy at 70-87% of cost on research/knowledge-work benchmarks, and advises pricing 'the hardest 10% of tasks'. Claude Code reports enterprise averages of about $13 per developer per active day for interactive coding, which is far heavier than a scripted content pipeline. - https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/optimizing-for-cost-and-intelligence.md
- (medium, 2026-10-09) Per-video LLM cost estimate (my calculation from the official price table; assumptions: 1,500-word script ≈ 2,700 output tokens on the new tokenizer since 1M tokens ≈ 555k words; a full single-language run of title rewrite + script + ~30-scene storyboard JSON with on-image text + SEO/tags + thumbnail brief ≈ 60k input tokens across calls and ≈ 15k output tokens including adaptive-thinking tokens): Haiku 5.5 ≈ $0.014 standard / $0.007 batch; Haiku 4.5 ≈ $0.14 / $0.07; Sonnet 5.5 ≈ $0.27 / $0.14; Opus 5.5 ≈ $0.54 / $0.27. Localizing into 9 further languages adds roughly +$0.03 (Haiku 5.5), +$0.6 (Sonnet 5.5) or +$1.3 (Opus 5.5) per video set. A Max 5x plan's $100 monthly API credit therefore covers on the order of 350+ Sonnet 5.5 single-language videos or ~7,000 Haiku 5.5 videos per month. - https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing

- (high, 2026-10-09 (fetched)) Ollama runtime facts (official docs): 'By default, Ollama uses a context window size of 4096 tokens' (raise via OLLAMA_CONTEXT_LENGTH or num_ctx, essential for 1,500-word scripts plus research context); OpenAI-compatible endpoint at http://localhost:11434/v1/ supports chat completions, streaming, JSON mode, vision and tools (no tool_choice, no logprobs) and a non-stateful /v1/responses; OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL defaults to 1 and parallel requests multiply context memory; models that fit on one GPU load there, otherwise spill to CPU (`ollama ps` shows CPU/GPU split). Windows install: `irm https://ollama.com/install.ps1 | iex`. - https://docs.ollama.com/faq
- (medium, 2026-10-09 (fetched)) LM Studio (official): Windows requires AVX2; 'At least 16GB of RAM is recommended'; 'at least 4GB of dedicated VRAM is recommended'; local OpenAI-compatible server at http://localhost:1234/v1 with /v1/chat/completions, /v1/responses, /v1/completions, /v1/embeddings, /v1/models; structured output and headless service mode are documented. Neither Ollama nor LM Studio publishes a per-model RAM table today; the practical rule (community, not official) is model file size plus a few GB for KV cache and the OS, so 16 GB machines are limited to roughly 8-9 GB models (Gemma 4 12B, Qwen3.5 9B) and 32 GB machines to roughly 18-20 GB models (Gemma 4 26B MoE/31B, Qwen3.8 27B, gpt-oss 20b). - https://lmstudio.ai/docs/app/system-requirements
- (medium, 2026-10-09 (fetched)) Claude Fable models on your plan: on Max you may spend 'up to 50% of your weekly usage limits on Fable models at no extra cost' (drawn from the same weekly limit, consumed faster); on Pro, Fable models 'aren't included in your plan's usage limits' and run only on pay-as-you-go usage credits. Not relevant to cost-minimising scriptwriting, but it shows per-model-family sub-limits exist. - https://support.claude.com/en/articles/15424964-claude-fable-models-on-your-plan
- (high, 2026-10-09 (fetched)) Agent SDK cost tracking (official): `total_cost_usd` is a client-side estimate from a bundled price table, not billing data; the SDK applies prompt caching automatically; ENABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_1H / promptCacheTtl select the 1-hour TTL; on a subscription you get the 1-hour TTL on your own turns without setting it. Claude Code's `/usage` shows plan usage bars and attribution to skills, subagents and MCP servers for Pro/Max users. - https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/cost-tracking

### Open questions

- Does a Console organization newly linked to a Max plan start in the reduced 'Evaluation tier', and does the $100/$200 monthly credit count as a credit purchase for tier progression? (Rate-limits page says new orgs 'may start in the Evaluation tier'; the credits article is silent.)
- Will Anthropic treat a desktop app that shells out to the user's own non-bare `claude -p` as 'ordinary, individual usage', or as a 'platform running Claude Code' requiring Commercial Terms acceptance and possibly usage-credit billing? The legal page permits end users signing into the unmodified binary even on a hosting platform, but the help center says third-party-tool traffic may be drawn from usage credits 'at its discretion'. Confirm with Anthropic sales if that path is kept.
- When `--bare` becomes the default for `claude -p` (announced 'in a future release'), will subscription OAuth still be usable in -p mode by passing an explicit flag? Today bare mode never reads OAuth or CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN.
- Current per-plan weekly allowances in tokens or hours for Sonnet 5.5 / Haiku 5.5 are not published; the July 2025 figures (Pro 40-80 h Sonnet 4/week, Max 140-480 h) are stale. How many full 10-language video runs fit in a Pro weekly limit via `claude -p` or Desktop MCP is unknown and should be measured with `/usage` attribution.
- The AUP page shows 'Effective November 12, 2026', after today's date; the exact wording currently in force (previous version) should be checked, although the 'route requests through consumer subscriptions' clause is expected to carry over.
- Haiku 5.5 was released 2026-10-07 and has no independent long-form, multilingual writing evaluations yet; its script quality versus Sonnet 5.5 across the top-10 YouTube languages needs an in-house eval before making it the default script writer.
- Monthly API credits 'don't roll over' and API requests stop when the org has no other credits: the app needs a policy for month-end (queue to batch after reset vs prompt the user to buy credits) and a way to read remaining credit, which the Console UI shows but no API in this research exposes.
- Local-model throughput on typical user laptops (CPU-only vs small GPU) for a 2,700-token script is unsourced here; benchmark Gemma 4 26B MoE, Gemma 4 12B and Qwen3.5 9B on representative 16 GB and 32 GB Windows machines, including non-English output quality, before promising an offline mode.
- Minimum initial API credit purchase for users who exceed or lack the monthly credit was not found on the official pages fetched (community lore says $5); confirm in the Console.
- Whether Team plan pooled credits ($20/$100 per seat, $500 cap) would suit a multi-user deployment better than individual Max plans depends on headcount and on whether per-user attribution is needed; not analysed here.
